From the beginning even unto the completion of the Sessional Hymns of the Octoechos, set down at the end of the Triodion at the 1st Psalter reading of the Kathisma, we chant all according to the general Lenten Typikon, then Psalm 50, and, omitting the Litany **"Save, O God, Thy people"**, we immediately chant the Great Canon;[^555] while it is being chanted, the Holy Doors remain open, and candles are lighted on the Holy Table. Before the Holy Doors in Cathedral Churches a carpet is spread, upon which first the Bishop, if there is one, then the other sacred dignitaries two by two according to the order of their dignity make at each Ode one great prostration after the chanting of each Troparion of the Ode of the Canon. In our Seminary before the Holy Doors the seminarians make prostrations, and in the sanctuary before the step of the Holy Table all eight Superiors make prostrations, according to the order of their dignity, one at a single Ode in turn; but at the 9th Ode all eight together, in epitrachelia.
2. Canons two: one the Great Nine-Ode Canon of Saint Andrew, and the other the Three-Ode Canon containing the 4th, 8th, and 9th Odes, which also occupies the first place in these Odes. The Odes of the Nine-Ode Canon do not have an equal number of Troparia, and each of them has at the end two Troparia to Venerable Mary of Egypt, and one to Venerable Andrew of Crete. We chant the Three-Ode Canon for 10 Troparia, with the first Heirmos; for the second Heirmos is not chanted. The refrains to the Troparia of the Great Canon we say: **"Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee"**, and lower: **"Venerable Father Andrew, pray to God for us"**...

...**pray to God for us"**, and: **"Venerable Mother Mary, pray to God for us."** And to the Troparia of the Three-Ode Canon we say: **"Holy Apostles, pray to God for us"**;[^556] for they are of the contents of the Thursday Service. At each Troparion of the Canon of Andrew the Typika appoint three prostrations, but we make only one, yet a great one; and in Cathedral churches the Clergy bow only in turn and alternately throughout the nine Odes of the Canon. The Choirs sing the Canon in the middle of the Church, each at a separate Analogion, making a small bow at the Troparia of the Canon. We take the Katavasia after all Odes, the Heirmos of the last Canon.[^557]
After the 3rd Ode, the Sessional Hymn of the Triodion; after the 6th Ode, the Kontakion and Ikos of the Triodion, with the Beatitudes; after the 9th Ode, the Triadikon Exaposteilarion; all these with the usual Litanies. At the 9th Ode we chant **"More honorable"**, as also the Moscow Typikon and the new Constantinopolitan Typikon have it. After the Canon, **"It is truly right"**.[^558]
3. All the rest, from the Psalms of Praise even unto the end of Matins, are taken according to the general Lenten Typikon; only if we do not join the 1st Hour, then after the 16 concluding bows made at the Prayer of Ephrem...

...we say the Trisagion with **"Our Father"** and 12 times **"Lord, have mercy"**, then the Priest: **"Glory to Thee, Christ God, our hope, glory to Thee"**, and the Dismissal.

#### At the Hours
All according to the usual Lenten Typikon; however, after the Trisagion we say at all Hours only the one Kontakion of the Canon, and the 1st Hour is without a Kathisma.

#### At the Typika and at Vespers
All according to the usual Lenten Typikon.

#### Liturgy
According to the Typikon it ought to be of the Presanctified, however today we do not chant it in the Forty Days except on Wednesday and Friday. In the Primatial Temple on this day there take place only Low Masses [spoken Liturgies].

### Fifth Saturday of the Great Fast: Akathist Saturday[^559]
*Note.*
The Service of this Feast is of the lesser rank; it has only at Matins a Katavasia after each Ode, and the Great Doxology; and it is combined...

...with the usual Service of Lenten Saturday and with the Temple Canon at Matins. The peculiarity of this Feast consists in this: that at its Matins there is chanted the entire Order of the Akathist to the Most Holy Theotokos, the 12 Ikoses and Kontakia being divided into four Staseis [Thirds], of which the first is chanted in place of the Sessional Hymn after the first Psalter reading, the second in place of the Sessional Hymn after the second Psalter reading, the third after the 3rd Ode, and the fourth after the 6th Ode of the Canon, the initial Kontakion being sung at the beginning and at the end of each Stasis, and before its final chanting, the first Ikos is sung again.[^560] This Matins is chanted, according to the Typikon, at the 4th hour of the night, but we chant it at the usual hour.[^561] The Service of the daily Saint according to the Greek Triodion is chanted at Compline.

#### Typikon
##### At Vespers with the Presanctified
1. The usual Kathisma.
2. At **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Господи воззвахъ*), Stichera for 10: Idiomelon of the day twice, and Martyrs' Sticheron one, and of the Feast for 7; **"Glory... Both now..."**, of the Feast.
3. After **"O Gladsome Light"**, the Prokimenon of the Triodion and Reading, and the second Prokimenon of the Triodion and Reading, and the rest of the Presanctified.

##### Little Compline
After **"It is truly right"**, the Kontakion of the Feast alone.

##### Saturday Midnight Office
##### At Matins
1. At **"The Lord is God"**, Troparion of the Feast twice; **"Glory... Both now..."**, again once.
2. After the 1st Psalter reading, Little Litany, and the initial Kontakion **"To thee, the Champion Leader"** (*Взбранной воеводѣ*) is sung by the Choir; and the Priest, vested in all light-colored sacred vestments, and having opened the Holy Doors, censes all according to custom; then having ascended to the Holy Table, he chants the first Stasis [Third] of the Ikoses and Kontakia, and at its completion the same Choir sings again the initial Kontakion **"To thee, the Champion Leader"**. All the Typika command to stand during this singing (for on this account it is called *Ἀκάθιστος*, that is, the Non-sitting [Hymn]); and the Choirs are to sing the Kontakion in the middle of the Church, with sweet singing, slowly.
3. After the 2nd Psalter reading, and after the 3rd and 6th Odes of the Canon, immediately after the Little Litany, the Priest sings also the other three Staseis of Ikoses and Kontakia, and the Choir the Kontakion **"To thee, the Champion Leader"** at the beginning and at the end of each Stasis, the Priest censing at it towards the beginning of each Stasis, all unchangeably as towards the beginning of the 1st Stasis.[^562] —
This Akathist Order according to the Typikon is chanted in the sanctuary; but in the Primatial Church it is chanted before...

---

...altar of the Most Holy Theotokos, which is in its right aisle; and then for the first time the Priest censes the Sanctuary and all the rest according to custom, and having left the Holy Doors open, he exits through them to the altar of the Most Holy Theotokos, and chants the Order of the Akathist there, censing from that altar at the beginning of each third [stasis] the icon of the Most Holy Theotokos, the sacred dignitaries, the Choirs, and the people. After the chanting of the fourth third [stasis] of Ikoses and Kontakia, the Priest returns to the Sanctuary, and having closed the Holy Doors, goes to the sacristy, divests himself of the sacred vestments, and having put on his epitrachelion, completes the rest of Matins.

4. Canons 2 for 12: of the Temple of the Saint with the Heirmos for 6, and of the Feast for 6. Katavasia: **"I shall open my mouth"** (*Ѿве́рзꙋ оу҆ста̀ моѧ̑*), even unto the 6th Ode; from the 6th Ode, however, the Canon of the Temple ceases, and we sing 3 Canons for 14: that is, of the Feast for 6, and both Canons of the Triodion for 8, with the addition of two Troparia, namely one of the Martyrs, with the refrain **"Wondrous is God"** (*Ди́венъ Бо́гъ*), and the other for the Departed, with the refrain **"Their souls"** (*Дꙋ́ши и҆́хъ*), and the Katavasia of the Triodion. The common refrain to the Troparia of the Canon of the Theotokos is: **"Most Holy Theotokos, save us"** (*Прест҃а́ѧ Богоро́дице, спасѝ на́съ*); however, among us they like to chant the Acrostic of the Canon, which is: **"Receptacle of joy, to thee alone does it belong to rejoice"** (*Ра́дости прїя́телище, тебѣ̀ подоба́етъ ра́доватисѧ є҆ди́нѣй*; Greek: *Χαρᾶς δοχεῖον, σοὶ πρέπει χαίρειν μόνῃ*). And we sing both the Troparia and this refrain according to the model set forth in the new *Hlasopisnets* for Akathist Saturday.

After the 3rd Ode, upon the final completion of the chanting of the Kontakion **"To thee, the Champion Leader"** (*Взбра́нной*), we say the Sessional Hymn of the Feast; after the 6th [Ode], immediately after the Little Ektenia, the final third [stasis] of the Ikoses and Kontakia is sung, preceded and followed by the Kontakion **"To thee, the Champion Leader"** (*Взбра́нной*); however, its final Kontakion, **"O all-hymned Mother"** (*Ѡ всепѣ́тая Ма́ти*), is sung thrice, and after this the 1st Ikos,

and thus **"To thee, the Champion Leader"** (*Взбра́нной*). At the 9th Ode we sing **"More honorable"** (*Честнѣ́йшꙋю*), and after the 9th [Ode] the Exaposteilarion of the Feast.
5. At the Praises, 4 Stichera, all of the Feast; and **"Glory... Both now..."**, of the Feast.
6. After the Great Doxology, the Troparion of the Feast.

#### At the Hours and at the Liturgy
All of the Feast alone; only the Epistle and Gospel first of the day, then of the Feast.

### Fifth Sunday of the Great Fast
All according to the Typikon of the 4th Sunday, here on p. 405.

### Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, and Friday of the Sixth Week of Fast
All according to the general fasting Typikon of the preceding weeks.

## Flowery Triodion
### Disposition of the Synod of Lviv to Pastors of Souls Concerning Church Divine Services from Lazarus Saturday until Bright Tuesday Inclusive
1. On the Saturday before Palm Sunday, called Lazarus [Saturday], they are to celebrate the Liturgy in church.

2. On Palm Sunday, they are to perform all things according to custom, according to the Typikon of a Master Feast of the First Rank, with the inclusion of the blessing of branches at Matins.
3. On the three following days of Holy Week, they are to sing the Liturgy of the Presanctified.
4. On Great Thursday of Holy Week, they are to sing the Liturgy together with Vespers.
5. On the same day after sunset, they are to celebrate the Order of the Passions, with the 12 Gospels.
6. On Great Friday, which is regarded as a distinguished Feast, they are to perform in church in the morning the Hours called Royal, and about midday Vespers with the procession of the Plashchanytsia,[^563] which, when brought out, is placed before the Iconostasis in the so-called Tomb, and is to remain there for continuous veneration by the people until the Matins of the Resurrection.
7. It would be most desirable that the Pastors on that same day, after the laying down of the Plashchanytsia, at some opportune hour should preach to the people a homily concerning the passions and death of Christ.
8. It is likewise enjoined upon the same Pastors of souls to celebrate Burial Matins in the church on that Friday after sunset, on account of the special character of the divine service of this day, and the solemn rites performed at its end.
9. On Great Saturday itself, they are to sing the Liturgy of Basil together with Vespers.
10. On Resurrection Sunday [Easter Sunday], they are to celebrate Matins very early, with all the rites accompanying it, pre-

-ceding it, and this according to the Typikon approved by this Synod. In Cathedral Churches, and likewise in other non-cathedral ones, this Matins is usually anticipated, so as to be performed on Saturday evening; the Synod judges that this custom may be retained.
11. On the first three days of Bright Week, they are daily to sing Matins, the Liturgy, and Vespers in church.
12. The same Synod forbids all Priests read Liturgies throughout all of Holy Week, and on its first three days appoints only the Liturgy of the Presanctified, while on Great Thursday and Saturday only one and that a sung [Liturgy], of Basil the Great with Vespers. (*Title IV, Chap. I, 3, 5*). On Great Friday, except for the Annunciation, one must not celebrate the Liturgy under any circumstances.

*Note.*
From this Saturday even unto Thomas Sunday inclusive, only the Triodion is sung; the Menaion [is used] only if the Annunciation occurs, and from Bright Monday even unto Thomas Sunday inclusive if a Saint with Vigil or Polyeleos occurs; while for Saints without Polyeleos of these 16 days, their Canons have been prescribed to be sung in advance at Complines, beginning from Monday of the 2nd Week of the Great Fast, as was indicated here on p. 403.

### Sixth Saturday of the Great Fast: Lazarus [Saturday]
*Note.*
The Service of this Feast is of the lesser rank, and only at Matins from the Canon downwards is it of the greater rank; yet even before the Canon it has certain elements of the Resurrection Service; namely, after the 2nd Kathisma, that is,

after the Evlogitaria (*Neporóchni*), it has **"The angelic assembly"** (*А҆́нгельскїй собо́ръ*), and after the 2nd Sessional Hymn **"Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ"** (*Воскресе́нїе Хрїсто́во ви́дѣвше*), and before the Exaposteilarion: **"Holy is the Lord our God"** (*Свѧ́тъ Госпо́дь Бо́гъ на́шъ*) thrice.

#### Typikon
##### On Friday Evening
###### At Vespers with the Presanctified
1. Kathisma in course.
2. At **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Господи воззвахъ*), Stichera for 10: Idiomelon of the day twice, and Martyr-sticheron 1, then of the Feast 7; **"Glory... Both now..."**, of the Feast.
3. After **"O Gladsome Light"** (*Свѣ́те ти́хїй*), the Prokeimenon of the Triodion, and one Reading of the Triodion, then the 2nd Prokeimenon, and the 2nd Reading of the Triodion, and the rest of the Presanctified.

##### At Little Compline
We sing the Canon of Lazarus in the Triodion, and after the Canon, instead of **"It is truly right"** (*Досто́йно*), the Heirmos of the 9th Ode of the Canon. After the Trisagion, the Kontakion of the Feast alone.[^564]

##### At Midnight Office
After the 1st Trisagion, instead of the Troparia **"Uncreated nature"** (*Несозда́нное естество̀*), we say the Troparion of Lazarus; after the 2nd Trisagion, instead of the Troparia **"Remember, O Lord"** (*Помянѝ, Го́споди*), the Kontakion of Lazarus; but the prayer **"Remember, O Lord"** (*Помянѝ, Го́споди*) we do not say.

#### At Matins
1. At **"The Lord is God"** (*Бо́гъ Госпо́дь*), the Troparion of the Feast twice; **"Glory... Both now..."**, the same.
2. After the 1st and after the 2nd Psalter Stichologia, the Sessional Hymn of the Feast; however, after the 2nd Stichologia, before the Sessional Hymn, we sing **"The angelic assembly"** (*А҆́нгельскїй собо́ръ*), without change, just as on Sunday; and after the Sessional Hymn, **"Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ"** (*Воскресе́нїе Хрїсто́во ви́дѣвше*), and Psalm 50, and immediately the Canon.
3. Canons of the Feast, 2: the 1st with the Heirmos for 8, and the 2nd for 6; from the 6th Ode, however, the Four-Ode Canon [Tetraodion], the Heirmos twice, and the Troparia for 6, then the other Canon for 6. Katavasia after each Ode: the Heirmos of the 2nd Canon. As the refrain to the Troparia we say: **"Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee"** (*Сла́ва Тебѣ̀, Бо́же на́шъ, сла́ва Тебѣ̀*).[^565] After the 3rd Ode, the Sessional Hymn, and after the 6th, the Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast; at the 9th we do not sing **"More honorable"** (*Честнѣ́йшꙋю*); after the 9th we do not say **"It is truly right"** (*Досто́йно*) even unto Thomas Sunday; after the 9th, the Exaposteilarion of the Feast, yet before the Exaposteilarion we sing **"Holy is the Lord our God"** (*Свѧ́тъ Госпо́дь Бо́гъ на́шъ*) thrice.
4. At the Praises, 8 Stichera, all of the Feast, with the two Resurrectional Refrains, as on all Sundays; **"Glory..."**, of the Feast; **"Both now..."**: **"Most blessed art thou"** (*Преблагослове́нна е҆сѝ*).
5. After the Great Doxology, the Troparion of the Feast alone.
6. The proper Dismissal.

#### At the Hours and at the Liturgy
All of the Feast alone; and at the Liturgy, instead of the Trisagion, we sing **"All who have been baptized"** (*Є҆ли́цы во Хрїста̀ крести́стеся*).

### Palm Sunday[^566]
All according to the general Typikon of a Master Feast with Vigil, without Afterfeast. It has all its proper texts, and at the Liturgy Antiphons and the Introit (*Vhódnoje*). However, before the Troparion of Palm Sunday, we sing yesterday's Troparion of Lazarus; only after the Great Doxology do we sing the Troparion of Lazarus alone.

#### At Great Vespers
At **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Господи воззвахъ*), Stichera for 10; and at the Blessing of Loaves, the Troparion of Lazarus twice, and of Palm Sunday once.

#### At Great Matins
1. At **"The Lord is God"** (*Бо́гъ Госпо́дь*), the Troparion of Lazarus twice; **"Glory... Both now..."**, of Palm Sunday.
2. The blessing and distribution of branches according to the Typikon takes place immediately after Psalm 50, at the kissing of the Gospel; according to our custom, the blessing is after Psalm 50, but the distribution during the anointing with oil (*myrovánnya*), that is, during the Canon. It takes place in this manner:
When the Priest has read the Gospel, he places it upon the Analogion near the Tetrapod, between two burning candlesticks,

the branches being placed on the Tetrapod, and the censer and aspersorium being prepared, the Priest approaches the Tetrapod, and while the Choirs read Psalm 50, having placed incense in and blessed the censer, he censes the branches from the four sides of the Tetrapod; and at the conclusion of Psalm 50, after the Deacon has exclaimed (or if there is none, the Priest himself): **"Let us pray to the Lord"** (*Го́споду помоли́мся*), he reads aloud (from the Sluzhebnik or from the Triodion)[^567] the first prayer, **"O Lord our God"** (*Го́споди Бо́же на́шъ*); and the Choir having responded: "Amen," the Priest says: **"Peace be to all"** (*Ми́ръ всѣ̑мъ*); the Deacon: **"Bow your heads unto the Lord"** (*Главы̑ ва́шѧ Го́сподеви приклони́те*); and after **"To Thee, O Lord"** (*Тебѣ̀, Го́споди*), the Priest [reads] the second prayer, **"O Lord God Almighty... and unto the ages of ages"** (*Го́споди Бо́же Вседержи́телю... и҆ во вѣ́ки вѣкѡ́въ*).[^568] And the Choir responding: "Amen," he sprinkles the branches with holy water, saying: **"These branches are blessed and sanctified..."** (*Благословля́ются и҆ ѡ҆свяща́ются вѣ̑тви сїѧ̑...*); and after the chanting of the Ektenia **"Save, O God, Thy people"** (*Спасѝ, Бо́же, лю́ди Твоя̑*), with his right hand he anoints the people with oil, while with his left hand he distributes a branch to each, which are orderly handed to the Priest by the Deacon, or if there is none, by one of the church servers.[^569]
After the 9th Ode: "We do not say the Exaposteilarion, but immediately **'Holy is the Lord our God'** (*Свѧ́тъ Госпо́дь Бо́гъ на́шъ*) thrice in Tone 4."

#### At the Liturgy
After the Introit: Troparion of Lazarus; **"Glory..."**, of Palm Sunday; **"Both now..."**, Kontakion of Palm Sunday; and we sing the Trisagion.

### Fast for the Entire Holy Week
On Monday, Wednesday, and Friday the Synod of Lviv prescribes xerophagy (*sukhojastije*); on Tuesday, Thursday, and Saturday it permits cooked food (*navil*). (*Title XI, Part 4*).

### Great Monday, Great Tuesday, and Great Wednesday[^570]
*Note.*
1. The sacred vestments for these days and for the entire Holy Week must be violet, beginning from Palm Sunday evening.
2. All these three days have one and the same form of divine service, which is penitential/fasting, as in the Forty Days, with only certain exceptions, as follows:

#### At Vespers
##### a) On Palm Sunday Evening
1. At **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Господи воззвахъ*), Stichera of the Triodion for 6; **"Glory... Both now..."**, of the Triodion.
2. Entrance; Prokeimenon of the day: **"Behold now, bless the Lord"** (*Сѐ ны́нѣ благослови́те Го́спода*), and immediately **"Vouchsafe, O Lord"** (*Сподо́би, Го́споди*).

3. Aposticha of the Triodion, with proper refrains.
4. After **"Now You dismiss"** (*Ны́нѣ ѿпꙋща́еши*) with the Trisagion, the Troparion **"Rejoice, O Virgin Theotokos"** (*Богоро́дице Дѣ́во*), and all the rest with prostrations, as on the past Sundays of the Holy Forty Days.
5. The proper Dismissal, likewise also on Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, and Thursday until the end of the Hours: **"May He Who comes to His voluntary Passion..."** (*Прише́дый на стра́сть во́льнꙋю...*).[^571]

##### b) On Monday, Tuesday, and Wednesday Evening
###### At Vespers with the Presanctified
1. At **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Господи воззвахъ*), 10 Stichera, all of the Triodion; **"Glory... Both now..."**, of the Triodion.
2. Entrance with the Gospel, and after **"O Gladsome Light"** (*Свѣ́те ти́хїй*), two Prokeimena of the Triodion, each with its Reading.
3. After **"Let my prayer arise"** (*Да и҆спра́витсѧ*), the Priest: **"And that we may be accounted worthy..."** (*И҆ ѡ҆ є҆́же сподо́битися на́мъ*), etc., and he reads the Gospel from Matthew, peric. 98, and the rest of the Presanctified, with the Dismissal: **"May He Who comes to His voluntary Passion..."** (*Прише́дый на стра́сть во́льнꙋю*).

*Note.* If there is no Presanctified, at **"Lord, I have cried"** we sing the Stichera for 6, transferring the three last Stichera to the Aposticha with the usual refrains, singing the first twice. — We make the Entrance with the Gospel, and after the 2nd Reading of the Triodion we read the Gospel as above, and all the rest with prostrations according to fasting custom.

#### At Compline
On Sunday evening we sing Little Compline, according to the Typikon of the other Sundays of the Great Forty Days, and on it the Three-Ode Canon [Triodion] of the Triodion, and after the Trisagion the Kontakion of the Triodion alone.
On Monday and Tuesday evening we sing Great Compline with prostrations according to the fasting Typikon, and on it likewise the Three-Ode Canon of the Triodion, and after the Trisagion the Kontakion of the Triodion, then **"The Lord of hosts be with us"** (*Го́споди си́лъ съ на́ми бꙋ́ди*).[^572]
On Wednesday evening we sing Little Compline,[^573] and on it likewise the Three-Ode Canon of the Triodion, and after the Trisagion the Kontakion of the Triodion alone.

#### At Midnight Office
Nothing special.

#### At Matins
1. At the Alleluia, instead of the Trinity Troparia, we sing the Troparion **"Behold, the Bridegroom comes"** (*Се́ Жени́хъ грѧде́тъ*) twice; **"Glory... Both now..."**, the same.

2. Three Kathismas in course with their Sessional Hymns, then **"And that we may be accounted worthy..."** (*И҆ ѡ҆ є҆́же сподо́битися на́мъ*), and the Matins Gospel[^574] following the title of the Gospel, and Psalm 50, and **"Save, O God, Thy people"** (*Спасѝ, Бо́же, лю́ди Твоя̑*).[^575]
3. Only one Canon: a Three-Ode Canon on Monday and Wednesday, or a Two-Ode Canon on Tuesday; Troparia for 12, Heirmoi twice. We do not say the Biblical Odes, but the refrain to the Troparia of the Canon: **"Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee"** (*Сла́ва Тебѣ̀, Бо́же на́шъ, сла́ва Тебѣ̀*);[^576] then the Katavasia: the Heirmos of the Canon once, both Choirs together. Before the Kontakion and Ikos the Priest says the Little Ektenia; at the 9th Ode we do not sing **"More honorable"** (*Честнѣ́йшꙋю*), nor **"It is truly right"** (*Досто́йно*) after the Canon, but after the Katavasia immediately the Little Ektenia, and the Exaposteilarion **"Thy bridal chamber"** (*Черто́гъ тво́й*) twice, then **"Glory... Both now..."**, the same a third time.
4. At the Praises, 4 Stichera of the Triodion; **"Glory... Both now..."**, of the Triodion; and the Little Doxology, and after the Ektenia **"Let us complete"** (*И҆спо́лнимъ*), the Aposticha of the Triodion, and all the rest with prostrations, as on the fasting days of the Holy Forty Days.

#### At the Hours
All according to the general fasting Typikon of the Great Forty Days; however:
1. At the 1st and 9th Hour there is no Kathisma, but only at the 3rd and 6th.
2. Directly after the Theotokion **"Theotokos, thou art the true vine"** (*Богоро́дице, ты̀ е҆сѝ лоза̀*) at the 3rd Hour, and after the corresponding Theotokia at the other

two Hours, the Typika appoint the Reading of the Four Gospels (*Tetraevangélion*), which among us today is read only in Cathedral or Collegiate Churches;[^577] at the 6th Hour, however, we read the Gospel not immediately after the Theotokion, but after the conclusion of the Troparion of the Prophecy and the Prokeimena of the Paremia. The Reading of the Four Gospels embraces all Evangelists, that is, Matthew, Mark, and Luke whole, but John only up to the Passions of Christ, that is, up to: **"Now is the Son of man glorified"** (*Ны́нѣ просла́висѧ*), in Pericope 46, Chapter 13:32 [13:31]. All the Evangelists taken together are divided into 9 Readings, according to the number of the three Hours (3rd, 6th, 9th) and three days (Monday, Tuesday, and Wednesday). Matthew is divided into 2 Readings, and Mark into 2, and John into 2, but Luke into 3. The 1st Reading embraces the first half of Matthew, the 2nd the second half of Matthew, the 3rd the first half of Mark, the 4th the second half of Mark, the 5th the first part of Luke, the 6th the second part of Luke, the 7th the third part of Luke; the 8th the first part of John, the 9th the second part of John. And the Typikon indeed does not determine where the first half of the three Evangelists and the first and second parts of Luke conclude; but since Matthew has 28 chapters, his half concludes with Chapter 14; Mark has 16 chapters, so his half concludes with Chapter 8; Luke has 24 chapters, so his first part concludes with Chapter 8, and the second with Chapter 16; John up to **"Now is the Son of man glorified"** has 12 chapters and part of Chapter 13, so his half concludes with Chapter 6 and part of 7.
The Priest, at the beginning of each Reading, vested in a phelonion of fasting colors, and having opened the Holy Doors, censes all things according to custom, then having gone out through the Holy Doors to the Analogion placed before the Holy Doors facing

the people, having upon it two burning candlesticks, reads from there the Gospel, beginning from **"And that we may be accounted worthy..."** (*И҆ ѡ҆ є҆́же сподо́битися на́мъ*). Upon completing each Reading, having kissed the beginning of the reading and left the Gospel on the Analogion until the completion of the 3rd Reading, he returns to the Sanctuary, and having closed the Holy Doors, divests himself of the phelonion.[^578]
3. After the Trisagion, the Kontakion of the Triodion.

#### At the Typika (Obidnytsia)
1. The Kontakion of the Triodion alone.
2. In the Slavic Typika and in the Greek Typikon of Sabbas, on Great Wednesday, instead of the Dismissal, the prayer **"O Master, Great in Mercy"** (*Влады́ко многоми́лостиве*), which is at Vespers with Vigil, is appointed; in the Slavic ones, moreover, also the Mutual Forgiveness for sins committed during the Forty Days, and commemorations. However, these are superfluous additions of the Graeco-Slavs, unknown to the Greeks; scarcely may the prayer **"O Master, Great in Mercy"** be retained, and the Mutual Forgiveness in monasteries; the commemorations here are superfluous, for they will be taken later at the conclusion of Compline and Midnight Office, having no connection with the forgiveness of the sins of the Forty Days.

#### On Wednesday at Vespers with the Presanctified, at Compline, and at Midnight Office
All according to the Typikon of Monday and Tuesday, here on p. 573 [428]; only on Wednesday after the dismissal of the Liturgy of the Presanctified

Presanctified, "the prostrations made in church cease, but in cells they are performed even until the bell for Matins of Christ's Bright Resurrection."[^579] We, however, even until the Resurrection of Christ make 3 great prostrations in church at the end of the divine service, singing the stichon **"Thou Who hast suffered..."** (*Претерпѣ́вый за на́съ*), and at the veneration of the Plashchanytsia, singing **"The noble Joseph"** (*Благообра́зный І҆ѡ́сифъ*).

### Great Thursday[^580]
#### At Matins
1. **"Blessed is our God..."** (*Благослове́нъ Бо́гъ на́шъ*). **"Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee"** (*Сла́ва Тебѣ̀, Бо́же на́шъ, сла́ва Тебѣ̀*). **"Heavenly King"** (*Царю̀ небе́сный*) and the rest as usual, with the Two Psalms and the Six Psalms, even unto the end of the Ektenia **"In peace let us pray to the Lord"** (*Ми́ромъ Го́споду помоли́мся*).
2. Instead of **"The Lord is God"** (*Бо́гъ Госпо́дь*), we sing Alleluia in Tone 8, that is, in the Tone of the following Troparion; but since this Troparion has a special melody in the Heirmologion, for this reason we sing Alleluia also according to the model of this melody.
3. Troparion: **"When the glorious disciples"** (*Є҆гда̀ сла́внїи оу҆чени́цы*) twice; **"Glory... Both now..."**, the same again.
4. There are no Kathismas and Sessional Hymns, but immediately the Gospel from Luke, peric. 108, and the 1st verse of the beginning of 109, that is, verse 39 of Chapter 22;[^581] then Psalm 50, and omitting **"Save, O God, Thy people"** (*Спасѝ, Бо́же, лю́ди Твоя̑*), we immediately sing the Canon.

5. Only one Canon: Heirmoi twice, Troparia for 6,[^582] with the refrain: **"Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee"** (*Сла́ва Тебѣ̀, Бо́же на́шъ, сла́ва Тебѣ̀*). Katavasia after each Ode: the Heirmos of the same Canon once, both Choirs together. After the 3rd Ode we say the Sessional Hymn; after the 6th, the Kontakion and Ikos; at the 9th we do not sing **"More honorable"** (*Честнѣ́йшꙋю*),[^583] nor **"It is truly right"** (*Досто́йно*) after the 9th, but instead of it the Heirmos of the Canon;[^584] and immediately the Exaposteilarion twice; **"Glory... Both now..."**, the same.
6. At the Praises, 4 Stichera; and after the Little Doxology, the Ektenia **"Let us complete"** (*И҆спо́лнимъ*), and the Aposticha with proper refrains.
7. **"It is good to give praise"** (*Бла́го е҆́сть*) once, Trisagion with the **"Our Father"**, and the Troparion **"When the glorious disciples"** (*Є҆гда̀ сла́внїи оу҆чени́цы*), without a Theotokion.
8. Priest: **"Wisdom!"** (*Премꙋ́дрость!*) Choir: **"Bless!"** (*Благословѝ!*) Priest: **"Blessed is He Who is..."** (*Сы́й благослове́нъ... и҆ во вѣ́ки вѣкѡ́въ*). Choir: "Amen," and the 1st Hour.

#### At the Hours
1. At all [Hours], the Troparion and Kontakion of Great Thursday; however, at the 1st Hour, after the Theotokion **"What shall we call thee..."** (*Что́ тѧ нарече́мъ*), we say the Troparion of the Prophecy, and the Prokeimenon, and the Paremia, and another Prokeimenon.
2. Dismissal: **"May He Who comes to His voluntary Passion..."** (*Прише́дый на стра́сть во́льнꙋю...*)[^585] we say at the 1st Hour; at the others, since they are sung joined with the Typika (Obidnytsia), we say this Dismissal at the end of the Typika; and we say all these things in our cells.

#### At the Typika (Obidnytsia)
1. It begins from the Beatitudes without Stichera. The Creed may be omitted, since it will be at the Liturgy.
2. Instead of the usual Kontakia, we say the Kontakion of Great Thursday.
3. After the Kontakion: **"Lord, have mercy"** 40 times, **"All-Holy Trinity..."** (*Всесвятáя Тро́йце*),[^586] and the Dismissal.

#### Great Thursday Evening
##### At Vespers with the Liturgy of Basil[^587]
*Note.*
"The Priest ought on Great Thursday at the Liturgy to consecrate two Lambs, and to consume one in Communion, but to place the other in the Pyx (Ciborium), and to keep it in the Tabernacle in the usual place even unto today (unto Friday)." Thus our Sluzhebnik has it. Concerning the Lamb itself that is to be kept in the Tabernacle until the day of exposition, the Synod of Lviv commands: "that it, before consecration, be fitted to its vessel, the so-called Melchizedek, properly placed therein, and carefully wiped, so that particles might not afterwards fall from it; and that having been thus enclosed in the Melchizedek, it be consecrated at the Liturgy on one of the preceding days, and thus consecrated be kept in the Pyx even unto the exposition itself."[^588] When the Priest prepares at the Proskomide the Lamb that is to be used for the exposition, he is not to say over it the words: **"In remembrance of our Lord..."**, etc., nor perform upon it the actions that are usually performed upon

the Lamb that is to be consumed at that same Liturgy, for it is deemed to be only for the Communicants; it suffices to trim it for its easier fracturing and consumption on another day.

###### Typikon
1. From the beginning even unto **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Господи воззвахъ*), all according to the Typikon of the Paramony of the Nativity of Christ, here on p. 189.
2. At **"Lord, I have cried"**, Stichera for 10.
3. Entrance with the Gospel, and 3 Readings of the Triodion, the first two with their Prokeimena, but the third without a Prokeimenon. The Prokeimenon of the day from the Horologion is omitted.
4. Immediately after the 3rd Reading, the Little Ektenia with the Exclamation of the Trisagion, and the Trisagion, and the Prokeimenon of the Triodion, and the Epistle and Gospel, and according to the order the Liturgy of Basil the Great.
5. Instead of the Cherubic Hymn, likewise also after the transfer of the Holy Gifts, and instead of the Kinonikon (Communion verse), and instead of **"Let our mouths be filled"** (*Да и҆спо́лнятся*), we sing the Troparion **"Of Thy Mystical Supper"** (*Ве́чери Твоеѧ̀ та́йныѧ*). Thus the general editions; but our manuscript Typikon, our Sluzhebnyks, and the Moscow Typikon, instead of **"All of creation rejoices in thee"** (*Ѡ҆ тебѣ̀ ра́дуется*), have the Heirmos of the 9th Ode of the Canon (**"Come, O believers, let us enjoy the Master's hospitality"** / *Стра́нствїя Влады́чня*); while the Benedictine Euchologion takes **"Of Thy Mystical Supper"** only instead of **"All of creation rejoices in thee"** (in the order of the Liturgy of Basil).

##### At Little Compline
The Three-Ode Canon of the Triodion; and after the Trisagion, the Kontakion of Great Friday alone.

##### At the Daily Midnight Office
All according to custom. — In cells.

### Great Friday
#### At Matins
### The Order of the Holy and Saving Passions of Our Lord Jesus Christ
*Note.*
1. We sing this Matins on Friday after sunset; according to the Typikon, in the 2nd hour of the night.
2. An Analogion is set before the Royal Doors with two candlesticks facing the people, having the Gospel upon it.

#### General Overview
From the usual beginning even unto the conclusion of the chanting of the Troparion that is after the Alleluia, and from the Canon even unto the end of Matins, all things are done according to the Typikon of Matins of Great Thursday, here on p. 423 [433]; but between the Troparion and the Canon there are five Sessional Hymns, each of which has before it three Antiphons of Passion Troparia, without Kathismas and without Little Ektenias; then the Beatitudes, and the Prokeimenon, and Psalm 50. Besides this, we read the 12 Passion Gospels: 1st after the Troparion; the 5 following, after each of the 5 Sessional Hymns; the 7th [6th in the original text] after the Beatitudes; the 8th after Psalm 50; the 9th after the Exaposteilarion; the 10th after the Praises; the 11th after "Let us complete"; the 12th after the Aposticha. Upon the completion of the reading of each Gospel, among us they toll all the church bells, and after the 12th Gospel they cease tolling even unto the Resurrection; and instead of bells they use wooden clappers (*byla*); however, it is better already at the Passion Matins not to toll bells, but to strike the wooden boards or *Klepalo*, for Passion Matins is a time of mourning,

with which the joyous sound of bells does not harmonize. Likewise in Rome at Passion Matins they already use clappers (*byla*).

#### Detailed Typikon
1. From the usual beginning even unto the end of the Ektenia **"In peace let us pray to the Lord"** (*Ми́ромъ Го́споду помоли́мся*), all as at Matins on all days.[^589]
2. Instead of **"The Lord is God"** (*Бо́гъ Госпо́дь*), we sing Alleluia in the Tone of the following Troparion.
3. Troparion: **"When the glorious disciples"** (*Є҆гда̀ сла́внїи оу҆чени́цы*) twice; **"Glory... Both now..."**, the same again.
4. While the Troparion is being sung for the third time, the Priest, having vested in a violet phelonion, goes before the steps of the Altar, and having made a metania to the Holy Mysteries and opened the Holy Doors, says the prayer of incense, and having blessed the censer with **"Blessed is our God..."** (*Благослове́нъ Бо́гъ на́шъ*), censes round about the Holy Table; then, standing before the steps of the Holy Table and having bowed deeply, he goes out through the Holy Doors before the Iconostasis and censes the patronal icons, the Choirs, and the people; and having returned through the Holy Doors to the Sanctuary and handed over the censer, and having again bowed deeply, he chants the Little Ektenia without the Exclamation; then he goes out through the Holy Doors to the Analogion, and proclaims: **"And that we may be accounted worthy..."** (*И҆ ѡ҆ є҆́же сподо́битися на́мъ*), and **"Peace be to all"** (*Ми́ръ всѣ̑мъ*), and **"Wisdom! Stand upright!"** (*Премꙋ́дрость, про́сти!*), etc.; and the Choir, instead of **"Glory to Thee, O Lord"** (*Сла́ва Тебѣ̀, Го́споди*), responds: **"Glory to Thy Passion, O Lord"** (*Сла́ва страсте́мъ Твои̑мъ, Го́споди*).[^590] Priest: **"Let us attend!"** (*Во́ньмемъ*), and reads the 1st Gospel; and at the conclusion of the Reading, the Choir, instead of **"Glory to Thee, O Lord"** (*Сла́ва Тебѣ̀, Го́споди*), sings: **"Glory to Thy longsuffering, O Lord"** (*Сла́ва долготерпѣ́нїю Твоему̀, Го́споди*).[^591] The Priest, having kissed the beginning of the Reading, returns into the Sanctuary, and having closed the Holy Doors and bowed down toward the Holy Mysteries, departs to the sacristy, and having divested himself of the phelonion, goes to his place.

---

All these things take place also at the Readings of the other Gospels, except for the Little Ektenia and the censing, which according to the Typikon of our Triodia and the Typikon of the University of Lviv takes place only at the 1st Gospel, and at the 5 Sessional Hymns, and this only the censing of the Holy Table. For they say: "We do not sit at such Sessional Hymns, because the Priest censes the holy Altar, but standing we chant them."[^592] For the rest, we deem

that one ought to cense not only at the Sessional Hymns, but also at all the other Gospels, for the censing is not for the sake of the Sessional Hymns, but for the sake of the Gospels, yet only around the Holy Table, as our Triodia have it, which mention censing only of the Holy Table; but it suffices to cense everything only twice, that is, at the 1st Gospel, and in the Canon at the Heirmos of the 4th Ode.
According to the Slavonic Typika, the 1st Gospel, and according to others also the 12th, is to be read by the Superior or Abbot. In the Cathedral Church the Hierarch reads them; while the other [Gospels are read by] the other sacred dignitaries according to the order of their dignity.[^593]
(The Deacons, if there be any, having vested from the beginning of Matins in violet dalmatics with the orarion, remain in the Sanctuary at their places until the Troparion **"When the glorious disciples"** (*Є҆гда̀ сла́внїи оу҆чени́цы*) begins to be sung for the third time; for then, having come with the Priest who is about to sing the Gospel, vested in a violet phelonion, before the steps of the Altar, and having bowed together with him toward the Holy Mysteries, they open the Holy Doors, cense all things according to custom, as at the beginning of the Liturgy, and return before the steps of the Altar; then, having handed over the censers and both bowed equally toward the Holy Mysteries, they go out each through his own door before the Royal Doors, and having bowed again toward the Holy Mysteries, they stand there until the second Deacon, after the chanting of the Troparion, recites the Little Ektenia; for then, having bowed toward the Holy Mysteries and changed their sides, they advance to the Analogion, so that, when the Priest has come to the Analogion, the first Deacon is at his right hand, and the second at his left. When the Priest has bowed toward the Holy Mysteries and come through the Holy Doors to the Analogion, the first Deacon proclaims: **"And that we may be accounted worthy..."** (*И҆ ѡ҆ є҆́же сподо́битися на́мъ*); Priest: **"Peace be to all"** (*Ми́ръ всѣ̑мъ*); second Deacon: **"Wisdom! Stand upright! Let us hear..."** (*Премꙋ́дрость, про́сти, оу҆слы́шимъ*); Priest: **"The Reading from N."** (*Ѿ и҆́мрекъ*); first Deacon: **"Let us attend!"** (*Во́ньмемъ*); Priest reads the Reading. When the Reading is finished, the Priest kisses the beginning of the Reading first himself, then the Deacon on the right, and after him also the one on the left, and all three return into the Sanctuary, the Priest through the middle doors, and the Deacons through the side doors; and standing together with the Priest before the

steps of the Altar, they close the Holy Doors, and having all bowed equally toward the Holy Mysteries, they depart to their places. At the other Gospels, since the Little Ektenia is not said, the Deacons, having come before the Holy Doors, bow, and having changed their sides, go to the Analogion; and the Priest having taken his stand in their midst, they proclaim: **"And that we may be accounted worthy..."** (*И҆ ѡ҆ є҆́же сподо́битися*), and the rest, as above.
If it happens that a Hierarch or other sacred dignitaries are present, the Deacons ought to cense them before the Choirs, and neither the Priest nor the Deacons are to kiss the Gospel before the Hierarch and sacred dignitaries; and for this reason both Deacons carry the open Gospel together to them for kissing, then to the Priest standing in the Holy Doors; and when they have kissed it, they also kiss the same, and place it upon the Analogion; and having bowed deeply toward the Holy Mysteries, they return, each through his own door, into the Sanctuary.
5. After the chanting of the 1st Gospel there follow five Sessional Hymns, each of which has before it three Antiphons of Passion Troparia, and after itself one Passion Gospel. The number of Troparia is not equal in all the Antiphons, and according to the Typikon each is to be repeated,[^594] except for the Theotokion, with which each Antiphon is concluded, with the refrain **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*).[^595]
6. The Beatitudes, with the Prokeimenon and the 6th Gospel at the end. The number of Stichera at the Beatitudes shall be 7, with **"Glory... Both now..."**, 8.[^596]
7. Psalm 50 and the 8th Gospel.
8. The Three-Ode Canon; Heirmoi twice, Troparia for 12, Katavasia after each Ode its Heirmos, both Choirs together. — After the 5th Ode, the Little Ektenia,

and the Kontakion and Ikos; at the 9th Ode we do not sing **"More honorable"** (*Честнѣ́йшꙋю*), but the Heirmos of the 9th Ode;[^597] after the 9th [Ode] we do not sing **"It is truly right"** (*Досто́йно є҆́сть*), but the Katavasia;[^598] then the Little Ektenia, and the Exaposteilarion thrice with the refrain **"Glory... Both now..."**, according to custom; then the 9th Gospel.
9. At the Praises, Stichera for 4; **"Glory"**, one Sticheron; **"Both now"**, another; then the 10th Gospel.
10. **"To Thee glory is due"** (*Тебѣ̀ сла́ва подоба́етъ*),[^599] **"Glory to Thee Who hast shown us the light"** (*Сла́ва Тебѣ̀ показа́вшему на́мъ свѣ́тъ*), and the Small Doxology.
11. The Ektenia **"Let us complete"** (*И҆спо́лнимъ*), and after the Exclamation **"For a God of mercy..."** (*Я҆́ко Бо́гъ ми́лости*)[^600] immediately the 11th Gospel.
12. The Aposticha; then the 12th Gospel.
13. **"It is good to give praise"** (*Бла́го є҆́сть*), Trisagion, **"Our Father"** (*Ѡ҆́тче на́шъ*), Troparion of Friday: **"Thou hast redeemed us"** (*И҆скꙋпи́лъ ны̀ є҆сѝ*).
14. The Ektenia **"Have mercy on us, O God"** (*Поми́луй на́съ, Бо́же*), and the Dismissal proper to Great Friday, the middle one, that is: Priest: **"Wisdom!"** (*Премꙋ́дрость*); Choir: **"More honorable"** (*Честнѣ́йшꙋю*); Priest: **"Glory to Thee, O Christ..."** (*Сла́ва Тебѣ̀, Христѐ*), and the rest according to custom;[^601] but the 1st Hour we do not join.

### Erection of the Lord's Tomb
On Friday morning, before the beginning of the Royal Hours, there are placed upon the Holy Table the Plashchanytsia and the most beautiful Gospel, and there is erected before the Royal Doors the Tomb of Christ, beside which, according to the custom of the Northern

and Western Latin Churches,[^602] the Holy Mysteries are exposed even until the Resurrection of Christ. And our Sluzhebnyks indeed command the Tabernacle (*Kivot*) with the Holy Mysteries to be placed upon such a Tomb; but the custom of our Cathedral Church is to expose the Holy Mysteries in the Monstrance (*Darookazalnytsia*) upon the High Altar, and to erect the Tomb of the Lord thus: above it there stands a Cross without the Crucified, with a shroud hung upon its crossbeam, and with the Instruments of Christ's Passion; it is adorned with costly cloth, and surrounded with many candlesticks and lamps, and vessels with small trees and flowers. The front board of the Sarcophagus is covered with crimson damask, to which is attached a white cloth, to which later the Plashchanytsia is to be pinned; the board itself is not flat, but inclined, so that the Plashchanytsia pinned to it may be visible and more accessible for kissing.

### Royal Hours
In the Cathedral Church they are sung at the 10th hour of the morning, together with the Typika (*Obidnytsia*) and with Vespers.[^603] We sing all things according to the Typikon of the Royal Hours of the Paramony of the Nativity of Christ, here on p. 181. However, since the Tomb is placed before the Royal Doors, the Analogion of the Gospel is placed a little below it, while the Analogion of the other Readings is placed still lower than this. And all things which at the Paramony of the Nativity

the sacred ministers say before the Royal Doors, here they say before the Tomb; but they do not cense the Tomb, because until now there is nothing within it.

### Typika (Obidnytsia)
This we join to the 9th Hour; however, we do not say it from the beginning, as on the Paramony of the Nativity, but from the Beatitudes, without Stichera, and it shall be thus:
1. The Beatitudes... **"Glory... Both now..."**; **"Remember us, O Lord"** (*Помянѝ на́съ, Го́споди*) thrice.
2. **"The heavenly choir"** (*Ли́къ небе́сный*), with the Verses; **"Both now"**: The Creed (*Вѣ́рую*).
3. **"Pardon, remit"** (*Ѡ҆сла́би, ѡ҆ста́ви*), **"Our Father"** (*Ѡ҆́тче на́шъ*), the Kontakion of Great Friday.
4. **"Lord, have mercy"** (*Го́споди, поми́луй*) 12 times; **"All-holy Trinity"** (*Всесвята́я Тро́йце*) by the Priest.
5. **"Blessed be the Name of the Lord"** (*Бꙋ́ди и҆́мя Госпо́дне*) thrice; **"I will bless the Lord"** (*Благословлю̀ Го́спода*).
6. **"It is truly right"** (*Досто́йно є҆́сть*) with **"More honorable than the Cherubim"** (*Честнѣ́йшꙋю херꙋві̑мъ*).
7. Priest: **"Glory to Thee, O Christ God"** (*Сла́ва Тебѣ̀, Христѐ Бо́же*), and the Dismissal of Great Friday, facing the people.
There is no Liturgy today under any circumstances, except for the Annunciation; but immediately after the Dismissal of the Typika we begin Vespers.

### On Great Friday Evening
#### At Vespers
*Note.*
1. At this Vespers there takes place an Entrance with the Gospel, and at its end, at the last Sticheron of the Aposticha, the Procession with the Plashchanytsia, and its deposition into the Tomb;[^604]

2. All actions which the sacred ministers perform at ordinary Vespers before the Iconostasis, at this Vespers they perform before the Tomb of Christ.

#### Typikon
1. From the usual beginning even unto **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Го́споди, воззва́хъ*), all as at daily Vespers, without a Kathisma.
2. At **"Lord, I have cried"**, Stichera for 6; **"Glory"**, one Sticheron; **"Both now"**, another. — Entrance with the Gospel.
3. Instead of the Prokeimenon of the day there shall be the Prokeimenon of the Triodion, and the Paremia, and the second Paremia, and the third Paremia; then the third Prokeimenon, which is of the Apostle, and the Apostle, and Alleluia, and the Gospel. At the Alleluia censing takes place. **"Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!"** we say only at the 1st and 3rd Prokeimena; the Gospel we begin from **"Wisdom! Stand upright!"** Instead of **"Glory to Thee, O Lord"**, before the Gospel we say: **"Glory to Thy Passion, O Lord"** (*Сла́ва страсте́мъ Твои̑мъ, Го́споди*); and after the Gospel: **"Glory to Thy longsuffering, O Lord"** (*Сла́ва долготерпѣ́нїю Твоему̀, Го́споди*); likewise also at Matins.
4. **"Let us all say"** (*Рце́мъ всѝ*), **"Vouchsafe"** (*Сподо́би*), **"Let us complete"** (*И҆спо́лнимъ*), and the Aposticha.
5. At the last Sticheron of the Aposticha, that is at **"Thou Who clothest Thyself with light"** (*Тебѐ ѡ҆дѣ́ющагося свѣ́томъ*), there takes place the procession with the Plashchanytsia, and its deposition into the Tomb, according to the custom of our Cathedral Churches, thus: During the chanting of the Aposticha the Procession is arranged in the church; and the Monstrance (*Darookazalnytsia*) having been placed

to the left on the Altar, the candles are lit on the Altar and on the Tomb, and the sacred ministers vest: the Hierarch and four most worthy Choir-clerics who are to carry the Plashchanytsia in all the sacred vestments of their Order, while the other Choir-clerics and Priests vest only in epitrachelia and phelonia, and others only in epitrachelia, and two Deacons in all the diaconal vestments. Burning candles are distributed to all the Choir-clerics and other Priests in the Sanctuary, and to the Brotherhood in the church; and the sacred ministers having approached the steps of the Altar and bowed deeply, the Hierarch ascends to the Altar, and having taken the Plashchanytsia spread out upon the Altar, hands it to four Choir-clerics, so that each holds one of its corners. Then the Deacon on the right ascends to the Altar, and having bowed deeply, brings out the Tabernacle (*Kivot*) with the Holy Mysteries, and having placed it upon the (folded) Iliton, takes out from it the Lamb with the pyx (*kletka*) in which It rests, not touching It with fingers, but holding by the pyx, and places It into the Monstrance (*Darookazalnytsia*), having securely closed it; and having closed the Tabernacle, he places it into the Tabernacle-shrine (*Skynia*), and having bowed and closed it, and having placed the Monstrance in the midst of the Iliton facing the people, he steps back a little to the side, until the Hierarch, kneeling, censes the Holy Mysteries thrice, each time with a bow at the end, and having taken upon himself the small omophorion (*omophorets*), approaches the Holy Table; for then the Deacon, having bowed down before the Holy Mysteries and taken Them with both hands, delivers Them to the Hierarch, who has first made a metania, who turns by the right toward the people; and the Singers beginning to sing **"Thou Who clothest Thyself"** (*Тебѐ ѡ҆дѣ́ющагося*), he goes out behind the Clergy from the Royal Doors by the north side of the Tomb into the church; and having entered under the Baldachin, surrounded by candle-bearers, with the two Deacons preceding and censing unceasingly, he advances behind all to the Doors of the church,

the Cross-bearer going before all, then the Banner-bearers, then four maidens bearing icons, then the Church Brotherhood, the students of the Seminary, the Choir of singers of the Seminary, then from the Royal Doors the Priests, the Choir-clerics, the four sacred dignitaries carrying the Plashchanytsia facing with its feet toward the people; and at the end the Hierarch with the Deacons censing, and the candle-bearers. As the procession exits from the church, it turns to the right, that is to its northern side, and having circled it thrice (or as they do there today, once), returns into the church; and those with the Plashchanytsia and the other Priests having halted before the Tomb, the Hierarch with the Deacons returns by the south side of the Tomb, through the Royal Doors, into the Sanctuary; and the Deacon on the right having received from his hands the Holy Mysteries and placed Them upon the Holy Table, the Hierarch, having bent his knees and laid aside the Omophorion (Velum), censes Them thrice with the bowing of his head; and the Deacon, having bowed, exposes Them above the Tabernacle-shrine (*Skynia*); then all three having bowed before the steps of the Altar, they go out, the Hierarch through the middle doors, and the Deacons through the side doors, before the Tomb; and the Hierarch, having received the Plashchanytsia from the Choir-clerics holding it, lays it upon the Tomb, and they pin it down to the Tomb; and when the Singers finish **"Now lettest Thou depart"** (*Ны́нѣ ѿпꙋща́еши*), and the Trisagion and the rest with **"Our Father"** (*Ѡ҆́тче на́шъ*), the Hierarch exclaims: **"For Thine is the Kingdom"** (*Я҆́ко Твоѐ є҆́сть Ца́рство*), and beginning, sings with the concelebrants the Troparion **"The noble Joseph"** (*Благообра́зный Іѡ́сифъ*), and at the end makes with the concelebrants a deep metania, while all the people make a great prostration to the ground; then for the second time the same Troparion the second Choir [sings], and all make a metania and prostration as before. And where there is only one Choir, then the first time this Troparion is sung by the sacred ministers, the second time by the Choir, the third time half by the sacred ministers, and the remainder from the Plashchanytsia and below

by the Choir.[^605] And after the chanting of the Troparion and after the prostrations they kiss the Plashchanytsia, all according to rank, having made before the kissing a single metania, first the Hierarch, then the other sacred ministers according to the order of their dignity; and there takes place the Great Dismissal proper to this Saturday, after which the sacred ministers return into the Sanctuary, and having left the Royal Doors open even until the end of Bright Week, divest themselves of the sacred vestments, and go each to his own home; while the people follow to kiss the Plashchanytsia.[^606]

*Note.*
1. Where there is only one Priest, let him choose for himself four most honorable lay men for the carrying of the Plashchanytsia.
2. The Pastor of souls, even if he has several churches, in the mother church sings Vespers with the carrying out of the Plashchanytsia, but in filial churches only carries out the Plashchanytsia, thus: after **"Blessed is our God"** (*Благослове́нъ Бо́гъ на́шъ*) and **"Come, let us worship"** (*Прїиди́те, поклони́мся*) thrice, immediately the Procession with **"Thou Who clothest Thyself"** (*Тебѐ ѡ҆дѣ́ющагося*), and after the Procession: **"Glory to Thee, O Christ God"** (*Сла́ва Тебѣ̀, Христѐ Бо́же*), and the Dismissal, and **"The noble Joseph"** (*Благообра́зный Іѡ́сифъ*) thrice, with prostrations.

### Little Compline
This, as also the Midnight Office, we read in private.
At Compline the Canon of the Crucifixion of the Lord, and of the Lament of the Most Holy Theotokos.
After **"It is truly right"**, the Kontakion of Great Saturday.

### Saturday Midnight Office
All according to custom.

### Matins of Great Saturday
*Note.*
1. This we sing usually on Friday evening after sunset, although the Typika appoint it in the 7th hour of the night.
2. The sacred ministers remain throughout the whole Matins before the Lord's Tomb, where seats are placed for them, and a table with

candlesticks and Books, so that sitting they may sing from there the Order, the Priests in epitrachelia and phelonia, and the Deacons in dalmatics, of violet color.

#### Typikon
1. From **"Blessed is our God"** (*Благослове́нъ Бо́гъ на́шъ*) even unto **"Come, let us worship"** (*Прїиди́те, поклони́мся*) exclusively, all as usually at Matins; then after the 12 **"Lord, have mercy"**, having omitted **"Come, let us worship"**, immediately the Priest: **"Glory to the holy..."** (*Сла́ва святѣ́й*), and the rest with the Six Psalms even unto **"The Lord is God"** (*Бо́гъ Госпо́дь*), as usually.[^607]
2. At **"The Lord is God"**, Troparion: **"The noble Joseph"** (*Благообра́зный Іѡ́сифъ*) twice. **"Glory"**: **"When Thou didst descend unto death"** (*Є҆гда̀ снизше́лъ є҆сѝ къ сме́рти*); **"Both now"**: **"Unto the Myrrh-bearing Women"** (*Мѵроно́сицамъ жена́мъ*).[^608]
3. Kathisma 17 (The Blameless / *Neporochni*) in three Stases, with the Funeral Troparia, with censing at the beginning of each Stasis, and the Little Ektenia after each Stasis.
Concerning the censing we deem that it will suffice to cense only the Plashchanytsia (together with the Holy Mysteries, if anywhere They are placed upon the tomb) and the Choirs and people.[^609]
Concerning the Funeral Troparia, called in the Greek Triodia *Ἐγκώμια* (Praises / *Pokhvaly*), be it known that before each of them is chanted one verse of the Kathisma, until all are completed; only the 1st Troparion of each Stasis (and in the editions of our Pochaiv Triodia also the 2nd Troparion of the 2nd Stasis)

is sung without a Verse, and this both at the beginning and at the end of the Stasis, except for the 3rd Stasis, after which the 1st Troparion is not sung, nor is the Little Ektenia said, but immediately **"The Angelic Host"** (*А҆́нгельскїй собо́ръ*), and after its chanting the Little Ektenia.
4. After **"The Angelic Host"** with the Little Ektenia: Sessional Hymn, and Psalm 50, and the Canon.
5. The Canon: Heirmoi twice, Troparia for 12. We say the refrain: **"Glory to Thee, O Lord, glory to Thy holy burial"** (*Сла́ва Го́споди, святѡ́му погребе́нїю Твоему̀*). Katavasia: the Heirmos of the Canon, both Choirs together.
After the 3rd Ode, the Sessional Hymn; after the 6th, the Kontakion and Ikos; at the 9th Ode we do not sing **"More honorable"** (*Честнѣ́йшꙋю*),[^610] and after the 9th [Ode] we do not say **"It is truly right"** (*Досто́йно є҆́сть*), but the Heirmos; and afterwards instead of the Exaposteilarion we sing **"Holy is the Lord our God"** (*Свѧ́тъ Госпо́дь Бо́гъ на́шъ*) thrice.
6. At the Praises, 4 Stichera; then one at **"Glory"**, and one at **"Both now"**.
7. At the Great Doxology we make the Entrance with the Gospel, and instead of the single Troparion which is after the Doxology, there shall be one Funeral Sticheron, and two Troparia, and three Readings, that is: one of Prophecy, one of the Apostle, and one of the Gospel.
And the Entrance takes place thus: During the chanting of the Stichera of the Praises, the Priest and Deacons, if there be any, having bowed deeply before the Plashchanytsia, together with the Holy Mysteries, go to the vestry and vest in all the sacred vestments of their Order. While the Doxastikon (*Naslavnyk*) is being sung, likewise the other sacred ministers who are before the Lord's Tomb, vested in phelonia, having bowed together deeply, depart into the Sanctuary;

and the Priest with the Deacons having come before the steps of the Altar, they also stand together with him on this side and that; and all having bowed down toward the Holy Mysteries, the Priest proclaims after the chanting of the Stichera of the Praises: **"Glory to Thee Who hast shown us the light"** (*Сла́ва Тебѣ̀ показа́вшему на́мъ свѣ́тъ*); then, having placed incense for the Deacon on the left, as the Doxology is ending he ascends to the Altar, and having taken the Gospel from the Holy Table, hands it to the Deacon on the right, and they make the Entrance in all things as takes place at the Liturgy, preceded by the candle-bearers, and the Deacon with the censer; and they halt before the Lord's Tomb, the Deacon on the right at the right hand, and the one on the left at the left hand of the Priest, closer to the Tomb, while the other sacred ministers are on two wings, a little lower, with the Priest, awaiting the end of the Trisagion and the Sticheron **"Come, let us bless Joseph"** (*Прїиди́те, оу҆блажи́мъ Іѡ́сифа*). When this has taken place, the first Deacon, raising his orarion toward the people, proclaims: **"Let us attend!"** (*Во́ньмемъ*); the Priest, signing the people: **"Peace be to all"** (*Ми́ръ всѣ̑мъ*); the second Deacon, raising his orarion: **"Wisdom, let us attend!"** (*Премꙋ́дрость, во́ньмемъ*); and we sing the Troparion **"The noble Joseph"** (*Благообра́зный Іѡ́сифъ*) once. The Deacon having placed the Gospel upon the Analogion facing the people, and the other having handed over the censer, we sing also the Troparion of the Prophecy once.[^611]

Then again: **"Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!"**, and the Prokeimenon, and the Reading of the Prophecy, and the other Prokeimenon, and the Reading of the Apostle, and Alleluia, at which the Deacons cense the Tomb, the presiding sacred ministers, and the people. After these, the first Deacon: **"Wis-**

**-dom! Stand upright! Let us hear..."**; Priest: **"The Reading from the Holy Gospel according to Matthew"**; Choir: **"Glory to Thy Passion, O Lord"** (*Сла́ва страсте́мъ Твои̑мъ, Го́споди*); the second Deacon: **"Let us attend!"**; and the Priest sings the Gospel facing the people; and at its conclusion, the Choir: **"Glory to Thy longsuffering, O Lord"** (*Сла́ва долготерпѣ́нїю Твоему̀, Го́споди*). Then the Priest, having kissed and closed the Gospel, leaves it upon the Analogion; and the first Deacon says the Ektenia **"Let us all say"** (*Рце́мъ всѝ*), and the second **"Let us complete"** (*И҆спо́лнимъ*), all according to custom.
8. [9 in original] After the Dismissal, having chanted the Troparion **"The noble Joseph"** (*Благообра́зный Іѡ́сифъ*) thrice with prostrations, as at Vespers, and the Deacon having taken the Gospel from the Analogion, the sacred ministers depart into the Sanctuary and divest themselves of their vestments; and the Deacon having placed the Gospel upon the Holy Table and bowed, departs and divests himself.

#### At the Hours
1. At all [Hours], the Troparion and Kontakion of Great Saturday.
2. The Dismissal of Matins of Great Saturday.

#### At the Typika (Obidnytsia)
As on Great Thursday, here on p. 435; however, the Greek Triodion does not appoint the Typika.

### On Great Saturday Evening
#### At Vespers with the Liturgy of Basil
1. According to the custom of the Cathedral Church, this Vespers begins at the 9th hour of the day.[^612]
2. The Vespers of this day is only one, and that great, with the Liturgy of Basil, and an All-Night Vigil shall not

take place; but after the Ambo Prayer, or (as the Typika have it) after the Dismissal of the Liturgy, there shall be the blessing of loaves, as usually at a Vigil.
3. The form and Typikon of this Vespers is the same as that of the Vespers with the Liturgy celebrated on the Paramony of the Nativity of Christ, here on p. 189, without **"Blessed is the man"** (*Блаже́нъ мꙋ́жъ*); only here after **"O Joyful Light"** (*Свѣ́те ти́хїй*) there shall be no Prokeimenon, but immediately the Readings, 15 in number, and between the Readings there shall be no Troparia; but at the 6th Reading all the Verses of the Canticle of Moses are read with the refrain: **"For gloriously is He glorified"** (*Сла́вно бо просла́вися*); and at the 15th Reading the Verses of the Canticle of the Three Youths are read with the refrain: **"Praise the Lord and exalt Him above all forever"** (*Го́спода по́йте, и҆ превозноси́те є҆го̀ во вѣ́ки*); and instead of Alleluia we sing the last verse of Psalm 81 with the other Verses thereof, in the manner of a Prokeimenon.[^613] Dismissal of Saturday Matins.
4. Although the Service of a Feast usually begins with the Vespers of the preceding day, and for this reason the entire Resurrection Service ought to be with bright sacred vestments, nevertheless, because the Liturgy with which Vespers is sung is still of Saturday, for this reason a portion of the Stichera sung at **"Lord, I have cried"** is concerning the descent of the Saviour's soul into Hades; and for this reason from the beginning of this Vespers we take violet vestments, but later we change them and take bright ones after the reading of the Apostle, at the verses replacing the Alleluia, so that we may read the Gospel already in bright vestments.[^614]

5. Throughout all of Bright Week, beginning from the Vespers of this day, the Resurrection Service is sung, and besides the Stichera of the Triodion there are sung also the Resurrection Stichera of the Octoechos, of a different Tone daily, that is: on Sunday, Tone 1; on Monday, Tone 2; on Tuesday, Tone 3; on Wednesday, Tone 4; on Thursday, Tone 5; on Friday, Tone 6; on Saturday, Tone 8, having skipped Tone 7.

#### At Compline
In Greek and Slavonic Triodia, except our own, Compline is not appointed; but from the Blessing of Loaves even unto the Funeral Order sung immediately before Paschal Matins, there is appointed the reading of the entire Book of the Acts of the Apostles, although among us today scarcely in Cathedral and Seminary Churches are these Acts sung; they are sung to verses by clerics alternating two by two.
This Compline would have to be small. For the rest, even our Triodion itself leaves to discretion whether to read it or not; for it says concerning it: "if it be." In that case, there is appointed in our Triodion the Canon of the Burial of Christ, and of the Lament of the Most Holy Theotokos. After the 9th Ode, instead of **"It is truly right"**, the Heirmos of this Ode; and after the Trisagion, the Kontakion of Great Saturday.

### Office in Place of the Midnight Office (Paschal Nocturns)[^615]
*Note.* This we sing before the Plashchanytsia immediately before Paschal Matins, either only in epitrachelion and phelonion, as usually in the provinces, or in all the sacred vestments, of violet color, as in Cathedral Churches.

#### Typikon
1. From the usual beginning even unto **"Come, let us worship"** (*Прїиди́те, поклони́мся*), we say all things as usually at the Midnight Office; and immediately Psalm 50 and the Canon.
2. The Funeral Canon of Great Saturday Matins: **"The wave of the sea"** (*Волно́ю морско́ю*); according to the Typikon, the Heirmoi twice, and the Troparia for 12 (today with the Heirmos for 4), with the refrain: **"Glory to Thee, O Lord, glory to Thy holy burial"** (*Сла́ва Го́споди, святѡ́му погребе́нїю Твоему̀*); and at the end the Katavasia, the Heirmos, both Choirs together.
After the 3rd Ode, the Sessional Hymn; and after the 6th, the Kontakion and Ikos of Great Saturday; after the 9th [Ode] there is no **"It is truly right"**, but the Heirmos of this Ode.
3. Trisagion, and the rest with **"Our Father"** (*Ѡ҆́тче на́шъ*), and the Troparion **"When Thou didst descend unto death"** (*Є҆гда̀ снизше́лъ є҆сѝ къ сме́рти*).
4. The Ektenia **"Have mercy on us, O God"** (*Поми́луй на́съ, Бо́же*), which is at the Midnight Office, and the Dismissal of Great Saturday Matins,[^616] beginning from **"Glory to Thee, O Christ God"** (*Сла́ва Тебѣ̀, Христѐ Бо́же*).
5. [4 in original] Then toward the end: **"The noble Joseph"** (*Благообра́зный Іѡ́сифъ*) thrice with prostrations, as after Great Saturday Matins; however, the Plashchanytsia is kissed only by the sacred ministers, according to rank; and the Hierarch, having taken the Plashchanytsia from the Tomb, delivers it to four Choir-clerics, and thus all the sacred ministers return into the Sanctuary; and four Choir-clerics having hung the Plashchanytsia behind the Holy Table above the High Place,[^617] they go to the vestry, and having divested themselves of the violet vestments, vest in all the bright sacred vestments of their Order; and at that time all the candles in the church are lit.

*Note Concerning the Plashchanytsia.*
Concerning the time and place of the deposition of the Plashchanytsia we find no ancient Typikon; for this rite is later, and varies according to different regions. According to the custom of our First-Cathedral Church, the Plashchanytsia remains hung behind the Holy Table above the High Place from Paschal Matins even unto the Apodosis of Pascha inclusively, that is until the Wednesday before the Ascension. According to the custom of other churches of ours, the Plashchanytsia, having been taken from the Tomb before Paschal Matins, is placed upon the Holy Table, where it lies throughout all of Bright Week, and from Thomas Sunday even unto the Apodosis of Pascha, behind the Holy Table. According to others, it lies upon the Holy Table from Pascha until its Apodosis.[^618] When therefore the Plashchanytsia lies upon the Holy Table, the Iliton with the Gospel is placed upon it, and the Liturgy is celebrated upon it. However, it is better to keep the Plashchanytsia behind the Holy Table, because lying upon the Holy Table it is not a little damaged by candles dripping upon it.

## Beginning of the Holy Pentecostarion

### Procession with the Holy Mysteries Around the Church
After the taking of the Plashchanytsia from the Tomb and carrying it into the Sanctuary, the sacred ministers, having vested in all the brightest sacred vestments of their Order, go out before the steps of the Altar, and having all bowed equally and deeply toward the Holy Mysteries, bend their knees, and take lighted candles; likewise also the Brotherhood in the church. The Deacon on the right (or the Priest himself, if there be no Deacons), having approached the middle of the Holy Table and bowed deeply, sets aside the Gospel on the right side thereof, and having taken the Holy Mysteries from the top of the Tabernacle (*Skynia*), and having placed them upon the Iliton, steps back a little to the side; and the Hierarch (or presiding Priest), having censed the Holy Mysteries thrice according to custom, and put on the Omophorion, standing up and ascending the steps, and having bowed down, receives the Holy Mysteries from the hands of the Deacon, turns toward the people, and when he begins to sing the Sticheron **"Jesus is risen from the tomb"** (*Воскре́съ І҆исꙋ́съ ѿ гро́ба*), while all the bells chime joyfully, the Procession around the church takes place, in all respects as was depicted on Great Friday at Vespers, here on page 445, except for the Plashchanytsia, which here, by order of Cardinal Sembratovych of blessed memory, is not to be carried, because it does not agree with the joy of the Resurrection; rather, it is fitting to carry the Icon of the Resurrection, as the Triodion has it at the beginning of Paschal Matins during the Procession to the church doors. And when the Superior begins to sing the Sticheron **"Jesus is risen from the tomb"**, the Choirs and the people follow in singing the same, and they sing it, repeating it until the Procession is completed, that is, a threefold, or at least a single circuit around the church, which when it is completed, all enter into the church, while the banner-bearers and icon-bearers remain before the church doors to avoid commotion during the second Procession; while in the church the cross-bearer with two candle-bearers, the Brotherhood, the Choirs, and the Clergy arrange themselves for the Procession; and when the sacred ministers have entered into the Sanctuary, the Superior ascends the Altar, and having turned toward the people, blesses them with the Holy Mysteries, saying in a gentle voice: **"Save, O God, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance"** (*Спасѝ, Бо́же, лю́ди Твоя̀, и҆ благословѝ достоя́нїе Твоѐ*); and having set down the Holy Mysteries upon the Altar, he descends before the steps, bends his knees, and having laid aside the Omophorion, censes the Holy Mysteries thrice according to custom; and the Deacon, having approached the Holy Mysteries and bowed deeply, removes them with the lunette from the Monstrance (*Darookazalnytsia*), and places them into the Tabernacle (*Kivot*),

---


and the Tabernacle (*Kivot*) into the Ark (*Skynia*), and having bowed down, closes it. And where there is no Monstrance (*Darookazalnytsia*), the Holy Mysteries are exposed with the Tabernacle (*Kivot*), and with it are closed in the Ark (*Skynia*).

*Note.* This Procession with the Holy Mysteries before Paschal Matins is a later adornment of our Churches, borrowed neither from the Greeks nor from the Romans, but from the Latin Churches of these regions, and an added magnificence to our Paschal Matins; nor is it set down in our Typika.

## Paschal Matins

1. **Procession Before the Church Doors.** As all the people go out from the church, the sacred ministers likewise go out after them, and the Procession takes place thus: when the sacred ministers have taken their stand, in all their bright sacred vestments, before the steps of the Holy Table, the Superior takes the Cross, and the Deacon the Censer; and when all have bowed equally and deeply toward the Holy Mysteries and turned toward the west, the Superior begins to sing the Sticheron **"Thy Resurrection, O Christ Savior"** (*Воскресе́нїе Твоѐ, Христѐ Спа́се*); and while all follow in singing the same, they go out from the Sanctuary, the Priests indeed through the Royal Doors, and the other sacred ministers through the Deacon's Doors into the church, and while all the bells are ringing, they go even unto the great Church Doors; and having gone outside the church, they stand orderly, facing the church doors, which are immediately closed.[^619] And inside the church there are placed upon the

Holy Table, first of all the Icon of the Resurrection of Christ, usually upon the Artos, and other venerable Icons, and Reliquaries, and the most precious Gospels, or whatever else of sacred things, all these according to the number of Priests who intend to give them to the people to kiss at the end of Matins; while outside before the church doors the beginning of Matins takes place thus.[^620]

2. **Beginning of Matins.** Immediately upon the closing of the church doors, the presiding Priest, holding in his left hand the Cross, and in his right the Censer, and censing the church doors,[^621] proclaims with a loud voice: **"Glory to the holy..."** (*Сла́ва ст҃ѣ́й*), and the rest.

and when the Choirs have answered: **"Amen"**, having handed over the Censer, he sings with a loud voice the Troparion **"Christ is risen"**, then all sing the same; and a second time the Priest sings the same, and all the same; and a third time the Priest sings the same, and all the same; then the Priest in a clear voice proclaims the Verses, while all after each verse chant the same Troparion once.[^622] Finally the Priest, having sung in a higher voice the same Troparion only up to **"trampling down death by death"** inclusively, opens the doors, while all conclude: **"and upon those in the tombs bestowing life"**; and while all the bells are rung again, all enter into the church.[^623] But the Morning Prayers at

Paschal Matins throughout all of Bright Week are not said, because these are linked to the Six Psalms, and since the latter here give way, the Prayers also give way. If the Prayers were to be said, the Typikon would have had to indicate where to say them, for during the Verses **"Let God arise"**, which occupy the place of the Six Psalms, they cannot be said, for at that time the Typikon commands the Priest to pronounce the verses, and not to add the Prayers besides.

3. **Entrance into the Church.** The Entrance of the Procession into the church is made in the reverse order from that in which it went out from the church, that is, first the Superior enters, then the other Priests and sacred ministers, and the entire Clergy, then the Chanters, the Brotherhood, the banner-bearers and icon-bearers, the cross-bearer, and all the people. And the Priests indeed enter into the Sanctuary and approach the Holy Table, while the Hierarch, if there be one, ascends his Throne; and the Deacon takes his stand at his place before the Royal Doors, that he may sing the Ektenia from there.

4. After the entrance into the church, the first Deacon says the Great Ektenia **"In peace let us pray to the Lord"** (*Ми́ромъ Го́споду помоли́мся*), standing at the accustomed place, and immediately we sing the Canon.

5. **The Paschal Canon:** Heirmoi 4 times (in the Greek 2 times), and Troparia for 12. (Today Heirmoi once, and Troparia with the Heirmos for 4). Katavasia after each Ode, the Heirmos of the Canon, by both Choirs together

once. The Priest sings the Heirmoi, and the Choirs sing the Troparia and Katavasia. Refrain to the Troparia of the Canon: **"Christ is risen from the dead"** (*Христо́съ воскре́се и҆зъ ме́ртвыхъ*); at the penultimate, **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*), and at the last, **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*), according to custom.

At the beginning of the Canon, after the singing of the Heirmos, the Priest censes all things according to custom.[^624]

The Little Ektenia after each Ode is said by the Deacons standing at their accustomed place before the Royal Doors, with their proper Exclamations sung in turn by the Priests in the holy Sanctuary.[^625]

After the 3rd Ode, the Hypakoe; after the 6th, the Kontakion and Ikos, and **"Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ"** (*Воскресе́нїе Христо́во ви́дѣвше*) thrice, and **"Jesus is risen from the tomb"** (*Воскре́съ І҆исꙋ́съ ѿ гро́ба*) once (according to others, thrice); at the 9th Ode, at **"The Theotokos and Mother of Light"** (*Богоро́дицу и҆ Ма́терь Свѣ́та*) the customary censing takes place, and instead of **"More honorable"** (*Честнѣ́йшꙋю*) we sing **"The Angel cried"** (*А҆́нгелъ вопїя́ше*) and **"Shine, shine"** (*Свѣти́ся, свѣти́ся*); after the 9th Ode, the Exaposteilarion of Pascha thrice.

6. **At the Praises**, 8 Stichera: 4 Resurrectional of the Octoechos, Tone 1, with the customary refrains from the Praises, and 4 of Pascha, with their proper refrains; then **"Glory... Both now..."** of Pascha, and **"Christ is risen"** thrice, then many times until the kiss is completed.

7. **The Kiss of Peace.** This in the Typika is appointed at **"Christ is risen"** at the conclusion of the last Sticheron of Pascha; nevertheless, today we do not kiss one another, as the Sticheron has it, saying: **"and let us embrace one another"**, but only the Venerable Cross, the Gospel, and the Holy Icons held by the Priests, who greet each of those approaching with the words: **"Christ is risen"**, and receive the response: **"Truly He is risen"**. Furthermore, we begin the kiss not after the Paschal Stichera, but at their beginning at the Verse **"Let God arise"**, because of the many people approaching for the kiss. When very many approach, the kiss takes place even during the Canon. And this kiss according to our custom takes place thus: if there be only one Priest, he, standing in the Royal Doors, gives the Cross from there to the people to kiss, saying to each: **"Christ is risen"**, and the one kissing answers: **"Truly He is risen"**.
