If there be more Priests, first the Superior, having gone out with the Cross in his hand from the Royal Doors, stands before the Iconostasis on the right side, that is, by the Icon of the Savior, looking toward the north; and the second Priest, having gone out after him from the Royal Doors with the Gospel, approaches him, and the Superior gives him the Cross to kiss, saying: **"Christ is risen"**, and he having kissed it gives him the Gospel to kiss, answering: **"Truly He is risen"**, and takes his stand at his left hand; then the third Priest, having gone out with the Icon of the Resurrection of Christ, approaches the first, who gives him the Cross to kiss, saying: **"Christ is risen"**, and he having kissed it gives him the Icon of the Resurrection to kiss, answering: **"Truly He is risen"**; and he approaches the second Priest, who gives him the Gospel to kiss, saying: **"Christ is risen"**, and he having kissed the Gospel gives him the Icon of the Resurrection to kiss, answering: **"Truly He is risen"**, and

takes his stand at the left hand of the second. And the fourth Priest, having gone out with the Icon of the Theotokos, approaches the first, then the second and the third, each of whom gives him his sacred object to kiss, saying: **"Christ is risen"**, and he having kissed it gives each of them the Icon of the Theotokos to kiss, answering: **"Truly He is risen"**; and in this manner do also the other Priests, as many as there be. In Cathedral Churches the Hierarch, sitting upon his Throne by the Icon of the Savior, gives the Cross to kiss, and likewise the Choir-clerics, each giving his sacred image to kiss, sitting upon the benches. When the kiss has been completed among the Priests, then the other sacred ministers and all the people approach one by one to each Priest according to rank, beginning from the first, and kiss his sacred emblem, answering: **"Truly He is risen"**.[^626] After the kiss there was sometimes read the Catechetical Homily of Chrysostom, which is in the Flowery Triodion.

8. **Ektenias:** **"Have mercy on us, O God"** (*Поми́луй на́съ, Бо́же*) and **"Let us complete"** (*И҆спо́лнимъ*), by the Deacons standing at the accustomed place.

9. **Great Dismissal**, proper, at which in place of **"More honorable"** we say **"Shine, shine"**, and in place of **"Glory... Both now..."** we say **"Christ is risen"** once. The Priest having taken his stand with the Cross in the Holy Doors, the Deacon (if there be none, the Priest himself) says: **"Wisdom!"** Choir (or second Deacon, if there be one):[^627] **"Bless!"**

> **Priest:** **"He Who is blessed and most glorified, Christ our God, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."**

> **Choir:** **"Amen. Confirm, O God, the Catholic faith, unto the ages of ages."**

> **Priest:** **"Most Holy Theotokos, save us!"**

> **Choir:** **"Shine, shine, O New Jerusalem... at the resurrection of thy Son."**

> **Priest:** **"Glory to Thee, O Christ God, our hope, glory to Thee!"**

> **Choir:** **"Christ is risen from the dead... bestowing life. Lord, have mercy"** (thrice), **"Lord, bless!"**

> **Priest:** "Dismissal with the Cross: **"May Christ, Who is risen from the dead, trampling down death by death, and upon those in the tombs bestowing life, our true God, through the prayers of His most pure Mother, of the holy, glorious, and all-praised Apostles, of our venerable and God-bearing Fathers, and of all the Saints, have mercy on us and save us, for He is good and the Lover of mankind."**"

Then elevating the Cross, he says: **"Christ is risen!"** thrice, and we each time answer: **"Truly

**He is risen!"** Then we sing the final **"Christ is risen"** thrice, the entire Troparion, and after it we conclude by chanting: **"And unto us He hath granted eternal life: we worship His three-day resurrection."**[^628]

### Note Concerning the Doors of the Iconostasis
"Be it known also this, that the Doors of the Royal Altar, both the great ones and the small ones at the sides, throughout all the churches during all of Bright Week we do not close, even at the time of Communion" (*Typikon of our Pochaiv Triodion* and *Russian Typika*).

## Paschal Hours
For each of the four Hours, likewise also for Compline and the Midnight Office, throughout all of Bright Week we say the brief Office set down in the Triodion, and in our Horologia and Prayer Books at Paschal Matins.

*Note.* If the Hours are read together, then we say **"Blessed is our God"** only at the first, while the rest we begin from **"Christ is risen"** thrice.

## At the Divine Liturgy
All things according to the general Typikon of a Feast of the Lord; only:

1. In place of **"O Heavenly King"** at the Proskomide and at the Liturgy, and wherever it may occur, we say **"Christ is risen"** thrice, even unto the Ascension; while from the Ascension unto the

Descent [of the Holy Spirit], in place of **"O Heavenly King"** we say nothing.[^629]

2. Concerning **"Christ is risen"** it should be known that after **"Glory to Thee, O Christ God"**, before the Dismissal of the Proskomide and Liturgy, in place of **"Glory... Both now..."**, we say **"Christ is risen"** once, **"Lord, have mercy"** thrice, and **"Lord, bless"**.

After **"Blessed is the Kingdom"** we sing **"Christ is risen"** thrice; that is, the Priest once, and both Choirs once each; (although our Triodion says: the Priest thrice, and the Choirs the same thrice each); and where there is only one Choir, the Priest once and the Choir once, then half by the Priest, and half (from **"and upon those in the tombs"**) by the Choir.

Likewise also after **"With fear of God"**, in place of **"Blessed is He that cometh"**, and in place of **"We have seen the true Light"**, **"Christ is risen"** once; while in place of **"Let our mouths be filled"** and **"Blessed be the name of the Lord"**, **"Christ is risen"** thrice.[^630] Our people usually say **"Christ

is risen"** also in place of **"Be Thou exalted above the heavens, O God"**; nevertheless, this impairs the memorial of the Mystery of Christ's Ascension, which was originally instituted at this place of the Liturgy. Our Priests also say **"Christ is risen"** (only these two words) after **"With fear of God"**, and after **"Save, O God"**, and after **"Always, now and forever"**; nevertheless, in no Typikon do we find this.

3. In place of the Trisagion we sing **"All who have been baptized into Christ"** (*Є҆ли́цы во Хрїста̀ крести́стеся*).

4. At the Reading of the Gospel, after each verse they strike all the bells.[^631]

"If the Liturgy is celebrated concelebratedly" (say our Sluzhebnyks), "then this Gospel is read by all the Priests and Deacons by verses, as the Superior shall arrange." And concerning this we have a detailed Typikon in our Triodia and the Russian ones. Nevertheless, to read one and the same Gospel by all the Priests and Deacons is a superfluous invention of the Graeco-Slavs, which today among us has a place only when the Gospel is to be

read in various languages, as takes place in our Cathedral Churches, wherein the Hierarch reads the same in the Greek tongue from the Holy Doors, then the Deacon in Slavonic from the Ambo, and at the end the Choir-clerics and Priests in various liturgical languages, positioned throughout the church. In Russia the Hierarch reads in Slavonic, the other Priests in Hebrew, Greek, and Latin, in which the Title of the Cross was written, and then in other modern languages (Muralt, *Epistle 14*, p. 142). The Greeks likewise read the Gospel according to modern custom in various languages, yet not at the Liturgy, but at Vespers (the Gospel which is read at Vespers of this day), as takes place in the Patriarchate of Constantinople.[^632]

5. In place of **"It is truly right"**, according to the new Typikon we sing the Heirmos **"Shine, shine"**, with the refrain **"The Angel cried"**, as the Triodion and Constantinople Typikon have it, and not also with the Troparia of the 9th Ode of the Canon, as our Sluzhebnyks have, because these Troparia are not concerning the Most Holy Theotokos, but concerning Christ's Resurrection.

6. [7 in original] Before the Dismissal, in place of **"More honorable"** we say **"Shine, shine"**, and in place of **"Glory... Both now..."**: **"Christ is risen"** once; the Dismissal itself with the Cross, and the rest that follow after the Dismissal with the elevation of the Cross, without change as indicated at Paschal Matins.

7. **Blessing of the Artos.** After the Ambo Prayer[^633] takes place the blessing of the Artos, for which thou hast a prayer in the Sluzhebnik after the Liturgy of Pascha; and at the end of the prayer, the sprinkling of the Artos with holy water. And this

Artos with the Feast, that is, with the Icon of the Resurrection, after the Dismissal of the Liturgy is carried out from the Sanctuary where it was blessed onto the Tetrapod, where it remains even unto Bright Saturday, and is kissed by the faithful entering the church. On Bright Saturday at the Liturgy after the Ambo Prayer, the Priest having gone out from the Holy Doors reads over it the Prayer for the Breaking thereof, set down in our Sluzhebnik only in the order of Bright Saturday; and thereafter having been taken from the Tetrapod, it is broken in the Sacristy, and on the morrow, that is, on Thomas Sunday after the Ambo Prayer, it is distributed to the faithful by the Priest standing in the Holy Doors.[^634]

*Note.* Throughout all of Bright Week there is dispensation for meat.

### Blessing of Paschal Foods
This, according to our custom and according to the Typikon of the Triodion, takes place outside the church, and that either after the conclusion of the Divine Liturgy (and then going out from the church we sing the Paschal Stichera, optionally), or after Matins, or in homes on Saturday evening. The Order of this Blessing thou hast in the Sluzhebnik, in the Trebnik, in the Triodion, and in the Prayer Book after the Paschal Hours, with the sprinkling of holy water at the end.

## On the Day of Pascha at Vespers
### At Vespers
#### Note
1. The sacred ministers, vested in all the sacred vestments of their order, stand throughout all of Vespers in the Sanctuary beside the Holy Table, going out only for the Entrance (and the Deacons also for the Ektenias) before the Holy Doors, and for the final kissing of the sacred emblems, if there be more Priests, as was appointed at Matins.[^635]
2. It is censed thrice, that is, at the customary beginning only before the Holy Table, then at **"Lord, I have cried"**, and at **"Gladsome Light"** according to custom.
3. The Entrance takes place with the Gospel which is to be read.

#### Typikon
1. The Priest and Deacons, if there be any, vested in all the sacred vestments of their order, come before the steps of the Altar and bow deeply according to custom; and the Priest having taken the censer, with the customary prayer of blessing of incense, and censing before the Holy Table, proclaims: **"Blessed is our God"**;[^636] then having handed over the censer, and ascended the steps with the Deacons, he sings **"Christ is risen"** once, and both Choirs once each. After this the Priest says clearly **"Let God arise"** and the other Verses, with **"Glory... Both now..."**, while the Choirs sing **"Christ is risen"** once or thrice after each Verse, without change as indicated at the beginning of Matins. Finally the Priest: **"Christ is risen"** up to **"trampling down death by death"**, and the Choirs: **"and upon those in the tombs bestowing life"**, and the rest.
2. **"In peace let us pray to the Lord"** the Priest says beside the Holy Table, or the Deacon before the Iconostasis.
3. **"Lord, I have cried"** in Tone 2, with the customary censing; and the Resurrection Stichera of the Octoechos of Tone 2, set down in the Triodion; **"Glory"** of the Triodion, **"Both now"**, the 1st Theotokion (Dogmatikon) of Tone 2.
4. **Entrance with the Gospel** and customary censing at **"Gladsome Light"**; and after the Great Prokimenon, the Deacon (if there be none, the Priest himself): **"And that we may be accounted worthy..."**; Priest: **"The Reading from John..."**; Deacon: **"Let us attend!"**

then the Priest himself reads the Gospel from the Holy Doors (from John, pericope 65, up to: "I will not believe").

5. **"Let us all say"**, **"Vouchsafe, O Lord"**, and **"Let us complete"**.

6. **At the Aposticha:** One Resurrection Sticheron of the Octoechos, set down in the Triodion; then the Paschal Stichera with their refrains and the kissing of the Cross, or also of the other sacred emblems, without change as was set down at Matins; and after the final **"Christ is risen"**, immediately the Dismissal with the Cross, and the rest without change as set down at Matins.

### Compline and Midnight Office
All things according to the pattern of the Paschal Hours, here on page 468.

## General Typikon for All Days of Bright Week
### At Vespers
All things as on the day of Pascha at evening, here on page 473, except for the Entrance, which takes place with the Censer and not with the Gospel, and there is no Gospel.

#### Note
According to the earlier Typikon, the Priest at Vespers of Bright Week, from Monday onward, is not to use all the sacred vestments, but from the beginning only the epitrachelion, and from the Entrance onward also the phelonion.[^637]

Among us the custom holds to use all the sacred vestments during the first three days of this Week, that is, on Sunday, Monday, and Tuesday evening, because our people celebrate these three days by abstaining from labor and by attending divine services. On the other days of this Week it suffices to use only the epitrachelion and phelonion.

### Compline and Midnight Office
According to the pattern of the Paschal Hours, here on page 473.

### At Matins
All things according to the Typikon of Pascha, here on page 460; only:
1. For its beginning we do not go out before the church doors, but begin it in the Sanctuary, censing before the Holy Table.[^638]
2. We say the Little Ektenia only after the 3rd, 6th, and 9th Odes.

### At the Hours
Without change as on the day of Pascha; only on Bright Saturday the 9th Hour is already said with three Psalms according to custom, and at it the Resurrection Troparion, Tone 8: **"From on high"** (*Съ высоты̀*); and the Kontakion of Pascha: **"Though Thou didst descend into the grave"** (*А҆́ще и҆ во гро́бъ*).

### At the Divine Liturgy
1. All things as on the day of Pascha, changing daily the Prokimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, and Gospel.

2. On Saturday after the Ambo Prayer we read the prayer for the breaking of the Artos, set down in our Sluzhebnik in the order of Bright Saturday.[^639]

## General Typika from Thomas Sunday unto the Ascension
The divine services from Thomas Sunday even unto the Ascension could be compressed into a general Rule within a Feast of the Lord. But since each Sunday, beginning from Thomas Sunday, has a distinct Title and a distinct Office extending to the other weekdays of the same week, and is as it were another Feast within the Feast of Pascha, for this reason in place of the Paschal Office there enters the Office of the Sunday, likewise also on weekdays, together with certain parts of the Resurrection Office of the Octoechos, except for the eight-day Feast of Mid-Pentecost, to which on weekdays the Sunday Office yields. The general rules of this time are the following:

1. At the beginning of every office, in place of **"O Heavenly King"** we sing **"Christ is risen"** thrice;[^640] and at the conclusion of every divine service, according to our custom, **"Christ is risen"** thrice; then at the conclusion we add: **"And unto us

**He hath granted eternal life: we worship His three-day resurrection"**;[^641] and before the Dismissal, in place of **"More honorable"** we sing **"Shine, shine"**.[^642]

2. **At Vespers:** On Sunday evening for Monday an Entrance does not take place, as takes place on the Sundays of Great Lent (for here there is no Great Prokimenon, and the Triodia appoint the Ektenia **"Have mercy on us, O God"** at the conclusion of Vespers), except on Thomas Sunday, which, as a Feast of the Lord, has also a Great Prokimenon. For the rest, in the provinces on all Sunday evenings they make the Entrance even without a Feast.

3. **At Compline:** From this day we begin to sing the Canons to the Theotokos. After **"It is truly right"** we say throughout the whole week the Kontakion of the Sunday; only during the octave of the Feast of Mid-Pentecost, the Kontakion of Mid-Pentecost.

4. **The Midnight Office** we sing according to custom; only after the 1st Trisagion on the Feast of Mid-Pentecost we say the Troparion of Mid-Pentecost, and at the Apodosis of Pascha the Sunday Resurrection Troparion with its Theotokion; and after the 2nd Trisagion within Mid-Pentecost we say the Kontakion of Mid-Pentecost, but outside Mid-Pentecost the Kontakion of Pascha; while the prayers **"Remember, O Lord"** we do not say.

5. **Matins** we begin from **"Glory to the holy..."**, and immediately **"Christ is risen"** thrice, then **"Glory to God in the highest"**, and the rest; and before Psalm 50 we say **"Having beheld the Resurrection"**\n Christ", on Sundays indeed thrice, but on Weekdays once.[^643]

---


6. **At the Hours:** after **"Blessed is our God"** (*Благослове́нъ Бо́гъ на́шъ*), we say **"Christ is risen"** (*Христо́съ воскре́се*) thrice; and after the Dismissal, **"Christ is risen"** thrice, with the addition: **"And unto us He hath granted eternal life"**, and the rest.

*Note.* Since we do not say **"Blessed is our God"** when we join an Hour with Matins, or with the preceding [Hour], and likewise since we do not say the Dismissal when we join an Hour with the following [Hour], on this account neither do we say **"Christ is risen"** at that time.

7. **At the Liturgy:** we say **"Christ is risen"** in the same places as on the Resurrection of Christ, here on pages 468, 469.

The first two Antiphons are Resurrectional, but the third is the weekday one with the Resurrectional Refrain; likewise also at **"Come, let us worship"** (*Прїидѣ́те поклони́мся*), both on Sundays and on weekdays.

We conclude the Troparia with the Kontakion of Pascha,[^644] except in Thomas Week and [during the Mid-Feast of] Pentecost, for then we conclude them with their Kontakion, as the Triodion has it; although in Thomas Week also the Troparia may be concluded with the Kontakion of Pascha, as our Sluzhebnyks, and our manuscript Typikon, and the *Tserkovne Oko* do.

In place of **"It is truly meet"** (*Досто́йно є҆́сть*), we sing **"The Angel cried"** (*А҆́нгелъ вопїя́ше*) and **"Shine, shine"** (*Свѣти́ся, свѣти́ся*), except during the Octave of the Mid-Feast, which has its own Heirmos.

We say the Dismissal of Pascha, except in Thomas Week, which has its own Dismissal. And we say the Dismissal with

the Cross only on Sundays,[^645] and on the Feast of the Mid-Feast,[^646] and at the Apodosis of Pascha.[^647] We deem that on certain other days also, when there is a sufficient number of people, it will be well to say the Dismissal with the Cross, yet not on all days, as some do, having for this neither Typikon nor general custom.

8. The Service of a saint without Polyeleos occurring on these Sundays, and the rest even unto Pentecost, and on the Feast of the Mid-Feast, and at the Apodosis of Pascha, we sing at Compline.

## Proper Typika
### From Thomas Sunday to the Ascension

### Thomas Sunday
**2nd from Pascha. Tone 1.**

We celebrate an All-Night Vigil.

We sing all things according to the General Typikon of a First-Class Feast of the Lord, yet at Great Vespers without Readings, and with **"Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ"** (*Воскресе́нїе Христо́во*) thrice (according to others, once) at Matins, and without proper Antiphons at the Liturgy; and the Feast has its Apodosis on the following Saturday.

#### Weekdays of Thomas Week

On weekdays even unto the Sunday of the Myrrh-bearers, we sing all things according to the General Typikon inside the Feast

of the Feast; only at the Aposticha of Vespers, and at the Sessional Hymns of Matins which follow the 1st Kathisma-reading, and at the Praises (for there are Stichera both at the Praises and afterwards at the Aposticha) there are certain Stichera, taken from the Weekday Service of the Octoechos, according to the Tone and day of the week, except on Saturday, which has the entire Service festal. Likewise at Matins before Psalm 50 we say **"Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ"** (*Воскресе́нїе Христо́во*) once. And on Saturday we sing 3 Canons: of the Feast with the Heirmoi for 6, and of the Saint to whom the Temple is dedicated for 4, and of the Menaion for 4.

### Sunday of the Myrrh-Bearers
**3rd from Pascha. Tone 2.**

#### At Great Vespers

1. **"Blessed is our God"** (*Благослове́нъ Бо́гъ на́шъ*); **"Christ is risen"** (*Христо́съ воскре́се*) thrice; Trisagion and the rest with the **"Our Father"** (*О҆́тче на́шъ*); **"Lord, have mercy"** (*Го́споди поми́луй*) 12; **"Come, let us worship"** (*Прїидѣ́те поклони́мся*) thrice.
2. At **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Го́споди воззва́хъ*), 10 Stichera: 4 Resurrectional, and 6 of the Myrrh-bearers (3 from **"Lord, I have cried"**, and 3 from the Litiya); **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) of the Myrrh-bearers; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*), the 1st Theotokion of the Tone (Dogmatikon).
3. At the Aposticha, the Stichera of Pascha, with their refrains; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*): **"Thou Who clothest Thyself"** (*Тебѐ ѡ҆дѣ́ющагося*); **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): **"This is the day of Resurrection"** (*Воскресе́нїя де́нь*).[^648]
4. After the final Trisagion: Resurrectional Troparion, Tone 2; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*): **"The noble Joseph"** (*Благообра́зный І҆ѡ́сифъ*); **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): of the Myrrh-bearers.
5. Dismissal of Pascha, without the Cross.

#### At Compline

After **"It is truly meet"** (*Досто́йно є҆́сть*), Kontakion of the Myrrh-bearers.

#### At Great Matins

1. At **"The Lord is God"** (*Бо́гъ Госпо́дь*): Resurrectional Troparion twice; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*): **"The noble Joseph"** (*Благообра́зный*); **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): of the Myrrh-bearers.
2. After the 1st and 2nd Kathisma-readings, Sessional Hymns of the Myrrh-bearers; and after the Evlogitaria (*Neporóchny*), **"The Angelic Host"** (*А҆́нгельскїй Собо́ръ*), and the Hypakoe of the Tone, and all the rest even unto the Canon is Resurrectional according to custom; the Resurrectional Gospel is the 2nd, and **"Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ"** (*Воскресе́нїе Христо́во*) thrice (according to others, once).
3. 3 Canons for 14: of Pascha with the Heirmos for 4, and of the Theotokos in the Triodion for 2, and of the Myrrh-bearers for 8. Katavasia of Pascha.

After the 3rd Ode, Sessional Hymn of the Triodion;[^649] after the 6th, Kontakion and Ikos of the Triodion; at the 9th, we do not sing **"More honorable"** (*Честнѣ́йшꙋю*),[^650] but **"The Angel cried"** (*А҆́нгелъ вопїя́ше*) and **"Shine, shine"** (*Свѣти́ся*); after the 9th, **"Holy is the Lord our God"** (*Свѧ́тъ Госпо́дь Бо́гъ на́шъ*) thrice, and the Exaposteilarion of Pascha once; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) of the Myrrh-bearers; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*), the same.

4. From the Praises even unto the end, all things according to the General Typikon of every Sunday.

#### At the Hours

At all [the Hours]: Resurrectional Troparion; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*): **"The noble Joseph"** (*Благообра́зный*). Kontakion of the Myrrh-bearers.

#### At the Liturgy

1. After the Entrance: Resurrectional Troparion and **"The noble Joseph"** (*Благообра́зный*); **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*), Kontakion of the Triodion; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*), of Pascha (**"Though Thou didst descend into the tomb"** (*А҆́ще и҆ во гро́бъ*)).

### Weekdays of the Week of the Myrrh-Bearers

All things according to the General Typikon of a Saint inside a Feast; only at the Aposticha of Vespers, and at the Sessional Hymns which follow the 1st Kathisma-reading of Matins, and at the Praises (for there are Stichera both at the Praises and afterwards at the Aposticha) there are certain Stichera or Troparia, taken from the Weekday Service of the Octoechos of the Tone of the week, differing according to the difference of the days of the week, except on Saturday, which has all things festal. Specifically: at Vespers at the conclusion: Troparion: **"The noble Joseph"** (*Благообра́зный*); **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*): to the Saint; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): to the Myrrh-bearers; and the Troparia **"The noble Joseph"** and **"When Thou didst descend"** (*Є҆гда̀ снизше́лъ є҆сѝ*) we say in the evening, alternating them daily.[^651]

#### At Compline

After the Trisagion, the one Kontakion of the Sunday: "Thus we say even unto the Sunday of All Saints" (*Triodion*).

#### At Matins

At **"The Lord is God"** (*Бо́гъ Госпо́дь*), Troparion **"The noble Joseph"** (*Благообра́зный*) once, and **"When Thou didst descend"** (*Є҆гда̀ снизше́лъ є҆сѝ*) once; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*): to the Saint; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): to the Myrrh-bearers. But when there are two Saints with

their own Troparia, then we deem that the Resurrectional one is to be omitted.

At the Sessional Hymns and at the Praises, there are two Troparia or two Weekday Stichera of the Octoechos of the Tone of the week, with the customary refrains; and before Psalm 50, **"Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ"** (*Воскресе́нїе Христо́во*) once; and at the conclusion of Matins, the Troparia as at **"The Lord is God"** (*Бо́гъ Госпо́дь*).

#### At the Hours

Since not a single Typikon makes this clear, on this account we deem that it is to be: at the 1st [Hour], Troparion: **"The noble Joseph"** (*Благообра́зный*); at the 3rd, Resurrectional, and **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*): to the Saint; at the 6th: to the Myrrh-bearers; at the 9th: again **"The noble Joseph"**, and **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*): to the Saint. Kontakia: at the 1st and 6th, to the Myrrh-bearers; at the 3rd and 9th, to the Saint. If two Saints occur, then at the 3rd after **"Glory"** there is the Troparion to the first, and at the 9th to the second Saint. Likewise also the Kontakia: at the 3rd to the first, and at the 9th to the second Saint.[^652]

#### At the Liturgy

Troparia: **"The noble Joseph"** (*Благообра́зный*), and Resurrectional, and to the Myrrh-bearers; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*), Kontakion to the Myrrh-bearers; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*), Kontakion of Pascha;[^653] or **"Glory... Both now..."**, Kontakion of the Myrrh-

bearers, without Pascha.[^654] If the Service of a Saint is also taken, or of some of the various Petitions, then after the three Troparia of the Sunday, we take also the Troparion to the Saint, or to the Petition; then Kontakion to the Myrrh-bearers, **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*), Kontakion to the Saint, or to the Petition; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*), Kontakion of Pascha; or **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*), Kontakion to the Saint, or to the Petition, and **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*), Kontakion to the Myrrh-bearers. Epistle and Gospel of the day; and all the rest of the Sunday of the Myrrh-bearers, or also of the Saint, or of the Petition, if there be one.

### Sunday of the Paralytic
**4th from Pascha. Tone 3.**

#### At Great Vespers

1. At **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Го́споди воззва́хъ*), Stichera for 10: 6 of the Octoechos (set down also in the Triodion), and 4 of the Triodion; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) of the Triodion; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*), Dogmatikon of the Tone.
2. At the Aposticha, one Resurrectional Sticheron, then the Stichera of Pascha; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) of the Paralytic; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): **"This is the day of Resurrection"** (*Воскресе́нїя де́нь*).
3. After **"Now lettest Thou"** (*Ны́нѣ ѿпꙋща́еши*): Resurrectional Troparion, Tone 3; **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*): Resurrectional Theotokion according to the Tone of the Troparion.
4. Dismissal of Pascha, without the Cross.

#### At Compline

After **"It is truly meet"** (*Досто́йно є҆́сть*), the one Kontakion of the Paralytic.

#### At Great Matins

1. At **"The Lord is God"** (*Бо́гъ Госпо́дь*): Resurrectional Troparion twice; **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*): Resurrectional Theotokion according to the Tone of the Troparion.
2. Two customary Kathismas, and Resurrectional Sessional Hymns, and the Evlogitaria (*Neporóchny*), and all the rest Resurrectional according to custom even unto the Canon, only the Resurrectional Gospel is the 4th, and **"Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ"** (*Воскресе́нїе Христо́во*) thrice.
3. 3 Canons for 14: of Pascha with the Heirmos for 6, and of the Theotokos in the Triodion for 2, and of the Paralytic for 6. Katavasia of Pascha.

After the 3rd Ode, Sessional Hymn of the Paralytic; after the 6th, Kontakion and Ikos of the Paralytic; at the 9th, we do not sing **"More honorable"** (*Честнѣ́йшꙋю*), but **"The Angel cried"** (*А҆́нгелъ вопїя́ше*) and **"Shine, shine"** (*Свѣти́ся*); after the 9th, **"Holy is the Lord our God"** (*Свѧ́тъ Госпо́дь Бо́гъ на́шъ*) thrice; and the Exaposteilarion of Pascha once; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) of the Paralytic; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*), the same.

4. At the Praises, 8 Resurrectional Stichera, according to custom; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) of the Paralytic; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): **"Most blessed art Thou"** (*Преблагослове́нна є҆сѝ*).
5. After the Great Doxology, one of the two Resurrectional Troparia.
6. Dismissal of Pascha; and after the Dismissal, the 4th Gospel Sticheron.

#### At the Hours

At all [the Hours]: Resurrectional Troparion, and Kontakion of the Paralytic.

#### At the Liturgy

After the Entrance: Resurrectional Troparion; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*), Kontakion of the Paralytic; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*), Kontakion of Pascha. All the rest of the Paralytic. Communion Hymn: **"Receive the Body of Christ"** (*Тѣ́ло Христо́во*) and **"Praise the Lord"** (*Хвали́те Го́спода*).

### Weekdays of the Week of the Paralytic

The Order of the Paralytic embraces only two weekdays, Monday and Tuesday, for

on Wednesday begins already the Feast of the Mid-Feast of Pentecost.

On these two weekdays we sing all things according to the Typikon of the weekdays of the Week of the Myrrh-bearers, here on page 483; only the Troparia are thus: at the conclusion of Vespers and Matins: Resurrectional Troparion; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*): to the Saint; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): Resurrectional Theotokion according to the Tone of the Troparion of the Saint.[^655] At **"The Lord is God"** (*Бо́гъ Госпо́дь*): Resurrectional Troparion twice; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*): to the Saint; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): Resurrectional Theotokion according to the Tone of the Troparion of the Saint.

At all the Hours: Resurrectional Troparion, then **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) at the 3rd and at the 9th also to the Saint; Kontakia: at the 1st and 6th, to the Paralytic; at the 3rd and 9th, to the Saint.

At the Liturgy: Resurrectional Troparion; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*), Kontakion of the Paralytic; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*), Kontakion of Pascha. Epistle and Gospel of the day; all the rest of the Sunday. When the Service of a Saint is also taken, or of a Petition, then: Resurrectional Troparion, and of the Saint, or of the Petition; then Kontakion of the Paralytic; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*): of the Saint, or of the Petition; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*), Kontakion of Pascha. All the rest as above, then to the Saint, or to the Petition; only Communion Hymn: **"Receive the Body of Christ"** (*Тѣ́ло Христо́во*), and to the Saint, or to the Petition.

*Note.* If two Saints occur, it is evident that at Vespers and Matins we select the Theotokion for the Troparia according to the Tone of the second, and at **"The Lord is God"** we sing all the Troparia once.

## On Wednesday of the 4th Week from Pascha
### The Mid-Feast of Pentecost

*Note.* This Feast is a Feast of the Lord, eight-day [an Octave], beginning with the Wednesday of the Paralytic, and ending with the Wednesday of the Samaritan Woman, on which it also has its Apodosis. On the Feast itself, and at the Apodosis, the Service of the Feast

alone is sung, while the Service of the Saint of the day from the Menaion is sung at Compline; but on the other days it is combined with the Menaion, except on the Sunday of the Samaritan Woman, for then it is combined with the Service of the Sunday. This Feast does not have an All-Night Vigil, nor a Polyeleos at Matins, but only the Great Doxology; while at Vespers there are both Entrance and Readings. We begin its Service from Tuesday evening, for the Church day begins from the evening of the preceding day.

#### Order of the Feast
##### [At Vespers]

1. The Kathisma of the day.
2. At **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Го́споди воззва́хъ*), Stichera of the Feast for 6.
3. After the Entrance, Prokeimenon of the day, and 3 Readings of the Feast.
4. Aposticha of the Feast, and Troparion of the Feast, and the Great Paschal Dismissal.

##### At Compline

We sing the Stichera and Canon of the Saint of the day, and after the Trisagion, the Kontakion of the Feast.

##### At Midnight Office

After the 1st Trisagion, Troparion of the Feast; and after the 2nd, Kontakion of the Feast; then **"Lord, have mercy"** (*Го́споди поми́луй*) 12, and the Dismissal. The prayers for the departed, **"Remember, O Lord"** (*Помянѝ Го́споди*), we do not say.

##### At Matins

1. At **"The Lord is God"** (*Бо́гъ Госпо́дь*): Troparion of the Feast twice; **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*), the same.
2. Two customary Kathismas, with the Sessional Hymns of the Feast; then **"Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ"** (*Воскресе́нїе Христо́во*) once and Psalm 50, and immediately the Canon.
3. Two Canons of the Feast, for 14: the 1st with the Heirmos for 8, and the 2nd for 6. Katavasia: the Heirmos of the 2nd Canon.

After the 3rd Ode, Sessional Hymn; after the 6th, Kontakion and Ikos; after the 9th, Exaposteilarion of the Feast; at the 9th, in place of **"More honorable"** (*Честнѣ́йшꙋю*), we sing the Heirmos of the Feast.

4. At the Praises, Stichera of the Feast for 4; and after the Great Doxology, Troparion of the Feast, and the Great Paschal Dismissal.

##### At the Hours

At all [the Hours], Troparion and Kontakion of the Feast.

##### At the Liturgy

All things of the Feast; only the first two Antiphons are Resurrectional, and the third is the customary one with the Resurrectional Refrain; likewise also at **"Come, let us worship"** (*Прїидѣ́те поклони́мся*). In place of **"The Angel cried"** (*А҆́нгелъ вопїя́ше*) and **"Shine, shine"** (*Свѣти́ся*), we sing the Heirmos of the Feast.

### Inside the Feast of the Mid-Feast of Pentecost
*(Except the Sunday of the Samaritan Woman and the Apodosis)*

We sing all things according to the Typikon of the Feast of Thomas Sunday, here on page 477; specifically, we say the Troparia thus:

1. At the conclusion of Vespers and Matins: Troparion of the Saint; **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*): of the Mid-Feast.
2. At Compline, the one Kontakion of the Mid-Feast.
3. At **"The Lord is God"** (*Бо́гъ Госпо́дь*): Troparion of the Mid-Feast twice; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*): to the Saint; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): of the Mid-Feast.
4. At all the Hours: Troparion of the Mid-Feast, then at the 3rd and 9th, **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*): to the Saint; Kontakia: at the 1st and 6th, of the Mid-Feast; at the 3rd and 9th, to the Saint.
5. At the Liturgy: Troparion; **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*), Kontakion of the Mid-Feast.

### Sunday of the Samaritan Woman
**5th from Pascha. Tone 4.**

*Note.* The Feast of the Mid-Feast detains the Service of the Samaritan Woman on Monday, Tuesday, and Wednesday of this week; on the remaining three days of it, that is, after the Apodosis of the Feast, the Service of the Samaritan Woman is resumed again; and on this Sunday we sing the Service of the Samaritan Woman with the Service of the Mid-Feast.

#### Order of Services
##### At Great Vespers

1. At **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Го́споди воззва́хъ*), 10 Stichera: 4 Resurrectional, and 3 of the Mid-Feast, and 3 of the Samaritan Woman; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) of the Samaritan Woman; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*), the 1st Theotokion of the Tone (Dogmatikon).
2. At the Aposticha: one Resurrectional Sticheron, then the Stichera of Pascha with their refrains, only the Doxastikon (*Naslávnyk*) is of the Samaritan Woman.
3. After **"Now lettest Thou"** (*Ны́нѣ ѿпꙋща́еши*): Resurrectional Troparion; **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*): of the Mid-Feast.
4. Dismissal of Pascha, without the Cross.

##### At Compline

After **"It is truly meet"** (*Досто́йно є҆́сть*): Kontakion of the Samaritan Woman; **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*), Kontakion of the Mid-Feast.

##### At Great Matins

1. At **"The Lord is God"** (*Бо́гъ Госпо́дь*): Resurrectional Troparion twice; **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*): of the Mid-Feast.
2. The Kathismas of the day, and all the rest even unto the Canon, is Resurrectional according to custom, only the Resurrectional Gospel is the 6th, and **"Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ"** (*Воскресе́нїе Христо́во*) thrice.

3. 4 Canons for 14: Resurrectional with the Heirmos for 4, and of the Theotokos in the Triodion for 2, and of the Mid-Feast for 4, and of the Samaritan Woman for 4. Katavasia of Pascha.

After the 3rd Ode, Kontakion and Ikos of the Mid-Feast, and Sessional Hymn of the Samaritan Woman; **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*): of the Mid-Feast. After the 6th Ode, Kontakion and Ikos of the Samaritan Woman. At the 9th Ode, in place of **"More honorable"** (*Честнѣ́йшꙋю*): **"The Angel cried"** (*А҆́нгелъ вопїя́ше*) and **"Shine, shine"** (*Свѣти́ся*); after the 9th Ode, **"Holy is the Lord our God"** (*Свѧ́тъ Госпо́дь Бо́гъ на́шъ*) thrice, and the Exaposteilarion of Pascha once; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) of the Samaritan Woman; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): of the Mid-Feast.

4. At the Praises, 8 Stichera: 5 Resurrectional, and 3 of the Samaritan Woman, with their two refrains; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) of the Samaritan Woman; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): **"Most blessed art Thou"** (*Преблагослове́нна є҆сѝ*).
5. After the Great Doxology, one of the two customary Resurrectional Troparia.
6. Paschal Dismissal, and **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*), the 6th Gospel Sticheron.

#### At the Hours

At all [the Hours]: Resurrectional Troparion; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*): of the Mid-Feast; Kontakia: at the 1st and 6th, of the Mid-Feast; at the 3rd and 9th, of the Samaritan Woman.

#### At the Liturgy

1. Antiphons, as on all Sundays.
2. After the Entrance: Resurrectional Troparion and of the Mid-Feast; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*), Kontakion of the Samaritan Woman; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): of the Mid-Feast. All the rest only of the Sunday. Communion Hymn: **"Receive the Body of Christ"** (*Тѣ́ло Христо́во*) and **"Praise the Lord"** (*Хвали́те Го́спода*).

### Monday and Tuesday Inside the Mid-Feast

We sing the Service of the Mid-Feast and of the Saint from the Menaion, according to the General Typikon of the weekdays of Thomas Week, here on page 477; specifically, we say the Troparia according to

the General Typikon inside a Feast, as we set down previously on page 488.

### Wednesday of the Apodosis of the Mid-Feast

We sing the entire Service only of the Feast, as on the day of the Feast itself, here on page 488, except for the Entrance and Readings at Vespers, and the Epistle and Gospel at the Liturgy, which are of the day; while the Saint of the Menaion we sing at Compline.

### Thursday, Friday, and Saturday of the Samaritan Woman

We sing all things according to the Typikon of the weekdays of the Paralytic, here on page 486.

### Sunday of the Blind Man
**6th from Pascha. Tone 5.**

All things according to the Typikon of the Sunday of the Paralytic, here on page 485; only at Great Matins the Resurrectional Gospel is the 8th, and the Katavasia is not of Pascha, but of the Ascension.

### Weekdays of the Week of the Blind Man

The Order of the Blind Man reaches only unto three weekdays: Monday, Tuesday, and Wednesday, on which it is sung together with the Apodosis of Pascha and the Forefeast of the Ascension.

#### Monday and Tuesday of the Blind Man

All things according to the Typikon of the weekdays of the Paralytic, here on page 486.

### Wednesday of the Apodosis of Pascha

#### [At Vespers]

1. At **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Го́споди воззва́хъ*), Stichera of the Blind Man for 6; **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*), of the same.
2. At the Aposticha, one Resurrectional Sticheron, then the Stichera of Pascha, and **"Christ is risen"** (*Христо́съ воскре́се*) once.
3. After **"Now lettest Thou"** (*Ны́нѣ ѿпꙋща́еши*): Resurrectional Troparion of the Sunday; **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*): Resurrectional Theotokion according to its Tone.
4. Paschal Dismissal of mid-rank, without the Cross, with commemoration of the Saint of the day.[^656]

#### At Compline

After **"It is truly meet"** (*Досто́йно є҆́сть*), the one Kontakion of the Blind Man.

#### At Midnight Office

After the 1st Trisagion, in place of **"Behold, the Bridegroom"** (*Сѐ Жени́хъ*), we say the Resurrectional Troparion **"The Word co-eternal"** (*Собезнача́льное Сло́во*); **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*): its Theotokion; after the 2nd Trisagion, in place of **"Remember"**, we say the Kontakion of Pascha; then **"Lord, have mercy"** (*Го́споди поми́луй*) 12, and the Dismissal; the prayers **"Remember, O Lord"** (*Помянѝ Го́споди*) we do not say.

#### At Matins

1. At **"The Lord is God"** (*Бо́гъ Госпо́дь*): Resurrectional Troparion twice; **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*): its Theotokion.
2. After the 1st Kathisma, Resurrectional Sessional Hymn; after the 2nd, Sessional Hymn of the Blind Man; and immediately **"Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ"** (*Воскресе́нїе Христо́во*) once, and Psalm 50, and the Canon.
3. 3 Canons for 14: of Pascha with the Heirmos for 6, and of the Blind Man for 4, and of the Forefeast of the Ascension for 4. Katavasia of the Ascension.

After the 3rd Ode, Kontakion and Ikos of the Blind Man, and his Sessional Hymn; **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*): of the Forefeast; after the 6th Ode, Kontakion and Ikos of Pascha; at the 9th Ode, in place of **"More honorable"** (*Честнѣ́йшꙋю*), we sing **"The Angel cried"** (*А҆́нгелъ вопїя́ше*) and **"Shine, shine"** (*Свѣти́ся*); after the 9th Ode, Exaposteilarion of Pascha twice; **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*): of the Blind Man.

At the Praises, Stichera for 8: of the Blind Man for 4, then the Stichera of Pascha with their refrains; **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*): **"This is the day of Resurrection"** (*Воскресе́нїя де́нь*), and **"Christ is risen"** (*Христо́съ воскре́се*) once.

After the Great Doxology: Troparion **"The Word co-eternal"** (*Собезнача́льное Сло́во*); **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*): its Theotokion; and after the Ektenias, the Great Paschal Dismissal, without the Cross, with commemoration of the Saint of the day.

#### At the Hours

At all [the Hours], Troparion **"The Word co-eternal"** (*Собезнача́льное Сло́во*), and Kontakion of the Blind Man.[^657]

#### At the Liturgy

After the Entrance: Troparion **"The Word co-eternal"** (*Собезнача́льное Сло́во*); **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*), Kontakion of the Blind Man; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*), Kontakion of Pascha. Epistle and Gospel of the day; all the rest as on the day of Pascha itself. Dismissal with the Cross.

## On Thursday of the 6th Week from Pascha
### The Ascension of the Lord

This Feast extends over 9 days; for it reaches even unto Friday after the Sunday of the Holy Fathers, on which it also has its Apodosis.

The Feast, the Afterfeast, and the Apodosis of the Feast we celebrate according to the General Rule of a Feast of the Lord; only at the Liturgy our Sluzhebnyks, in place of **"We have seen"** (*Ви́дѣхомъ*), and in place of **"Let our mouths be filled"** (*Да и҆спо́лнятся*), appoint the Verse: **"Be Thou exalted above the heavens, O God, and Thy glory over all the earth"** (*Вознеси́ся на небеса̀, Бо́же, и҆ по все́й землѝ сла́ва Твоя̀*), which we say even unto the Apodosis; and on the following Sunday of the Holy Fathers, the Service of the Ascension has a proper Typikon, for it is combined both with the Resurrectional Service and with that of the Holy Fathers, as follows.

### Sunday of the Holy Fathers of the 1st Nicene Council
**7th from Pascha. Tone 6.**

#### At Great Vespers

1. At **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Го́споди воззва́хъ*), 10 Stichera: 3 Resurrectional, and 3 of the Ascension, and 4 of the Fathers; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) of the Fathers; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*), the 1st Theotokion of the Tone.
2. After **"The Lord is King"** (*Госпо́дь воцари́ся*), 3 Readings of the Fathers.
3. Aposticha Resurrectional; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) of the Fathers; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): of the Feast.
4. After **"Now lettest Thou"** (*Ны́нѣ ѿпꙋща́еши*): Resurrectional Troparion; **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*): of the Feast.

#### At Compline

After **"It is truly meet"** (*Досто́йно є҆́сть*): Kontakion to the Fathers; **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*), Kontakion of the Feast.

#### At Great Matins

1. At **"The Lord is God"** (*Бо́гъ Госпо́дь*): Resurrectional Troparion twice; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) to the Fathers; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): of the Feast.

2. Customary Kathismas, and all the rest even unto the Canon, is Resurrectional according to custom; only the Resurrectional Gospel is the 10th.
3. 3 Canons for 14: Resurrectional with the Heirmos for 4, and of the Ascension for 4, and of the Fathers for 6. Katavasia of the Descent of the Holy Spirit (*Soshéstviye*).

After the 3rd Ode, Kontakion and Ikos of the Ascension, and Sessional Hymn of the Fathers; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) of the Fathers; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): of the Ascension; after the 6th Ode, Kontakion and Ikos of the Fathers; at the 9th Ode, **"More honorable"** (*Честнѣ́йшꙋю*); and after **"Holy is the Lord our God"** (*Свѧ́тъ Госпо́дь Бо́гъ на́шъ*), Resurrectional Exaposteilarion; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) of the Fathers; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): of the Feast.

4. At the Praises, Stichera for 8: 4 Resurrectional, and 3 of the Fathers for 4; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) of the Fathers; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): **"Most blessed art Thou"** (*Преблагослове́нна є҆сѝ*).
5. After the Great Doxology, one of the two customary Resurrectional Troparia, and after the Dismissal, **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*), the 10th Gospel Sticheron.

#### At the Hours

At all [the Hours]: Resurrectional Troparion, then **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) at the 1st and 6th also of the Feast, at the 3rd and 9th to the Fathers. Kontakia: at the 1st and 6th of the Feast, at the 3rd and 9th to the Fathers.

#### At the Liturgy

1. After the Entrance: Troparion Resurrectional, and of the Feast, and to the Fathers; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*), Kontakion of the Fathers; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): of the Feast.
2. All the rest only to the Fathers; only Communion Hymn: **"Praise the Lord"** (*Хвали́те Го́спода*) and to the Fathers (**"Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous"** (*Ра́дꙋйтеся пра́веднїи*)).

### On Sunday Evening of the Holy Fathers

The Apodosis of the Service of the Holy Fathers takes place according to the following Typikon:

1. At **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Го́споди воззва́хъ*), 6 Stichera: 3 of the Fathers, and 3 of the Saint from the Menaion; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) of the Fathers; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): of the Feast.
2. At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Fathers; only **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): of the Feast.
3. After **"Now lettest Thou"** (*Ны́нѣ ѿпꙋща́еши*): Troparion of the Fathers; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*): of the Saint; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): of the Feast. And thus the Order of the Holy Fathers has its Apodosis.

### On Friday After the Sunday of the Holy Fathers
#### Apodosis of the Ascension

On Thursday evening and Friday morning, the Service of the Ascension is sung according to the General Rule of the Apodosis of a Feast. The Service of the Saint we sing at Compline.

### Soul Saturday Before the Descent [of the Holy Spirit]

All things according to the Typikon of Meatfare Saturday, here on page 363.

## Sunday of Pentecost

1. The Service of this Feast is celebrated over seven days, for it has its Apodosis on the coming Saturday. It has this proper feature, that on Sunday evening at Vespers toward Monday the Kneeling Prayers are read; and on the morrow, that is, on Monday, the entire Service of the Feast is sung without the Menaion, and at the Liturgy also the Service of the Holy Trinity, if desired,[^658] as we indicate immediately.

All the rest, from the Feast even unto its Apodosis, is performed according to the General Rule of a Feast and Inside a Feast of the Lord. At the Liturgy, **"As many of you as have been baptized into Christ"** (*Є҆ли́цы*), only on the Feast.

2. Here they have the custom on the day of Pentecost itself at the beginning of the Liturgy after **"Blessed is the Kingdom"** (*Благослове́нно Ца́рство*) (better before **"Blessed is the Kingdom"**), and afterwards at the beginning of Vespers after **"Blessed is our God"** (*Благослове́нъ Бо́гъ на́шъ*), to sing with kneeling **"O Heavenly King"** (*Царю̀ небе́сный*).

3. We sing Vespers on the day of the Feast itself immediately after the conclusion of the Liturgy, for the sake of the Kneeling Prayers, so that all the people may have a part in them.[^659]

4. The Service of a Saint occurring on Sunday and Monday, we sing on another day at Compline, at the discretion of the Superior (*Tserkvenachálnyk*).

5. Throughout this entire week the Canons of the Most Holy Theotokos are omitted at Compline, and we are dispensed to meat-eating (*Miasoyástiye*).

### Monday of the Holy Spirit
#### On Sunday Evening

*Note.* The Kneeling Prayers take place in three places at Vespers: after the Great Prokeimenon; after **"Let us all say"** (*Рцѣ́мъ всѝ*); and after **"Vouchsafe, O Lord"** (*Сподо́би, Го́споди*). At each of these three Stations of Prayers, the Little Ektenia is said with each time its proper Exclamation, yet thus: **"Again and again, having bended our knees, let us pray to the Lord"** (*Па́ки и҆ па́ки прекло́ньше колѣ́нѣ Го́споду помоли́мся*); and the Choir: **"Lord, have mercy"** (*Го́споди поми́луй*) thrice; then the Priest [reads] one Station of the Prayers with kneeling; and at the end of the Station the Deacon, or the Priest, follows with the remainder of the Little Ektenia, that is: **"Help us, save us"**, **"Remembering our most holy, most pure"**; and the Exclamation. Thus it takes place at each Station.

---

## Monday of the Holy Spirit
### Typikon
#### At Vespers

1. Immediately after the conclusion of the Liturgy, the Priest, and the Deacons if there are [any], vested in all the sacred vestments, standing by the Holy Table, begin Vespers. Priest: **"Blessed is our God"** (*Благослове́нъ Бо́гъ на́шъ*), and after **"Amen"**, all having bended their knees, we sing **"O Heavenly King"** (*Царю̀ небе́сный*), then we say the Trisagion and the rest with the **"Our Father"** (*О҆́тче на́шъ*), and **"Come, let us worship"** (*Прїидѣ́те поклони́мся*) thrice, according to custom.
2. The Great Ektenia: **"In peace let us pray to the Lord"** (*Ми́ромъ Го́споду помоли́мся*), with the addition of proper petitions.[^660]
3. At **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Го́споди воззва́хъ*), Stichera of the Feast on 6; **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*): **"O Heavenly King"** (*Царю̀ небе́сный*).
4. After the Entrance with the Censer, the Great Prokeimenon of the Feast.
5. The Deacon, or the Priest, after the chanting of the Prokeimenon, standing in the sanctuary, begins the Little Ektenia thus: **"Again and again, having bended our knees, let us pray to the Lord"** (*Па́ки и҆ па́ки прекло́ньше колѣ́нѣ, Го́споду помоли́мся*); Choir: **"Lord, have mercy"** (*Го́споди поми́луй*) thrice; and all having bended their knees, the Priest, kneeling outside the sanctuary before the Royal Doors,[^661] reads in the hearing of all the first and second Prayer of Kneeling; and at the conclusion thereof, all having stood up, the Deacon takes up the remainder of the Little Ektenia, saying: **"Help us, save us"** (*Застꙋпѝ, спасѝ*); Choir: **"Lord, have mercy"** (*Го́споди поми́луй*) once. Deacon:

**"Remembering our most holy, most pure..."** (*Пресвяту́ю, пречи́стую*); the Priest [says] the proper Exclamation. We do likewise both after **"Let us all say"** (*Рцѣ́мъ всѝ*), and after **"Vouchsafe, O Lord"** (*Сподо́би, Го́споди*).[^662]
6. At the Aposticha, [Stichera] of the Feast; and after **"Now lettest Thou"** (*Ны́нѣ ѿпꙋща́еши*), the Troparion of the Feast; and the Great Dismissal proper to this Vespers.

#### At Compline

The Canon to the Theotokos is omitted, and the Canon of the Holy Spirit which is in the Triodion is sung, with the refrain: **"Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee"** (*Сла́ва тебѣ̀, Бо́же на́шъ, сла́ва тебѣ̀*); and after the 9th Ode, in place of **"It is truly meet"** (*Досто́йно є҆́сть*), the Heirmos of the same Ode; and after the Trisagion, the Kontakion of the Feast.

#### At the Midnight Office

After the 1st Trisagion, the Troparion, and after the 2nd, the Kontakion of the Feast, without the prayer **"Remember, O Lord"** (*Помянѝ*).[^663]

#### At Matins

1. At **"God is the Lord"** (*Бо́гъ Госпо́дь*): Troparion of the Feast thrice.
2. After the two customary Kathismata, Sessional Hymns of the Feast; and immediately Psalm 50 and the Canon.

3. Canons of the Feast, two, on 14: the first with the Heirmos for 8, the second with the Heirmos for 6. Katavasia of the Feast.
After the 3rd Ode, Sessional Hymn of the Feast; after the 6th Ode, Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast; at the 9th Ode, we do not sing **"More honorable"** (*Честнѣ́йшꙋю*); after the 9th Ode, Exapostilarion of the Feast; **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*), the other.
4. At the Praises, Stichera of the Feast on 4; **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*): of the Feast.
5. After the Great Doxology, Troparion of the Feast; and after the Ektenias, the Great Dismissal of the Feast: **"He Who in the form of fiery tongues..."** (*Въ ви́дѣ ѻ҆́гненныхъ я҆зы́къ*).

#### At the Hours

At the Hours, Troparion and Kontakion of the Feast.

#### At the Liturgy

1. The first two Antiphons of the Feast, the third weekday with the Refrain of the Feast.
2. The Entrance Hymn (*Vkhódnoye*) proper; Troparion, **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*), Kontakion of the Feast, and the Trisagion.
3. Prokeimenon and Alleluia proper; Epistle and Gospel of the day; Communion Hymn of the Feast.

*Note.* If the Service to the Holy Trinity is joined, then after the Entrance, as we deem (for the Typikon has nothing [concerning this]), the entire Service is to be taken first to the Holy Trinity, then to Monday. Namely the Troparia thus: Troparion of the Holy Trinity, then of the Descent; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*), Kontakion of the Holy Trinity; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*), of the Descent; likewise all the rest first to the Holy Trinity, then to Monday.

### Apodosis of the Feast of Pentecost

Takes place on Saturday, according to the General Typikon of the Apodosis of a Feast of the Lord.

### Sunday of All Saints: 1st After the Descent
**Tone 8.**
We do not celebrate a Vigil.

#### At Great Vespers

1. **"Blessed is the man"** (*Блаже́нъ мꙋ́жъ*), according to custom.
2. At **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Го́споди воззва́хъ*), 10 Stichera: 4 Resurrectional, and 6 of the Saints; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) of the Saints; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*), the 1st Theotokion of the Tone (Tone 8, Dogmatikon).
3. After the Great Prokeimenon, Readings to the Saints, 3.
4. At the Aposticha, Resurrectional [Stichera]; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) of the Saints; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*), their Theotokion.
5. After **"Now lettest Thou"** (*Ны́нѣ ѿпꙋща́еши*): Resurrectional Troparion; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) of the Saints; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*), Resurrectional Theotokion according to the Tone of the Saints.

#### At Compline

We begin to read the Canon to the Theotokos. After **"It is truly meet"** (*Досто́йно є҆́сть*), the Kontakion of All Saints.

#### At the Midnight Office

All things according to custom, that is, the Canon of the Holy Trinity of the Tone of the week (Tone 8).

#### At Great Matins

1. At **"God is the Lord"** (*Бо́гъ Госпо́дь*): Resurrectional Troparion twice; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) of the Saints; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*), Resurrectional Theotokion according to the Tone of the Saints.
2. Kathismata of the cycle, and all the rest even unto the Resurrectional Canon according to custom. (Gospel of the cycle, 1st).
3. 4 Canons on 14: Resurrectional on 4, and Cross-Resurrectional on 2, and to the Theotokos on 2, and of the Saints on 6. Katavasia of the cycle (**"I shall open my mouth"** (*Ѿве́рзꙋ оу҆ста̀ моя́*)).

After the 3rd Ode, Sessional Hymn of the Saints; after the 6th Ode, Kontakion and Ikos of the Saints; at the 9th Ode, **"More honorable"** (*Честнѣ́йшꙋю*); after the 9th Ode: **"Holy is the Lord our God"** (*Свя́тъ Госпо́дь Бо́гъ на́шъ*) thrice, and the Resurrectional Exapostilarion of the cycle (1st); **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) of the Saints; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*), their Theotokion.
4. At the Praises, 8 Stichera: 5 Resurrectional from the Octoechos, and 3 of the Saints, with their two refrains; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*), 1st Gospel Sticheron; **"Both now"** (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): **"Most blessed art thou"** (*Преблагослове́нна е҆сѝ*).
5. After the Great Doxology, one of the two customary Resurrectional Troparia, and all the rest customary, as on all Sundays.

#### At the Hours

At all [the Hours]: Resurrectional Troparion; **"Glory"** (*Сла́ва*) of the Saints; but Kontakion only of the Saints.

#### At the Liturgy

1. After the Entrance: Troparion Resurrectional and of the Saints; **"Glory... Both now..."** (*Сла́ва, и҆ ны́нѣ*), Kontakion of the Saints.
2. Prokeimenon Resurrectional of the Tone, and of the Saints; Alleluia of the Saints; Epistle and Gospel of the Saints; Communion Hymn: **"Praise the Lord"** (*Хвали́те Го́спода*), and of the Saints (**"Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous"** (*Ра́дꙋйтеся*)).

### Monday of the 2nd Week After the Descent
#### Beginning of the Fast of the Holy Preeminent Apostles Peter and Paul

This Fast begins with Monday after the Sunday of All Saints, and ends with the Eve of the Feast of the Holy Apostles. Regarding Divine Services, on this first Monday, if a celebrated Saint did not occur, Alleluia was formerly sung, and the entire Church Rule

fasting, as in the fast-days of the Holy Forty Days; yet today we restrict the Alleluia to the Great Forty Days, and here we sing **"God is the Lord"** (*Бо́гъ Госпо́дь*).
Regarding the Fast: the Synod of Lviv restricts this Fast to Mondays, Wednesdays, and Fridays of each of its weeks, and dispenses those same [days] to dairy products; the other four days it dispenses to meat, yet under the condition of reciting Psalm 50 for the Clergy, and five **"Our Father"** and five **"O Theotokos and Virgin"** for the laity, before the midday meal and before the evening meal.[^664]

### On Thursday After the Sunday of All Saints
#### Feast of the Most Holy Eucharist
**Transferred to the 2nd Sunday After the Descent.**

This Feast is celebrated with an Octave (*osmodnéviye*), from the Thursday after the Sunday of All Saints unto the following Thursday, on which it also has its Apodosis. But since according to our custom it is transferred from Thursday to the following Sunday, on this account the Synod of Lviv commands that its Service be sung on Sunday, with a Vigil, unalterably as it is set down in the Triodion on the Thursday of the Feast itself, according to the General Typikon of a First-Class Feast of the Lord, without the Resurrectional [hymns]; and on the days preceding and following this Sunday to sing it according to the General Typikon Inside a Feast of the Lord. Moreover, the Synod commands that on the Sunday itself there take place according to custom the Procession with the Holy Mysteries after the Prayer behind the Ambo, that is, out of the church to four Altars erected

outside the church, equally distant from one another. Where this would be impossible, it suffices to begin the Procession from the Altar which is inside the church, and having circled the church thrice, to conclude this rite at the very same Altar in the church with the blessing with the Holy Mysteries. Regarding the four Gospel Readings, commonly chanted one at each of the four Altars, the Synod commands that those be chosen which discourse concerning the Most Holy Mysteries, according to the Typikon prescribed for this Feast.[^665]
After these things it is necessary to present two Typika: 1. How the Exposition of the Holy Mysteries takes place on this Feast; 2. How the Procession with the Holy Mysteries takes place.

### Typikon I
#### Concerning the Exposition of the Holy Mysteries at the Liturgy

The Priest, on one of the days preceding the Feast, consecrates at the Liturgy two Lambs; and from the one he communicates, but the other, placing it in the Pyx (*Kivot*), he preserves until the day of the Feast. All these things he performs according to the Typikon written for Great Thursday, here on page 435, in the Note. When the day of the Feast itself arrives, after the conclusion of the Proskomide, immediately before the beginning of the Divine Liturgy, the Deacons (or the Priest himself) having censed all things according to custom, there takes place the Exposition

of the Holy Mysteries, thus: The ranking Deacon (the one at the right hand of the Priest), having laid aside the Gospel Book to the right side of the Holy Table, and having opened the Tabernacle (*Skíniya*), and having bowed profoundly toward the Holy Mysteries, brings forth the Pyx (*Kivot*) from the Tabernacle, all having bended their knees, and places it upon the folded Iliton; and if there is an Ostensorium (*Daroukazálnitsya* / Monstrance), having opened the Pyx, and having taken the Lamb out of it, not touching it with the fingers, but with the forceps in which it is held, places the Lamb into the receptacle (*rovéts*) of the Ostensorium, and having closed it well, and having placed the Pyx back into the Tabernacle, and having bowed toward it, and having closed the Tabernacle, sets the Ostensorium upon the Iliton, turned with its front side toward the people, and steps aside a little to the right. And the Priest, having placed incense into the Censer, and having blessed it, saying: **"Blessed is our God"** (*Благослове́нъ Бо́гъ на́шъ*), censes the Holy Mysteries with both hands thrice, bowing his head after each censing; and having thus taken upon himself the humeral veil (*Omofórets*) with the assistance of the second Deacon, and having approached the Holy Table, and having bowed profoundly, takes, with the assistance of the first Deacon, with both hands through the humeral veil the Ostensorium, and having turned its back side toward himself, turns by his right toward the people, and sings slowly and devoutly the beginning of the first stanza: **"O Saving Victim"** (*Ѡ҆ спаси́тельная Же́ртво*); all the people following with the remainder of that stanza. And again the Priest [sings] the beginning of the second stanza: **"To the One God in Trinity"** (*Є҆ди́номꙋ въ Тро́йци Бо́гꙋ*); while all follow with the rest, he blesses the people with Them crosswise, completing a circle from the south to the north side; and having thus turned by the north side toward the Holy Table, he sets the Ostensorium upon the Iliton, turning it, with the Deacon assisting, with its front side toward the people; then having stepped aside a little to the left, he descends before the step of the Holy Table in such a way that his back is not turned toward the Holy Mysteries, bends his knees, and having laid aside the humeral veil, and having censed again

the Holy Mysteries thrice, as before, waits there kneeling until the Deacon, having approached the middle of the Holy Table, and having bowed profoundly, sets the Holy Mysteries atop the Tabernacle. This having been done, the Priest, having ascended the steps, begins the Liturgy from **"Blessed is our God"** (*Благослове́нъ Бо́гъ на́шъ*), **"O Heavenly King"** (*Царю̀ небе́сный*), and the rest. During the Liturgy, the Sacred Ministers never turn their backs toward the Holy Mysteries, as our Sluzhebniks command, saying: *"When the Priest, according to the order of the rites, is to turn his face to the people, he ought not to turn from the middle of the sanctuary, lest he turn his back to the Body of Christ, but having made a profound bow, and having stepped somewhat toward the left side, from this side he turns to the people, and having turned back to the sanctuary, he makes a profound bow to the Body of Christ; and where the Priest is to make a full circle, as at 'Always, now and ever' (*Всегда̀, ны́нѣ и҆ при́снѡ*), when he turns with the Holy Mysteries, at that time he does not complete a full circle in turning, lest he turn his back to the Body of Christ."*[^666] We add also this: that when the Priest, or the Deacon, goes forth before the Iconostasis for the censing, he is not to cense from the middle, nor turn his back toward the Holy Mysteries, but to stand somewhat to the side, the Priest indeed from the side of the Theotokos, and the Deacons, one from the Savior's side, the other from the Theotokos' side.

*Note.*
1. If there is no Ostensorium, then the Holy Mysteries are exposed with the Pyx.
2. After the cleansing of the Chalice, the Priest does not place the Gospel Book upon the Iliton, since on the Holy Table it will be necessary after the Prayer behind the Ambo to set the Holy Mysteries.
3. If there are no Deacons, the Priest himself performs all these things.

### Typikon II
#### Concerning the Procession with the Holy Mysteries After the Prayer Behind the Ambo

After the conclusion of the Prayer behind the Ambo, the Sacred Ministers having stood before the step of the Holy Table, the first Deacon, having ascended the step, and having bowed profoundly, takes down the Holy Mysteries from atop the Tabernacle, and having set Them upon the folded Iliton, steps aside a little to his own side. And all having bended their knees, the Priest, having censed the Holy Mysteries thrice, and having taken upon himself the humeral veil, ascends the step, and having taken hold of the Holy Mysteries with both hands, with the Deacon assisting, and having turned to the people, as at the beginning of the Liturgy, and having intoned the beginning of the Troparion: **"Bread Supernatural"** (*Хлѣ́бъ преесте́ственный*), or some other devout hymn, goes forth by the Holy Doors into the church, two Deacons preceding with Censers; and having entered beneath the Canopy (*Baldakhin*), advances unto the doors of the church; and with the bells chiming, the Procession proceeds from the church unto the first Altar, the Choirs and the people following singing the Troparion, or something else. And the order of the Procession is as follows: First of all goes the Cross-bearer between two Taper-bearers, then the Banner-bearers and Icon-bearers, then four Maidens carrying icons, after them the Church Brotherhood, two by two, with candles, the Choirs, and all the Clergy two by two with candles, according to the order of their dignity, the Priests nearest in Phelonia, the others in Epitrakhelia, and last of all the celebrant Priest with the Holy Mysteries, two Deacons preceding and unceasingly censing from the side, and Taper-bearers surrounding the Canopy. When

the Procession has arrived at the first Altar, and has drawn up beside it, and the entire Clergy having divided into two wings by the Altar, and the Canopy having stopped facing the Altar, the Priest with the Holy Mysteries ascends to the Altar, and with the Deacon assisting, sets the Holy Mysteries upon the folded Iliton; then having bended his knees, he censes Them thrice, and having risen steps aside a little to the left, and turns to the people. Then the Gospel-bearers having approached the Priest, and he having opened the place of the 1st Reading, the first Deacon exclaims: **"And that we may be accounted worthy..."** (*И҆ ѡ҆ є҆́же сподо́битися на́мъ*), and the Choir having answered: **"Lord, have mercy"** (*Го́споди поми́луй*) thrice, and the Priest having intoned: **"Peace be unto all"** (*Ми́ръ всѣ̑мъ*), the second Deacon says: **"Wisdom. Let us attend. Let us hear..."** (*Премꙋ́дрость про́сти, оу҆слы́шимъ*), the Priest: **"The Reading from the Holy Gospel according to [Name]"**; and the first Deacon having prompted: **"Let us attend"** (*Во́ньмемъ*), the Priest reads the 1st Gospel (Matthew, Pericope 108, [ch. 26:] 17–30); and after the reading, having kissed the beginning thereof, and having bended his knees before the Holy Mysteries, he sings (if such is the custom anywhere) with the other Sacred Ministers the first clause of the Penitential Supplication (*Péniye Oumilítelnoye*): **"Holy God"** (*Святы́й Бо́же*) thrice, and all the people the same thrice. Then having censed the Holy Mysteries again thrice, and having risen, and having made a profound bow (*metania*), he takes Them with both hands, and having turned by his right toward the people, blesses them crosswise once (a Bishop thrice), saying in a gentle voice: **"O God, save Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance"** (*Спасѝ, Бо́же, лю́ди твоя̀, и҆ благословѝ достоя́нїе твоѐ*); and thus, the Festal Troparion or another hymn being sung again, and the Priest having entered beneath the Canopy, the Procession continues to the second Altar, and from this to the third, and from this to the fourth; and at each there take place unalterably all things as at the first Altar, varying only the Clauses of the Penitential Supplication, and the Gospel Readings, of which the 2nd is from Mark, Pericope 64, [ch. 14:] 12–26; the 3rd from Luke, Pericope 108, [ch. 22:] 7–22; the 4th from John, Pericope 23, [ch. 6:] 48–58. If there are multiple Priests, they read the Gospels in

turn. After these things the Procession returns into the church, and the Priest having set the Holy Mysteries upon the Altar, and having bended his knees, and having censed Them thrice, rises, and having taken the Holy Mysteries, and having turned to the people, sings slowly and devoutly the verse of the Ambrosian Hymn (*Te Deum*): **"Save Thy people, O Lord"** (*Спасѝ лю́ди твоя̀, Го́споди*), thrice, the people responding with the same the first and second time; and when the Priest sings it the third time, he concludes it with the words: **"and bless Thine inheritance"** (*и҆ благословѝ достоя́нїе твоѐ*), with the blessing of the people once; then while the people sing the remaining verses of the same Hymn, the Priest, having turned by the north side toward the Altar, and having set upon it the Holy Mysteries, descends before the steps, and having laid aside the humeral veil, bends his knees, and censes Them thrice. And the Deacon on the right, having approached the Holy Mysteries, and having bowed profoundly, brings forth the Pyx from the Tabernacle, and having taken the Lamb from the Ostensorium, places It with the forceps into the Pyx, and the Pyx into the Tabernacle, and having bowed again, closes the Tabernacle, and places the Gospel Book upon the Iliton. If there is no Deacon, the Priest himself performs all these things. Then, all having stood up, Choir: **"Blessed be the name of the Lord"** (*Бꙋ́ди и҆́мя Госпо́дне*), and the rest of the Liturgy according to custom.

*Note.*
1. In case it is not possible to have four Altars, see what the Synod says here on page 504.
2. The Lamb placed into the Pyx the Priest, celebrating the Liturgy on the morrow, consumes together with the other which he consecrates at that Liturgy, first the newly consecrated, then the previously consecrated.
3. On Sunday evening at Vespers we sing the Great Prokeimenon of the Feast, set down in the Triodion on the preceding Thursday evening.

### On Thursday of the 3rd Week After the Descent
#### Apodosis of the Feast of the Eucharist

Takes place according to the General Rule of the Apodosis of a Feast of the Lord.

### On Friday After the Apodosis of the Eucharist
#### Feast of the Compassion of the Most Holy Theotokos

We celebrate this Feast as a single day, without a Vigil, but only with Polyeleos.
At Great Vespers: **"Blessed is the man"** (*Блаже́нъ мꙋ́жъ*). At **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Го́споди воззва́хъ*), Stichera on 6; and after the Prokeimenon of the day, Readings of the Feast, 3; and the rest of the Service of the Feast alone, according to the Typikon of the Service of July 2nd, here on page 345. The Litiya which is appointed in the Triodion we do not use today, apart from an All-Night Vigil.
At Great Matins: one Canon, with the Heirmos on 12.
At the Liturgy: at the 3rd Antiphon, and at **"Come, let us worship"** (*Прїидѣ́те поклони́мся*), if thou wilt, say the Refrain: **"By the prayers of the Theotokos, [save us who sing unto Thee]"** (*Моли́твами Богоро́дицы пою́щыя тѝ*), as the Pochaiv Triodion has it.

---

### Notes

[^472]: The Moscow Typikon concludes the Resurrectional Troparion with the Kontakion of the Triodion, as if Dominical; more correctly, however, our Sluzhebniks append also the Theotokion; for the Kontakion of the Triodion is not Festal, but Penitential.
[^473]: This is against the Armenians, who fast on the Friday of this Week in memory of the fast of the Ninevites, and on Saturday in memory of St. Sergius the Stratilates; the Greeks, however, being exceedingly hostile to the Armenians, forged a tale against them, as though they then fasted for Artzivurtz, the dog of the Armenian Bishop Sergius, devoured by wolves, which Sergius was accustomed to send before himself into every city and place whither he himself intended to go (Nilles *Calend.* Part. 2, pag. 8).
[^474]: In the Greek Typika on these Sundays neither the Polyeleos is sung, nor **"By the waters of Babylon"**, but only the *Amomoi* (Blameless); for the Greek Typikon appoints the Polyeleos on Sundays only from September 20 until the Nativity of Christ (*Kephal.* 5).
[^475]: Thus the Typikon at the conclusion of the Sunday of the Prodigal Son.
[^476]: Both at Vespers and at Matins, two Litanies: **"In peace let us pray to the Lord"**, and at the conclusion **"Have mercy on us, O God"**, we take for the departed; for even if the Typikon of the Triodion does not appoint these, nevertheless it appoints after Vespers a Pannychis (Parastas), at which these Litanies occur. Since, therefore, we do not nowadays celebrate a Pannychis after Vespers, for this reason we say its Litanies at least at Vespers and at Matins; although our Horologia (*Molytvoslovy*) (at the conclusion) appoint the Funeral Litany **"Have mercy on us, O God"** only at the end of Matins. We also append after the Dismissal of this Litany: **"In blessed falling asleep"**, as at the Parastas. At Matins we say the Small Litanies at their places, namely after the Kathisma, and in the Canon after the 3rd, 6th, and 9th Odes; but we say the usual ones, and not for the departed, for our Pochaiv Triodion does not then take Litanies for the departed. The *Tserkovne Oko* and the Moscow Typikon appoint a Litany for the departed after the 6th Ode. The Greek Triodion commands to commemorate the departed at the Litany after the Kathisma, and at all Litanies of the Canon: *Ἐν δὲ ταῖς συναπταῖς πάσαις τῆς Στιχολογίας, καὶ τοῦ Κανόνος μνημονεύονται πάντες οἱ προαπελθόντες Πατέρες, καὶ Ἀδελφοὶ ἡμῶν* [And in all the synaptes of the Stichologia, and of the Canon, all our fathers and brethren who have departed before us are commemorated]. Unto this agrees also our Manuscript Typikon, saying: "The Priest says the Diaconal parts; whether after the Kathisma or after the Canon he always commemorates the departed." The *Euchologion* of Benedict XIV on the Funeral Litany appoints the prayer only when it begins from **"Have mercy on us, O God"**, in nowise when it begins from **"Again and again"**.
[^477]: Likewise also our Manuscript Typikon and the *Tserkovne Oko* say: "The Verses of Vespers." The usual Verses, however, appointed in the Horologion at the Alleluia after **"God is the Lord"**, were formerly read on the Saturdays of the whole year, in which the Saint indeed had a Service, but did not have a Troparion.
[^478]: *Ποιοῦντες καὶ τὰς συνήθως μετανοίας ἔν τε τῷ Ἑσπερινῷ, καὶ τῷ Ὄρθρῳ, καὶ ταῖς ὥραις* [Making also the customary metanoias both at Vespers, and at Matins, and at the Hours] (Triodion, on Cheesefare Wednesday at Matins). Likewise also we sing Great Compline with prostrations, as the Triodion says; the Midnight Office with the 16 customary prostrations, as in the Great Forty Days.
[^479]: On Wednesday evening for Thursday the Triodion has something more, as will be indicated later.
[^480]: See the Note on p. 362.
[^481]: Concerning two Saints the Typikon here is silent; nevertheless, on Monday of the 1st Week of the Fast the *Tserkovne Oko* takes [two], as we said.
[^482]: Since in the Greek Triodia the Triodion-Canon is not always with two Heirmoi, but sometimes with one Heirmos, for this reason the Greek Triodia give for these cases the following Typikon: *Ἐν αἷς δὲ ᾠδαῖς ψάλλομεν τὸ Τριῴδιον, καταλιμπάνομεν τὰς τοιαύτας ᾠδὰς τοῦ δευτέρου Κανόνος τῆς Ὀκτωήχου ὁλοκληρῶς. Εἰ δὲ δύοιрμόν ἐστι τὸ Тριῴδιον, καταλιμπάνομεν καὶ τῶν δύο Κανόνων τῆς Ὀκτωήχου τὰς ᾠδάς* [And in whichever Odes we chant the Triodion, we omit entirely such Odes of the second Canon of the Octoechos. If, however, the Triodion has two Heirmoi, we omit the Odes of both Canons of the Octoechos].
[^483]: The Greek Typikon (*Kephal.* 5) does not indicate prostrations after the Proemial Psalm; nevertheless, the Greek Triodion indicates them on Monday of the 1st Week of the Great Forty Days. The Pochaiv Triodion speaks insufficiently: "At Vespers we begin prostrations"; more precisely the *Tserkovne Oko* and the Moscow Typikon: "From the beginning of Vespers we begin prostrations." For the rest, where the Typika do not precisely indicate the prostrations here, there they are to be supplemented by the Typikon of the Great Forty Days.
[^484]: These Troparia according to our Horologia and our Manuscript Typikon are sung in Tone 1; according to the Greek Triodion (1724 *Venet.*) in Tone 2; according to the Russian Typika in Tone 4; according to the Greek Horologion of the Propaganda in Tone 5. The Troparion **"Beneath Thy compassion"** in the Greek Triodion is appointed without chanting: *λέγεται χωρὶς μετανοίας, καὶ μέλους* [it is said without metanoia and without melody] (On Cheesefare Sunday evening). The *Horologion* of the Propaganda sings also this Troparion, and in this case appends **"Glory..."** to **"Pray for us..."**, and **"Both now..."** to **"Beneath Thy compassion"**, and with this in the new Constantinopolitan Typikon agrees the Patriarch of Constantinople Gregory VI (On Cheesefare Sunday).
[^485]: The Greek Typikon appoints only 12 (*Kephal.* 5); our Greek Triodion, however, 16 (on Monday of the Forty Days).
[^486]: Concerning the place of the placement of **"Lord, have mercy"** 3 times, **"Glory... Both now..."**, **"More honorable than the Cherubim"**, **"In the name of the Lord"**, **"He that is blessed"** (or **"O God, be gracious unto us"**, or **"Through the prayers of our holy Fathers"**), we find three different editions.
