For some place them before the Prayer of St. Ephrem (our Horologia and the *Tserkovne Oko* and our Manuscript Typikon); some place them before the prayer **"O Heavenly King"** (the Greek and Pochaiv Triodion and the *Horologion* of the Propaganda, and the Russian Typika); some entirely omit them (the Greek Typikon, *Kephal.* 5).
[^487]: We consider that somewhat more reasonably the Greeks first stretch out the hand, then sign themselves with the cross, rather than we who first sign ourselves with the cross, then stretch out the hand; for in the East the stretching out of the hand is a sign of honor, upon which the sign of the cross ought to rest.
[^488]: Empress Irene in her Typikon gives the manner of the greater and smaller metanoia, not however at the Prayer of St. Ephrem, but at **"O God, be merciful to me, a sinner"**, with **"I have sinned against Thee, O Lord, forgive me"**; she appoints in both the kneeling of the knees, the difference between the one and the other she places in this: that the greater is made more slowly, while the smaller more quickly; at the beginning of the bow she commands to stretch out the hands, as also the Moscow Typikon of 1859 at the Prayer of Ephrem (Nilles *Calend.* t. 1 p. LVII).
[^489]: The Greek Triodion which we have, and the Moscow Typikon, do not mention prostrations at the 2nd **"Come, let us worship"**; they are mentioned, however, by the *Tserkovne Oko* and our Manuscript Typikon.
[^490]: These prostrations we have supplemented from the 1st day of the Great Forty Days, for the Typikon speaks here only in general: "We sing Great Compline with prostrations"; likewise also at the Midnight Office.
[^491]: Thus the Moscow Typikon; a little more obscurely the Pochaiv Triodion; and the *Tserkovne Oko* says: "The daily Troparia and to the Temple."
[^492]: "We recite the Psalter in 3 Kathismata, at each *Glory* making 3 prostrations to the ground." The *Tserkovne Oko* on Monday of the 1st Week of the Great Fast. The Greek Typikon, however, does not have "to the ground".

[^494]: *Ἡμεῖς δὲ εἰς τὴν πλήρωσιν τῶν Αἴνων, ἤτοι εἰς τὸ Δόξα ἐν ὑψίστοις Θεῷ ποιοῦμεν μετανοίας γ΄* [And we at the completion of the Praises, that is at the "Glory to God in the highest", make 3 metanoias] (Greek Typikon, *Kephal.* 5).
[^495]: These we have supplemented from the Typikon of Matins of the Great Forty Days, for here the Triodion mentions prostrations only in general.
[^496]: The Peremyshl Typikon appoints also **"Save, O God"**; the other Typika, however, do not have this; for although the Service of these days has a Lenten aspect, yet not in all points; thus here only two Kathismata are taken, and in the Canon the Octoechos, whereas in the Great Forty Days three Kathismata, and Canons without the Octoechos.

[^498]: *Tserkovne Oko*.
[^499]: This Martyr's Troparion is a Sessional Hymn taken from the Sessional Hymns of Matins of the Octoechos, which follows the 2nd Kathisma. When the Saint has a Kontakion, this Martyr's Troparion is sung after the 1st Kathisma together with the Penitential Sessional Hymns, before their Theotokion, with the Martyr's Refrain: **"Wondrous is God"** (*Дивенъ Богъ*). *Εἰς τὴν ζ΄. ᾠδὴν λέγεται τὸ Μαρτυρικὸν, εἰ οὐκ ἔχει τὸ Μηναῖον Κοντάκιον, εἰ δὲ ἔχει, λέγεται τοῦτο ὁμοῦ μετὰ τῶν καθισμάτων* [At the 7th Ode the Martyrikon is said if the Menaion does not have a Kontakion; if however it has one, this is said together with the Sessional Hymns] (*Τριῴδιον*, at the end of the Octoechos after the Sessional Hymns).

At the 2nd verse of the Troparion **"In the morning..."** only the *Tserkovne Oko* and the Moscow Typikon appoint prostrations.
[^501]: Pochaiv Triodion on Monday of the 1st Week of the Great Fast.
[^502]: Other Typika do not command the Choirs, but only the Reader to make genuflections: *Καὶ ἡμῶν ἀνταποκρινομένων, ὁ αὐτὸς (the Reader) ποιεῖ γονυκλισίαν ἐπὶ γῆς, ὡσαύτως καὶ εἰς τοὺς λοιποὺς δύο στίχους* [And as we respond, he (the Reader) makes a genuflection upon the earth, and likewise also at the remaining two verses]. Thus our Greek Triodion and Manuscript Typikon on Monday of the 1st Week of the Fast. The *Tserkovne Oko* commands the Reader to make only a single prostration to the ground, and this after the 2nd verse (Monday of the 1st Week of the Forty Days); although inconsistently with this, in Chapter 12 it appoints 3 prostrations. The Moscow Typikon, however, prescribes genuflections both to the Choir and to the Reader, saying: "When the Canonarch says the Verse, at that time we bend the knees; and when the Singers chant, then he bends the knees" (Ch. 9).

[^512]: For thus we do also within the Feast; for example, January 7 and September 15.
[^513]: Concerning the Litany we find disagreement in the Typika. For some Typika are silent concerning it, some prescribe it, and some exclude it. Those which are silent concerning it are 4 Vatican manuscript Typika, placed in the Library Catalogue under Nos. 784, 101, 782, and 785: the 1st from the year 1317, the 2nd 1373, the 3rd of unknown year, yet of the 14th century, the 4th of the 14th or 15th century. Those which prescribe the Litany are: the Greek Venice Triodion of the year 1724, the Kyiv-Pechersk of 1640, the Lviv of 1753, and certain manuscripts. Those which exclude it are: the Moscow Typikon of 1859 for the use of the All-Russian Church with the approval of the Directing Synod; the *Tserkovne Oko* and the ancient manuscript Typikon which we possess; for although these two Typika are silent concerning the Litany on Cheesefare Sunday, nevertheless on the 1st Sunday of the Fast they exclude it, for immediately after the Prokimenon they say: "After this, **'Vouchsafe, O Lord'**." We, however, deem that the Typika which omit the Litany are more reasonable: for at all other Lenten Vespers and Matins that Litany is not present, but after the Troparia in its place is appointed **"Lord, have mercy"** 40 times, and **"He that is blessed"** (or something else) with the prayer **"O Heavenly King"**. Since, therefore, Vespers of these Sundays is also Lenten, for this reason on them there ought not to be a Litany, all the more since the prayer **"O Heavenly King"** can stand in place of the petitions which are in the Litany. And that not every Entrance draws after itself the Litany **"Let us all say"**, this is evident from the Typikon of the Finding of the Precious Head of the Forerunner when it occurs on Lenten days, there being no Liturgy of the Presanctified; for then also the *Tserkovne Oko* in Chapter Mark 92 says: "We do not say **'Let us all say'**, but **'Vouchsafe, O Lord'**," and our and the Russian Typika say: "Reading of the day and of the Forerunner, and immediately **'Vouchsafe, O Lord'**."

[^516]: These bows are indicated in all Slavic Typika.
[^517]: *Tserkovne Oko*.
[^518]: Thus has the Moscow Typikon corresponding to the Greek Typikon; the *Tserkovne Oko*, however, and our Pochaiv Triodion, in the Odes in which there is no Triodion, take not only the Canon of the Saint, but also both Canons of the Octoechos, unchangeably as also outside the Forty Days. "In our Ruthenian borders (says the Pochaiv Triodion) it was established from of old to chant (or rather to read) the Octoechos, I deem because of the scarcity of books, not only the Menaion, but also the others, from which the instructive homilies of the Holy Fathers ought to be read." But I deem that this was on account of the abundance of piety of the Slavs, unto whom the length of the Greek divine services does not suffice. Nevertheless, since nowadays times of abbreviation have arrived, for this reason it suffices for us to adhere to the Greek Typikon.

[^520]: These almost in their entirety the Greek Triodion also has: *Τῷ κυρίῳ ᾄσωμεν εἰς τὸν ἦχον τοῦ κανόνος τοῦ Μηναίου, τοῦ κατὰ τὴν ἡμέραν Ἁγίου. Ψάλλομεν δὲ αὐτὸν μετὰ τοῦ Εἱρμοῦ εἰς ϛ΄· ἀπὸ δὲ τοῦ στίχου τοῦ Τότε ἔσπευσαν εἰσέρχονται τὰ Τριῴδια, καὶ λέγομεν τὰ δ΄ Τροπάρια τοῦ Κυρίου Ἰωσήφ· εἰς δὲ τὸ Κύριος βασιλεύων ἄρχεται τὸ Τριῴδιον τοῦ Κυρίου Θεοδώρου, καὶ ψάλλομεν ὁ εἷς χορὸς τὸ ἓν Τροπάριον, ὁ δὲ ἕτερος τὸ ἄλλο. Εἶθ' οὕτως ἑνουμένων τῶν δύο χορῶν δοξάζομεν ὑψηλοτέρᾳ φωνῇ, καὶ ψάλλομεν τὸ Τριῳδικὸν Τροπάριον τοῦ Τριῳδίου, Καὶ νῦν Θεοτοκίον, καὶ πάλιν ὑψηλοτέρᾳ τῇ φωνῇ λέγομεν τὸ Δόξα σοι, καὶ ψάλλομεν τὸ ἕτερον Τροπάριον τοῦ Κυρίου Θεοδώρου, ἐπεὶ γὰρ ἀνὰ έ Τροπάρια ἔχει ἡ Ὠδή· ὕστερον δὲ λέγομεν καταβασίαν τὸν Εἱρμὸν τοῦ δευτέρου Κανόνος* [Let us sing unto the Lord in the tone of the canon of the Menaion, of the Saint of the day. And we chant it with the Heirmos unto 6; and from the verse "Then they made haste" the Triodia enter, and we say the 4 Troparia of Lord Joseph; and at "The Lord reigneth" begins the Triodion of Lord Theodore, and the one choir chants the one Troparion, and the other the other. Then, the two choirs having thus united, we doxologize with a higher voice, and chant the Triodic Troparion of the Triodion, Both now, Theotokion, and again with a higher voice we say "Glory to Thee", and chant the other Troparion of Lord Theodore, since the Ode has 5 Troparia each; and afterwards we say as Katavasia the Heirmos of the second Canon].

[^533]: Of the 6 Stichera appointed in the Octoechos at **"Lord, I have cried"**, the first and the three last are Martyrs' Stichera, the second, generally speaking, is of a Hierarch, the third of a Venerable One.
[^534]: We deem that it is proper to say **"Let us attend. Peace be unto all. Wisdom. Let us attend"** not only before the 1st, but also before the 2nd Prokimenon.
[^535]: Concerning the Tricerion the *Tserkovne Oko* also makes mention in the Typikon of the Great Fast, fol. 42 verso. More correctly, however, the Greek Typika make mention only of a candlestick with a candle: *Ὁ δὲ Ἱερεὺς λαβὼν τῇ δεξιᾷ χειρὶ θυμιατὸν καὶ μανουάλιον μετὰ κηροῦ, ἵσταται ἐνώπιον τῆς ἁγίας Τραπέζης, καὶ σφραγίζων σταυρὸν, λέγει: Σοφία ὀρθοί! Φῶς Χριστοῦ φαίνει πᾶσι* [And the Priest, having taken in his right hand the censer and the candlestick with a candle, stands before the Holy Table, and signing a cross, says: Wisdom! Stand upright! The Light of Christ illumines all] (*Euchologion* of Benedict XIV). Concerning the turning towards the people, however, of the Greek books only the Barberini *Euchologion* makes mention, saying: *Καὶ ἀνιστάμενος ὁ Ἱερεὺς μετὰ κηροῦ εἰς τὰ διάστυλα (holy Doors) λέγει: Φῶς Χριστοῦ φαίνει* [And the Priest rising with the candle at the folding-doors (holy Doors) says: The Light of Christ shines forth].
[^536]: To chant **"Let my prayer be set forth"** the Greek *Euchologion* commands the Cantor, the *Triodion* the Domestikos, the Slavic Typika the Reader, other Greek ones Morellius, and the Barberini to the Priest, which also corresponds to the new Constantinopolitan Typikon, saying: *Τὸ Κατευθυνθήτω ψαλ. ἐκ τοῦ Βήματος ἅπαξ, καὶ ἀνὰ δὶς ἐκ τῶν Χορῶν, καὶ αὖθις ἐκ τοῦ Βήματος ἅπαξ* [The "Let my prayer be set forth" is chanted from the Bema once, and twice by each of the Choirs, and again from the Bema once].

[^542]: For what reason the commemoration of the Holy Great-Martyr Theodore is celebrated on this Saturday, Nilles shows in *Kalendarium Manuale (Heortologion)*, writing thus: "At Constantinople the cult of the same Holy Martyr was very celebrated on account of the miracle of the *kolyva* (*τῶν κολλύβων*), whose anniversary commemoration is celebrated on the first Saturday of Lent by an established feast of its own. Julian the Apostate wishing indeed to compel the Constantinopolitans to eat idol-meats, and for this reason ordering in the year 362 that all things for sale in the marketplace be secretly tainted and polluted with the blood of sacrifices, Saint Theodore the Tyro, appearing by night in a vision to the Bishop of the royal City, commands that another food supply, called by the Euchaitian word *kolyva* (*κόλλυβα*), be furnished to the poor." (At Feb. 17, p. 106). And *kolyva* (*κόλλυβα*), according to the words of Nikephoros Kallistos, is boiled wheat. Thus also the Synaxarion of this Saturday has it.

[^546]: And there being thus 3 Canons, Great Compline would be unending.
[^547]: Orthodoxy, *Ὀρθοδοξία*, signifies Right-belief, that is, the right faith in the veneration of the holy Icons, which the Iconoclasts were destroying. The Seventh Ecumenical Council (Second of Nicaea) indeed restored their veneration, in the year 785 [787]; and another Council convened in Constantinople in the year 842, under Saint Methodius, by the cooperation of the Holy Empress Theodora, commanded that the memory of this restoration be celebrated every year, on the 1st Sunday of the Great Forty Days.

[^558]: Kyiv and Lviv Triodion.
[^559]: The Akathist Order, that is, the Non-sitting [Hymn], is chanted in memory of the thrice-repeated deliverance of Constantinople from the invasion of the heathen, and this by the intercession of the Great Mother of God, whose precious Robe, brought from Jerusalem and placed in the Blachernae Church by Saint Pulcheria, and her icon under the name Hodegetria, painted according to the opinion of the Greeks by Saint Luke, they carried around the City, calling upon her aid. The first invasion was under the Emperor Heraclius (reigned 610 to 641) by the Persian army under the command of Satrap Sarbaros, and likewise by the Scythian army under the command of the Khagan. It was defeated partly by a small number of Christian Warriors who went out against the adversaries from the Church of the Most Holy Theotokos; and partly by a violent tempest at sea, which destroyed them with their ships. The second invasion was 36 years later, namely under Constantine Pogonatus (668–685), and ended partly by pestilence among the soldiers, and partly by a tempest at sea, when they were returning home. The third was under Leo the Isaurian (717–741), and ended by a tempest at sea, and famine, and the division of their forces against the Bulgars, by whom they were crushed. (*Synaxarion* of this Saturday, concerning which also Nilles, *Kalendarium Manuale*, Vol. 2, p. 152).
[^560]: Apart from the initial Kontakion, **"To thee, the Champion Leader"** (*Взбранной Воеводе*), the rest of the Akathist consists of 12 Ikoses and 12 Kontakia, together of 24 clauses, according to the number of the 24 letters of the Greek Alphabet, by which each clause in the Greek text begins in order.
[^561]: In the Patriarchal Church of Constantinople they sing the Akathist not at Matins, but earlier at Little Compline, namely at **"It is truly right"** the 1st Stasis the Patriarch himself; the 2nd, after the 3rd Ode of the Festal Canon, the Metropolitan of Caesarea; the 3rd after the 6th Ode, the Metropolitan of Ephesus; the 4th after the 9th Ode, the Metropolitan of Heraclea. (*New Typikon of Constantinople*).
[^562]: The Greek Triodion does not appoint even a single censing. The *Tserkovne Oko* and our manuscript Typikon appoint 4 censings, as do also our and the Russian Typika; however, they command to cense only the Icons and the Brethren, whereas our and the Russian [Typika command to cense] the Icons and Brethren only the first time, and at the other three Staseis only the Superior and the Choirs. It would be better for us to cense only once, at the beginning.
[^563]: And with the exposition of the Holy Mysteries. (*Synod of Lviv*, Title IV, Chap. IV, Part VIII).
[^564]: The Pochaiv Triodion says: "We sing the usual Compline." But the *Tserkovne Oko*, and after it the Russian Typika, appoint Great Compline, and then after the 1st Trisagion the Troparion of the Feast is sung, after the 2nd the usual Troparia **"Have mercy on us, O Lord"**; after the Canon of Lazarus, instead of **"It is truly right"**, the Heirmos of the 9th Ode of the Canon, and after the Trisagion the Kontakion of the Feast.
[^565]: Likewise also the new Typikon of Constantinople: *Δόξα σοι ὁ Θεὸς* [Glory to Thee, O God]; but the Russian Typika take the Biblical Odes.
[^566]: The Greek *βαΐον* signifies a palm branch, that is, of the palm tree, whence the Latin *Dominica Palmarum*; as John also says (12:13): *λαβον τὰ βαΐα τῶν φοινίκων* [They took branches of palm trees], although without doubt the people cut branches also from olive trees, since Christ rode into Jerusalem from the Mount of Olives. Where palms do not grow, they use only branches of olives; among us, however, since olives do not grow either, they use willow [branches]; for this reason the *Tserkovne Oko* also says: "Saying the prayer over the willow."

[^569]: The Patriarch of Constantinople blesses the Palms before the Praises, and distributes them at the Praises, thus: "The Archdeacon vests the Patriarch with epitrachelion and omophorion, and says: *Τοῦ Κυρίου δεηθῶμεν* [Let us pray to the Lord]; and the Patriarch reads the prayer of the Palms. Then the 1st choir begins *Πᾶσα πνοὴ* [Let every breath]. At *Αἰνεῖτε* [Praise Him], the Patriarch venerates the holy Icon of the Palm-bearer, blesses, and ascends the throne. Then the Grand Archimandrite presents him the customary braid of palm-leaves. While the Praises are being sung, the holy Hierarchs also venerate the holy Icon and receive Palms from the Patriarch, likewise also the Officials." (*New Typikon of Constantinople*).
[^570]: The Service of these days is penitential-passion, forefeast to the Passions of Christ the Lord, Who is presented here under the type of the Patriarch Joseph, persecuted by his brethren, and of the Jewish Synagogue, which is presented under the type of the unfaithful and adulterous wife of Potiphar; with the interweaving of the Gospel history of these days, that is: on Monday concerning the withered fig tree, on Tuesday concerning the Dread Judgment, on Wednesday concerning the woman who anointed Him with myrrh, and concerning Judas's conspiracy with the Jews.
[^571]: The Pochaiv Molitvoslov of 1793 appoints this Dismissal "On Sunday evening, and on Monday, Tuesday, and Wednesday of the Holy and Great Week at Matins and at the Hours"; the Pochaiv Horologion of 1811 joins also Thursday at Matins until Wednesday. However, at these Matins the Typika of the Triodion do not indicate a Dismissal, since they are minor and on the first three days are of a fasting form and joined to the 1st Hour; at the Hours, however, one must not omit this Dismissal, nor at the Presanctified, as we deem; the *Tserkovne Oko* of 1615, and before it the Leitourgikon of Balaban of 1604, extend this Dismissal to the whole of Holy Week, except Saturday.

[^573]: Thus our Menaion and the Russian ones. More reasonably, the *Tserkovne Oko* takes Great Compline on Wednesday evening as well, and only on Thursday and Friday evening Little Compline, on account of the length of the Passion and Burial Matins. Likewise also the Greek Typikon of Sabbas: for at the end of Compline it says: *Ἐν δὲ τῷ τέλει τῶν Ἀποδείπνων ποιοῦμεν προσκυνήματα ἀργὰ ἐπὶ γῆς ἅπαντες, καὶ τῆς εὐχῆς γενομένης ὑπὸ τοῦ Πρεσβυτέρου ἀπολυόμεθα* [At the end of Compline we all make slow prostrations to the ground, and after the prayer has been said by the Priest, we are dismissed]. And that prayer is without doubt: **"O Master, Great in Mercy"** (*Влады́ко многоми́лостиве*), since Little Compline has more prayers, whereas here only one is mentioned. All the more so, since the Greek Triodion, concerning that falling or prostrations to the earth made at Great Compline before the prayer **"O Master, Great in Mercy"**, says: *Δεῖ εἰδέναι, ὅτι τὰ πλείονα τῶν Τυπικῶν, καὶ ἡ συνήθεια ὁρίζουσι, μετὰ τὸ τελευταῖον Τρισάγιον, πίπτειν ἅπαντας ἐπὶ γῆς, καὶ λέγειν τὸν Ἱερέα μεγαλοφώνως τὴν ἐν τῷ μεγάλῳ Ἑσπερινῷ Εὐχὴν: Δέσποτα πολυέλεε* [One must know that most of the Typika and custom prescribe that after the last Trisagion all fall upon the earth, and the Priest says aloud the prayer of Great Vespers: *O Master, great in mercy*] (Monday of the 1st Week of the Fast, at Great Compline).
[^574]: Neither our manuscript Typikon, nor that of the University of Lviv, nor the *Tserkovne Oko*, nor the Pochaiv Triodion of 1767, nor the Ostromir Gospel appoint the Gospel here at Matins; but two very ancient Vatican codices appoint it, one Greek of the 4th century, the other Slavonic-Glagolitic of the 12th or 13th century (Assemani, *Kalendaria Ecclesiae Universae*, t. 6, p. 563), likewise also the Greek Typikon of Sabbas; and among later ones, the Pochaiv Triodion of 1786, the Greek Triodion of Bonos of 1724, the New Typikon of Constantinople, and the new Rudnytsia Triodion.

[^592]: In the Greek Typika, and in one manuscript Slavonic Triodion which we possess, censing is not appointed at any of the 12 Gospels, nor the Little Ektenia. The *Tserkovne Oko* appoints the Little Ektenia both at the 1st Gospel and before all 5 Sessional Hymns, but commands censing to be omitted at the 8th, 9th, 10th, and 11th Gospels. The New Typikon of Constantinople appoints a single censing, not at the Gospel, but at the beginning of Matins during the Two Psalms according to the general Typikon of Matins; but since among us at minor Matins this censing is neglected, for this reason we have here at least appointed at the 1st [Gospel] the censing not only of the Holy Table, but also of the Icons and Choirs, as the general Typikon of the beginning of Matins has it. And if the Greek Typika do not appoint censing at these Gospels, it is no wonder, since in Greek and Slavonic Typika censing is not appointed at the Matins Gospels. The *Tserkovne Oko* appoints two censings at the 1st Gospel, one at the beginning, of the Holy Table and the whole Sanctuary only, and another at the end, of the whole church, both Icons and Choirs; at the 6 following, the censing of the Icons, Superior, and Choirs (however, here it is not entirely consistent with itself, when it says that at the Sessional Hymns the Priest or Deacon censes at the Holy Table); at the 8th, 9th, 10th, and 11th Gospels it omits censing, but at the 12th it again appoints the censing of the whole church, and of the Choirs and Brethren. The *Tserkovne Oko* is followed by the Moscow Typikon; however, at the 1st Gospel it appoints only one censing at the beginning, of the whole Temple; nor does it say accurately that the Ektenias of the Sessional Hymns are said until the 8th Gospel, since the Sessional Hymns extend only until the 6th Gospel. The Greek Typika, even if they do not appoint censing at the Gospels, nevertheless command [the faithful] to stand at the Sessional Hymns: *Οὐ καθήμεθα δὲ ἐν τοῖς τοιούτοις Καθίσμασιν, ἀλλ’ ἱστάμενοι ψάλλομεν αὐτὰ* [We do not sit at such Sessional Hymns, but standing we chant them].

Ἀρχιμανδρίτης, ἐνδεδυμένος χρυσοῦν φελώνιον* [The Grand Archimandrite, vested in a golden phelonion]. (*New Typikon of Constantinople*).

According to the *Tserkovne Oko*, all at the 3rd hour of the day; according to the New Typikon of Constantinople, at the 1st hour of the day.
[^604]: The majestic rite of the Plashchanytsia is not appointed in ancient Triodia and Typika, for it was introduced later; however, today it is used by all followers of the Greek Rite, and among us also with the exposition of the Holy Mysteries, according to the custom of the Latins of the Austrian Empire and various German dioceses (Nilles, *Kalend.* t. 2, p. 312). It is described in our Sluzhebnyks published in Pochaiv as early as the reign of Stanislaus Augustus in 1767, and later in the Russian Typika, and in the new Typikon of the Patriarchate of Constantinople; however, in our Sluzhebnyks the procession with the Plashchanytsia is appointed only at Vespers, in the Russian Typika only at Saturday Matins after the Great Doxology, while in the Typikon of Constantinople the carrying out into the temple to the Tomb is appointed at Vespers, and later the procession around the temple at Matins.
[^605]: Many Typika join to **"The noble Joseph"** also the Troparion of the Myrrh-bearing Women (with the refrain **"Glory... Both now..."**); but the *Tserkovne Oko* and our manuscript Typikon and manuscript Triodion which we possess appoint only **"The noble Joseph"**, and thus they do in the Cathedral Church.
[^606]: According to the Typikon of the New Typikon of Constantinople, the deposition of the Plashchanytsia in the Tomb takes place during the chanting of the Aposticha, and this as follows: *Ψάλλονται τὰ Ἀπόστιχα, καὶ ἐξέρχονται ἐκ τῆς ἀριστερᾶς Πύλης τοῦ Βήματος ἕξ Ἱερεῖς ἀσκεπεῖς, ἐνδεδυμένοι, μετὰ Ἱερατικῆς στολῆς φέροντες μετ’ εὐλαβείας ἐπὶ κεφαλῆς τὸν Ἐπιτάφιον, ἐξ ὧν ὁ Ἀρχιμανδρίτης κρατεῖ καὶ τὸ ἅγιον Εὐαγγέλιον, προπορευομένων τῶν ψαλτῶν, τοῦ Πριμμικηρίου κρατοῦντος τὸ Διβάμβουλον (ро́дъ свѣ́щника съ двѣма̀ лампа́дама), τῶν Διακόνων μετὰ λαμπάδων καὶ θυμιατῶν, καὶ οὕτω προχωροῦσι μέχρι τοῦ ηὐτρεπισμένου κουβουκλίου, καὶ οἱ μὲν Ψάλται ἵστανται κατὰ σειρὰν, ψάλλοντες τὰ Ἀπόστιχα, οἱ δὲ Διάκονοι θυμιάζοντες περιέρχονται γ’ μετὰ τῶν Ἱερέων κύκλωθεν τοῦ κουβουκλίου. Εἶτα τιθέασι τὸν Ἐπιτάφιον ἔνδον τοῦ κουβουκλίου, ὁ δὲ Πορτάρης ῥαίνει εὔοσμα ἄνθη ἐπάνω εἰς τὸν Ἐπιτάφιον, καὶ πλησιάσας ὁ Πατριάρχης προσκυνεῖ τὸν Ἐπιτάφιον, εὐλογεῖ τοὺς Χριστιανοὺς, καὶ ἀνέρχεται εἰς τὸν θρόνον. Τότε ὁ Πορτάρης προσφέρει αὐτῷ ἄνθη εὔοσμα ηὐτρεπισμένα. Εἶτα καὶ οἱ ἅγιοι Ἀρχιερεῖς, ἀνὰ δύο ἀνὰ δύο, προσκυνοῦντες, τὸν Ἐπιτάφιον, λαμβάνουσιν ἄνθη παρὰ τοῦ Πατριάρχου, ὡσαύτως καὶ οἱ Ἐκκλησιαστικοὶ ὀφφικιοῦχοι. Τούτων δὲ γινομένων, ὁ β’. χορὸς ψάλλει τὸ Δοξαστικὸν, ἀπὸ δὲ τοῦ Δοξαστικοῦ... Νῦν ἀπολύεις*, and the rest [The Aposticha are chanted, and there exit from the left door of the Sanctuary six priests uncovered, vested in priestly robes, carrying with piety upon their heads the Epitaphios, of whom the Archimandrite holds also the Holy Gospel, while the chanters precede, the Primicerius holding the Divamboulon (a kind of candlestick with two lamps), the Deacons with lamps and censers; and thus they proceed as far as the prepared canopy (Kouvouklion); and the Chanters stand in order, chanting the Aposticha, while the Deacons, censing, circle thrice with the Priests around the canopy. Then they place the Epitaphios inside the canopy, and the Portaris sprinkles fragrant flowers upon the Epitaphios; and the Patriarch, drawing near, venerates the Epitaphios, blesses the Christians, and ascends the throne. Then the Portaris offers him prepared fragrant flowers. Then also the Holy Hierarchs, two by two, having venerated the Epitaphios, receive flowers from the Patriarch, likewise also the ecclesiastical dignitaries. While these things take place, the second choir chants the Doxastikon, and from the Doxastikon... Now lettest Thou depart, etc.]. But a manuscript addition which we have from the Island of Corfu has as follows: *Ἡ ἐπιτάφιος εἰκὼν ἐπιφέρεται τῇ ἁγ. καὶ μεγ. Παρασκευῇ ἀπὸ τοῦ Βήματος εἰς τὸ τέλος τοῦ Ἑσπερινοῦ, ὅταν ψάλλονται τὰ Ἀπολυτίκια Τроπάρια Ὁ εὐσχήμων Ἰωσὴφ, κτλ. καὶ ἀναποτίθεται εἰς τράπεζαν ηὐτρεπισμένην ἐν τῷ μέσῳ τοῦ Πρεσβυτερίου, ὅπου ὑπάρχουσι καὶ ἀφίενται κηρία ἡμμένα μετὰ σκευῶν ἀνθέων καθ’ ὅλης τῆς ἡμέρας ἕως τῆς ὥρας τοῦ Ὄρθρου τοῦ μεγάλου Σαββάτου (τελουμένου τούτου συνήθως κατὰ τὴν ἑσπέραν τῆς μεγ. Παρασκευῆς), ὅπου πάλιν εἰς τὸ τέλος τῆς Δοξολογίας τῆς μεγ. εἰς τὸ Ἅγιος ὁ Θεὸς ἐκφέρεται ὁ Ἐπιτάφιος, ἀλλὰ τῆς ἐκκλησίας ἔσωθεν καὶ ἔξωθεν κατὰ τὰς περιστάσεις, καὶ πάλιν ἐναποτίθεται εἰς τὴν ἰδίαν τράπεζαν, μέχρι τῆς ὥρας τῆς Λειτουργίας τοῦ μεγ. Σαββάτου, ὅτε ἰδιωτικῶς παρ’ ἑνὸς Ἱερέως μετ’ Ἐπιτραχήλιον τίθεται εἰς τὸ Βῆμα εἰς τὸν συνήθη τόπον* [The icon of the Epitaphios is carried on Holy and Great Friday from the Sanctuary at the end of Vespers, when the Dismissal Troparia "The noble Joseph," etc. are chanted, and is placed upon a table prepared in the midst of the Presbytery, where lit candles with vessels of flowers are and remain throughout the whole day until the hour of Matins of Great Saturday (this being celebrated usually on the evening of Great Friday), where again at the end of the Great Doxology at "Holy God" the Epitaphios is carried out, either inside or outside the church according to circumstances, and is again deposited upon the same table, until the hour of the Liturgy of Great Saturday, when privately by one Priest wearing an epitrachelion it is placed into the Sanctuary in the usual place].
[^607]: Thus our Pochaiv Triodion; other editions, from the usual beginning even unto **"The Lord is God"**, take all things according to custom; only the Typikon of the University of Lviv begins from the Six Psalms.
[^608]: Our Triodion and the Greek one which we possess, and the Typikon of the University of Lviv do not say: "twice"; however, "twice" is found in the *Tserkovne Oko* and the manuscript Triodion and Typikon which we possess.
[^609]: Our Pochaiv Triodion does not command anything to be censed, saying only: "And having censed crosswise." The Greek Triodion mentions censing only at the 2nd and 3rd Stases, but does not say what is to be censed. The *Tserkovne Oko* appoints the censing at the 1st Stasis, of the whole church and the Brethren according to rank, but at the others only of the Choirs. The Typikon of Constantinople appoints it only at the 1st Stasis, and that only of the Plashchanytsia and the people: *Ὁ Πατριάρχης... θυμιάζων τὸν Ἐπιτάφιον, καὶ τὸν λαὸν ἅπαντα* [The Patriarch... censing the Epitaphios and all the people].
[^610]: *Tserkovne Oko*.
[^611]: This Troparion is taken only once by the Greek Typikon of Sabbas, the Greek Triodion, our manuscript Typikon, and another of the University of Lviv; other Typika take it twice, the second time with the refrain **"Glory... Both now..."**. The *Tserkovne Oko* takes it first once, then the end with **"Glory"**, and all with **"Both now"**. And this Troparion all Typika have; but the Troparion **"The noble Joseph"**, chanted before this, various Greek and Slavonic Typika have, in the first place the Greek Typikon of Sabbas; while the Sticheron **"Come, let us bless Joseph"** only our editions have. The New Typikon of Constantinople, before the Troparion **"The noble Joseph"**, appoints also the Resurrection Troparion **"When Thou didst descend unto death"**, and [that of] the Myrrh-bearing Women. Before the Troparion **"The noble Joseph"**, our Typika and the Greek appoint: **"Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!"** (*Πρόσχωμεν, Εἰρήνη πᾶσι, Σοφία*); other Slavonic ones only: **"Wisdom! Stand upright!"**; and those which do not appoint **"The noble Joseph"** here say **"Wisdom! Stand upright!"** before the Troparion of the Prophecy. The *Tserkovne Oko* has **"Wisdom! Stand upright!"** before **"The noble Joseph"**, and before the Troparion of the Prophecy: **"Let us attend! Peace be to thee! Wisdom, let us attend!"** — The Entrance according to the Russian Typikon takes place thus: "Then the Superior enters into the Holy Altar with the Priests and Deacons, and vests in all the sacred vestments, but the Priests only in robes (phelonia): and he comes out with the Gospel under the Plashchanytsia, the Priests holding it above, and the Deacons censing. At that time there is chiming on all the bells, while those with candles and with two lamps precede him, and both Choirs chant the Funeral Trisagion. The Superior comes before the Holy Royal Doors, and there stands waiting for the end of the last Trisagion. When this has ended, the Superior enters with the Gospel to the Royal Doors, and places the Gospel upon the Table (Holy Table), and the Plashchanytsia upon the prepared table, in the image of the Tomb. After the Dismissal there takes place veneration in monasteries at the Plashchanytsia of the laying down in the Tomb." (*Moscow Typikon*). In the Patriarchate of Constantinople it takes place thus: *Δοξολογία Μ. Εἶτα ἀρχόμεθα τοῦ Ἀσματικοῦ, προπορευόμενοι ἔμπροσθεν τοῦ Ἐπιταφίου, καὶ ἐξερχόμενοι τοῦ Ναοῦ, γίνεται ἡ Εἴξοδος, ὡς εἴθισται; ἐπειδὰν δ’ ἐπανέλθωμεν, εἰσέρχεται ὁ Πατριάρχης κατ’ εὐθεῖαν εἰς τὸ ἱερὸν Βῆμα, μετὰ τῶν λοιπῶν, καὶ εὐθὺς ἐκφωνεῖ· Πρόσχωμεν. Εἰρήνη πᾶσι. Σοφία· καὶ ψάλ. τὸ Τроπάριον Ὅτε κατῆλθες πρὸς τὸν θάνατον, καὶ ἐν τῷ ψάλ. θυμιᾷ κύκλῳ τὴν ἁγίαν Τράπεζαν, προπορευομένου ἔμπροσθεν τοῦ Ἐπιταφίου, εἶτα ταῖς Μυροφόροις Γυναιξὶ, μέχρι τοῦ ἐδείχθη ἀλλότριος· ἑπομένως ψάλ. Ὁ εὐσχήμων Ἰωσὴφ, μέχρι τοῦ, κηδεύσας ἀπέθετο· καὶ τιθέασι τὸν Ἐπιτάφιον ἐπὶ τῆς ἁγίας Τραπέζης. ἅма ὁ Κανονάρχης ἔξωθεν ἀναγινώσκει τὸ Τроπάριον, τὴν Προφητείαν, καὶ τὸν Ἀπόστολον, τὸ δὲ ἅγ. Εὐαγγέλ. λέγεται παρὰ τοῦ Πατριάρχου, ἔμπροσθεν τῆς Πύλης* [Great Doxology. Then we begin the Asmatikon, going before the Epitaphios, and exiting from the Church, the Entrance takes place as is customary; and when we have returned, the Patriarch enters directly into the Holy Sanctuary with the rest, and immediately exclaims: *Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom!* and he chants the Troparion *When Thou didst descend unto death*, and during the chanting censes around the Holy Table, going before the Epitaphios, then *Unto the Myrrh-bearing Women*, up to *appeared as an alien*; subsequently he chants *The noble Joseph*, up to *having wrapped it in linen, he laid it down*; and they place the Epitaphios upon the Holy Table. At the same time the Kanonarch from outside reads the Troparion, the Prophecy, and the Apostle, and the Holy Gospel is said by the Patriarch before the Door]. (*New Typikon of Constantinople*). In various regions of Greece, according to the testimony of Allatius, the Procession takes place thus: *Mos obtinuit, ut, dum Psalm. 118 canitur, et singulis versiculis singula Troparia concinuntur, Christus decore exsculptus, vel affabre in tabula depictus, et in feretro depositus, solemni cereorum ardentium apparatu, Sacerdotibus, quemadmodum, quum sacra Missarum solemnia celebrant, vestitis, reliquo Clero non sine ordine comitante, et Christi fidelibus feretrum prosequentibus per urbis plateas deferatur, proponaturque omnibus adorandus, atque exosculandus: sicque omnium venerationi patet. Interim flores imagini superpositi, maximo studio ab omnibus conquiruntur, quibus sibi maximo bono, non corporis modo, sed rerum etiam domesticarum pollicentur* [The custom has prevailed that, while Psalm 118 is sung and to each verse the several Troparia are chanted in harmony, Christ gracefully sculptured, or skillfully painted on a panel, and laid upon a bier, with a solemn display of burning tapers, the Priests being vested just as when they celebrate the sacred solemnities of the Mass, the rest of the Clergy accompanying in orderly procession, and the faithful in Christ following the bier, is carried through the streets of the city, and is set forth to be adored and kissed by all; and thus is open to the veneration of all. Meanwhile the flowers placed upon the image are sought out by all with the greatest zeal, promising themselves thereby the greatest blessing, not only of the body, but also of domestic affairs]. (Nilles, *Kalend.* t. 2, p. 241).
[^612]: According to the Typika: at the tenth hour of the day, so that the Liturgy concludes at the second hour of the night (*Ὥρα βʹ τῆς νυκτὸς*).
[^613]: The manuscript Typika which we possess, and the *Tserkovne Oko*, appoint only 3 verses: 1. **"God stood"**; 2. **"How long will ye judge"**; 3. **"They knew not"**. The Greek Typikon of Sabbas appoints the whole Psalm for five verses.
[^614]: Thus according to the Typikon of our Triodia and the Russian ones; similarly also the Greek Typikon of Sabbas, only instead of violet vestments it appoints dark ones: *Μέλλοντος δὲ τοῦ Ψάλτου ἄρξασθαι τοῦ παρόντος στίχου, τοῦ Ἀνάστα ὁ Θεὸς, ἀνιστάμενοι οἱ Ἱερεῖς, ἀποδύονται τὰ μαυρὰ φελώνια, καὶ περιβάλλονται ἀνθ’ αὐτῶν λευκὰ* [When the chanter is about to begin the present verse, "Arise, O God," the Priests, standing up, take off the dark phelonia and put on white ones instead of them]. According to the Greek Triodion, however, and according to the *Tserkovne Oko* and other manuscript Typika, white vestments are appointed from the beginning of Vespers. *Μαυρὸς* we interpret as dark, not black, for black is called *μέλας*.
[^615]: The new Rudnytsia Triodion calls it *Παννυχίς* [Pannychis].
[^616]: *Sluzhebnik of Lviv, 1780*, and the *Moscow Typikon*.
[^617]: In Constantinople the Patriarch places the Plashchanytsia upon the Holy Table.
[^618]: Thus it takes place in Russia, according to the testimony of Moralt, *Epistle 14*: *um die h. Tafel herum, auf welchem das Leichentuch ausgebreitet ist, das hier bleibt bis zum Tage der Himmelfahrt* [around the Holy Table, upon which the burial shroud is spread out, which remains here until the day of the Ascension].
[^619]: According to the ancient Typikon of Coenobia, this Procession was made from the Church into the Narthex through the northern Doors of that same Narthex, and there before its middle Doors leading into the Temple the beginning of Matins was sung; and only the Gospel was carried by the Priest, and the Censer by the Deacon, preceded by candle-bearers, and the Brethren with lighted candles, as thou hast it in the Greek Pentecostarion. To these the Pochaiv Triodion joins also the Icon of the Resurrection of Christ, and the *Tserkovne Oko* yet also the Icon of the Most Holy Theotokos, and other holy Icons carried by the Priests and Deacons, which contributes more to the magnificence of the Procession than candles alone. According to the custom of the lands of Greece independent of the Patriarchate of Constantinople, before this Procession the Hierarch or Priest, having taken his stand in the Royal Doors with a lighted candle in his hand, proclaims with a loud voice: *Δεῦτε λάβετε φῶς ἐκ τοῦ ἀνεσπέρου φωτὸς, καὶ ἀσπάσασθε Χριστὸν τὸν ἀναστάντα ἐκ νεκρῶν* [Come, receive ye light from the unwaning Light, and embrace Christ Who is risen from the dead]. And thus all approach one by one, and having kindled their candles from him, form the Procession. (*Tà hágia Páthē*, Athens, 1892).
[^620]: The Greek Triodion thus describes this Procession: *Περὶ ὥραν Ὄρθρου σημαίνουσι πάντα τὰ σήμαντρα. Καὶ ὁ μὲν Ἐκκλησιάρχης διανέμει τοῖς Ἀδελφοῖς τὰ κηρία; ὁ δὲ Ἱερεὺς μετὰ τοῦ Διακόνου λαμπροφοροῦσιν ἅπασαν τὴν ἱερατικὴν αὐτῶν στολήν. Εἶτα, λαβόντων, τοῦ μὲν Ἱερέως τὸ ἱερὸν Εὐαγγέλιον, τοῦ δὲ Διακόνου θυμιατήριον, καὶ λαμπάδων προπορευομένων αὐτῶν, ἀπερχόμεθα πάντες εἰς τὸν Νάρθηκα, κρατοῦντες τὰ κηρία ἡμμένα, καὶ ψάλλοντες τὸ Τὴν Ἀνάστασίν σου, Χριστὲ Σωτὴρ, κτλ. Καὶ ἐν μὲν τῷ Ναῷ ἐναπομείνας μόνος ὁ Κανδηλάπτης, ἅπτει τὰ κηρία πάντα καὶ τὰς κανδήλας; καὶ ποιήσας πυρεῖον, καὶ βαλὼν εἰς αὐτὸ θυμίαμα, τίθησιν ἐν τῷ μέσῳ τοῦ Ναοῦ.* [About the hour of Matins they sound all the semantra. And the Ecclesiarches distributes the candles to the Brethren; and the Priest with the Deacon vest in all their bright priestly vestments. Then, the Priest having taken the holy Gospel, and the Deacon the censer, and lamps going before them, we all depart into the Narthex, holding lighted candles, and chanting "Thy Resurrection, O Christ Savior," etc. And while only the Candle-lighter remains behind in the Church, he lights all the candles and the lamps; and having prepared a fire-pan and cast incense upon it, he places it in the midst of the Church].
[^621]: The Slavonic Typika command to cense all beforehand; but we, according to the Greek Typikon, cense here only directly before the doors of the church. And our Typika and the Russian ones, following the *Tserkovne Oko*, command the Priest to cross the doors of the church with the censer thrice in the form of a cross. The Typikon of the University of Lviv commands to cense crosswise, but not thrice, nor the doors of the church: "he makes a cross with the censer, saying: Glory to the Holy." The Greek Pentecostarion says only: *καὶ θυμιῶν λέγει* [and censing he says].
[^622]: Thus the Greek Typikon of Sabbas. But the Typikon of the University of Lviv, and the manuscript Flowery Triodia which we possess, and our Heirmologia and the Pochaiv Sluzhebnik of 1788, at the Liturgy of Pascha, and the Moscow Typikon at Paschal Matins, and other Typika appoint it thrice.
[^623]: According to the modern custom of the Greeks, which the Slavs do not accept, before the beginning of Matins the Priest chants the Resurrectional Matins Gospel, usually the 2nd. This the Greek Typikon of Sabbas does not have, but only the newly printed Greek Pentecostaria. Likewise, by custom (and not by Church Typikon) of the Greeks independent of the Patriarchate of Constantinople, the entrance into the church takes place through questions and answers borrowed from Psalm 23: **"Lift up your gates, ye princes, and the King of glory shall enter in,"** etc. And when it is said: **"The Lord of hosts, He is the King of glory,"** then the doors are opened, and the entrance into the church takes place. *Μάλιστα ἐπικρατεῖ τὸ ἔθος εἰσέρχεσθαι διὰ τῶν ἐρωταποκρίσεων: Ἄρατε πύλας, καὶ ἀνοίγειν τὴν θύραν μόνον εἰς τὸ Κύριος τῶν δυνάμεων, αὐτός ἐστιν ὁ βασιλεὺς τῆς δόξης* [Especially does the custom prevail of entering through the dialogues: "Lift up the gates," and opening the door only at "The Lord of hosts, He is the King of glory"], says a dispatch from Corfu. This custom we found also among the Greek-Melkites and the Vlachs, and likewise in various Churches of the Latin Rite, concerning which Nilles writes thus: *Aliae ecclesiae contra, magna ex parte Graecos imitantur, ac proinde horum caeremonias hic breviter describimus. Ad repraesentandum igitur Christi triumphum de inferno delatum, summo mane ante Matutini recitationem ordinatur processio, in cuius reditu inveniuntur ecclesiae portae clausae: tunc episcopus vel sacerdos illa psalmi verba Tollite portas etc. profert, et ex interiori parte clericus vel alius cantor respondet: Quis est iste rex gloriae etc. Tandem post contentionem celebrans percutiens valvas, intonat: Surrexit Christus, et statim ecclesiae janua expanditur. Tunc altum extollit cereum triangulum, quem in manu gestat, quemque ipsi Τρικήριον appellant; quam eandem caeremoniam populus assistens facit cum lumine, quod unusquisque in manu gerit.* (*Kalend.* II, p. 312). However, these things are found neither in the Greek nor in the Slavonic Typika, nor are the words of the said Psalm appropriately applied here, because according to the common interpretation they refer not to Christ's descent into Hades, but to His Ascension. *Cum hic Psalmus,* says Bellarmine, *ascensionem Domini manifeste praedicet. Quod ad Ascensionem hic Psalmus proprie pertineat, testantur Cyprianus Serm. de Ascensione, Hieronymus epistola 142 ad Damasum, Ruffinus in expositione Symboli, Gregorius Nissenus, Ioannes Chrysostomus, Augustinus, Leo, et alii in Serm. de Ascens.* [Since this Psalm manifestly foretells the Ascension of the Lord. That this Psalm properly pertains to the Ascension is attested by Cyprian, *Sermon on the Ascension*; Jerome, *Epistle 142 to Damasus*; Rufinus, *In Exposition of the Creed*; Gregory of Nyssa; John Chrysostom; Augustine; Leo; and others in their *Sermons on the Ascension*].
[^624]: *Ὁ δὲ Ἱερεὺς θυμιᾷ εἰς τὴν ἔναρξιν τοῦ Κανόνος, κατὰ τὴν τάξιν.* [And the Priest censes at the beginning of the Canon, according to the order] (Greek Triodion).
[^625]: That which the *Tserkovne Oko* says, and after it our Triodion and the Russian ones, that the Little Ektenia after each Ode takes place outside the Sanctuary on this Holy Day, does not agree with the Greek Triodion; for this, instead of "outside the Sanctuary", has "within the Sanctuary", *ἔσωθεν τοῦ Βήματος*, as has also the Typikon of the University of Lviv and the manuscript Slavonic Triodion which is among us. But this contradiction can be put to rest if we say that the Greek Triodion and other Typika command the Priest on this day to say all the Ektenias, and not the Deacon; and the Deacon only to carry the censer, and to pronounce the verses: **"Let God arise"**; and on this supposition the Priest sings all the Ektenias within the Sanctuary at the Holy Table. *Ἐν ἑκάστῃ δὲ Ὠδῇ γίνεται παρὰ τοῦ Ἱερέως, ἔσωθεν τοῦ Βήματος, Συναπτὴ μικρὰ* [And at each Ode there is made by the Priest, within the Sanctuary, a Little Ektenia], says the Triodion, which attributes also the other Ektenias to the Priest. For the rest, *ἔσωθεν* (within) and *ἔξωθεν* (without) differ by only a single letter, so that from this Greek copyists might easily have been deceived.
[^626]: According to the Typikon of the Greek Triodion, the kiss takes place thus: *Λαμβάνει ὁ Ἱερεὺς τὸ ἅγιον Εὐαγγέλιον, καὶ ἵσταται πρὸ τῶν θυρῶν τοῦ ἁγίου Βήματος· ὁ δὲ Καθηγούμενος ἐλθών, καὶ ἀσπασάμενος τὸ ἅγιον Εὐαγγέλιον, καὶ τὸν Ἱερέα, λαμβάνει αὐτὸ ἐκ τῶν χειρῶν αὐτοῦ, καὶ ἵσταται ἐκ δεξιῶν τοῦ Ἱερέως. Εἶτα οἱ Ἀδελφοὶ πάντες, κατὰ τὴν τάξιν αὐτῶν, ἀσπάζονται πρῶτον τὸ ἅγιον Εὐαγγέλιον, εἶτα τὸν Ἱερέα καὶ τὸν Καθηγούμενον, καὶ ἵστανται κἀκεῖνοι ἔνθα ἂν τύχωσι, καὶ ἀσπάζονται ἀλλήλους.* [The Priest takes the holy Gospel and stands before the doors of the holy Sanctuary; and the Superior, having come and kissed the holy Gospel and the Priest, takes it from his hands and stands at the right of the Priest. Then all the Brethren, according to their rank, kiss first the holy Gospel, then the Priest and the Superior; and they also stand wherever they happen to be, and kiss one another]. According to the Typikon of our Pochaiv Triodion, and the Russian ones, and the *Tserkovne Oko*, and our manuscript Typikon, the kiss takes place first in the Sanctuary among the sacred ministers themselves, then in the church before the Iconostasis by the church-servers and the Elders, and at the end also by the laity, with the sacred ministers, and among themselves. Here is the Typikon: "The kiss of the Superior with the other Priests and Deacons in the holy Sanctuary takes place thus. The one approaching says: **'Christ is risen'**; and he answers: **'Truly He is risen'**, and they kiss. Then the Superior, having taken the Cross, stands outside before the Holy Royal Doors. Likewise also all the Priests, as many as there be, and the Deacons, having taken each their holy Gospel and the Venerable Icons, according to their rank stand all in a row, on the right side of the Superior. And the more honorable church-servers and the Elders come first, one by one, and bowing slightly before the Superior, kiss each their Venerable Cross and the holy Gospel which the Priest holds, and the other Icons, and kiss also the Superior himself on the lips, saying: **'Christ is risen'**, while he answers: **'Truly He is risen'**. In the same manner they kiss also the Priests, and among themselves according to their rank, and take their stand in a row on the right side of the Priests. Then after them the laity likewise make the kiss" (*Moscow Typikon* and *Pochaiv Triodion*, 1786).

[^628]: Thus our Sluzhebnyks; but in our Triodia and the Russian ones, in place of **"More honorable"** there is not appointed **"Shine, shine"**, nor in place of **"Glory to Thee, O Christ God, our hope"**: **"Christ is risen"** up to **"trampling down death by death"**, with the response: **"And upon those in the tombs bestowing life"**; while the Greek Typika have none of these things, nor a Dismissal with the Cross, but appoint the Dismissal *Ὁ ἀναστὰς ἐκ νεκρῶν* [He Who rose from the dead], as on Sunday. (*Euchologion*, Rome, 1873, p. 79).
[^629]: Compare the Typikon of Thomas Sunday evening with the Typikon of Small Vespers of the Ascension of the Lord. Concerning this the new Typikon of the Patriarchate of Constantinople writes thus: *Πολλοὶ καὶ μετὰ τὴν Ἀνάστασιν τοῦ Κυρίου ἡμῶν Ἰησοῦ Χριστοῦ, ἀγνοοῦντες, ἐπικαλοῦνται τὸ Βασιλεῦ Οὐράνιε Παράκλητε, κτλ. ἐν ᾧ τοῦτο δὲν εἶναι συγχωριμένον, παρόντος ἔτι τοῦ Σωτῆρος ἡμῶν ἐπὶ γῆς, παρὰ ἀπὸ τὴν ά. τῆς Ἀναλήψεως, ἤτοι ἀπὸ τὴν πρώτην Πεντηκοστὴν, καθότι κατὰ τὴν τρίτην ὥραν ταύτης τῆς ἡμέρας κατῆλθε τὸ Ἅγιον Πνεῦμα... ὡς ἐκ τούτου λοιπὸν ἔλαβεν ἀρχὴν ἡ ἐπίκλησις τοῦ Παρακλήτου Πνεύματος, ἀπὸ ταύτην τὴν ἡμέραν, ἥτις παύει ἀπὸ τὴν ἡμέραν τῆς Ἀναστάσεως μέχρι τῆς Πεντηκοστῆς.* [Many, even after the Resurrection of our Lord Jesus Christ, through ignorance invoke the "O Heavenly King, Comforter," etc., whereas this is not permitted while our Savior is yet present upon the earth, until the 1st [day] of the Ascension, that is, until the first Pentecost, because at the third hour of this day the Holy Spirit descended... Therefore, the invocation of the Spirit, the Comforter, took its beginning from this day, and ceases from the day of the Resurrection until Pentecost] (*On the Monday of the Holy Spirit*).
[^630]: These things concerning **"Christ is risen"** our Sluzhebnyks have, except for **"We have seen"**, in whose place they do not have **"Christ is risen"**; nevertheless, our custom has it. And the *Pochaiv Triodion* of 1786, leaf 88, and the *Moscow Typikon* of 1859, leaf 352. The earlier Greek Typika appoint *Χριστὸς ἀνέστη* [Christ is risen] only in place of **"Blessed be the name"**, and in place of Psalm 33 **"I will bless the Lord"**, as has also the Slavonic Typikon of the University of Lviv. But the later custom of the Greeks holds to chanting *Χριστὸς ἀνέστη* after **"Blessed is the Kingdom"** thrice, and in place of **"We have seen the true Light"**, and in place of **"Blessed be the name of the Lord"** thrice; however, some of them after **"Blessed is the Kingdom"** chant **"Christ is risen"** with its verses, as was set down at the beginning of Paschal Matins; which the Moscow Typikon follows, but in no way the new Typikon of Constantinople. These things which we have said concerning the custom of the Greeks, we took from a dispatch which we have from the Island of Corfu, sounding thus: *Μετὰ τὸ Εὐλογημένη ἡ βασιλεία ὁ Ἱερεὺς τὸ Χριστὸς ἀνέστη, καὶ ἀκολούθως ἐπαναλαμβάνουσιν αὐτὸ, πρῶτον ὁ δέξιος, ἀκολούθως ὁ εὐώνυμος χορὸς; καὶ τοῦτο γίνεται καὶ πάσαις ταῖς ἡμέραις τοῦ Πάσχα ἄχρι τῆς Ἀναλήψεως. Ἀλλὰ ἐν τῇ μεγάλῃ κυριακῇ, καὶ ἐν ταῖς τῆς Διακαινησίμου ἡμέραις ἀκολουθοῦμεν ψάλλειν αὐτὸ μετὰ τῶν στίχων αὐτοῦ ἀπαραλλάκτως ὡς προείρηται ἐν τῷ Ὄρθρῳ... Ψάλλεται ἀκολούθως τὸ Χριστὸς ἀνέστη· τόσον ἐν τῇ μεγ. Κυριακῇ, ὅσον καὶ ἐν ταῖς λοιπαῖς τοῦ Πάσχα ἡμέραις ἐν τῷ Μετὰ φόβου ἀντὶ τοῦ Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς, καὶ πρὶν τῆς Ἀπολύσεως τρὶς χύμα ἀντὶ τοῦ Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα, καὶ ἐν τῇ Ἀπολύσει τελευταῖον ἀντὶ τοῦ Δι’ εὐχῶν.* [After "Blessed is the Kingdom," the Priest says "Christ is risen," and subsequently they repeat it, first the right choir, then the left choir; and this takes place on all the days of Pascha until the Ascension. But on the Great Sunday and on the days of Bright Week we follow chanting it with its verses without variation, as was said before at Matins... There is chanted subsequently "Christ is risen," both on the Great Sunday and on the remaining days of Pascha at "With fear" instead of "We have seen the Light," and before the Dismissal thrice plainly instead of "Blessed be the name," and at the Dismissal finally instead of "Through the prayers"].
[^631]: The *Tserkovne Oko* has thus: "And at every Exclamation, or Stasis of the Gospel, they strike in the church once upon the Kandia; while the Parecclesiarches outside the church strikes upon the great semantron, and upon the great bell. At the last Exclamation they strike upon all the bells, and upon the great semantron."
[^632]: *Εἶτα ἀναγινώσκεται τὸ Ἅγιον Εὐαγγέλιον ὑπὸ τοῦ Πατριάρχου, καὶ ὑπὸ τῶν ἁγίων Ἀρχιερέων (περιοδικῶς εἰς διαφόρους διαλέκτους, ὡς εἴθισται).* [Then the Holy Gospel is read by the Patriarch, and by the holy Hierarchs (in turn in various languages, as is customary)] (*New Typikon of Constantinople*, on the day of Pascha at Vespers).
[^633]: Or, as the Triodion has it, after the Dismissal of the Divine Liturgy.
[^634]: In the Order of the Panagia, set down in the Horologion before or after the 6th Hour, it is related that the Apostles after Christ's Resurrection, even until their dispersion throughout the whole world, dwelling with one mind in Jerusalem, had the custom to dine together, and at that time to leave an empty place at table, upon which they placed a cushion, and upon it a piece of bread for Christ's Portion, that is, in memory of Jesus Christ; and after dinner to elevate the piece of bread, saying: "Great is the name of the Holy Trinity," and: "O Lord Jesus Christ, help us"; and from the Resurrection until the Ascension: "Christ is risen." When they were doing this after the Dormition of the Most Holy Theotokos, she then appeared to them in the air in a bright cloud, alive, surrounded by Angels, saying: "Rejoice, for I am with you all the days"; and they, astonished, in place of "O Lord Jesus Christ," cried aloud: "Most Holy (*Panagía*) Theotokos, help us." This rite of the Panagia, so called from the name *Panagía*, by which the Apostles then invoked the Most Holy Theotokos, in imitation of the Apostles was used sometimes by the Emperor at Court (Codin. *De Officiis C. P.* cap. 7, in Migne, *Patrologia Graeca*, vol. 157, pp. 79–80), and by the people in churches or homes, but preeminently and daily in monasteries, as is evident from the Order of the Refectory set down in the Horologion; however, more solemnly in Bright Week, in which not a piece of the bread which they were eating, but a whole (*holóklēron*) loaf of bread they elevated throughout all the days of the Week, not partaking of it until Bright Saturday, but only kissing it. And this Order was formerly performed only in the refectory, as the Greek Pentecostarion has it; but the Slavs, for the sake of greater solemnity, did not perform this whole rite only in the refectory, but daily throughout all of Bright Week, with lamps and the Icon of the Resurrection preceding, carried the Artos from the church to the refectory, and from the refectory to the church, striking at that time all the bells, as according to the *Tserkovne Oko* our Pochaiv Triodion and Russian Typika have it. Today this Rite is performed among us only in Bright Week, and not with all the prayers and rites as the Triodion has it, but only as we have set down here in the text; however, not only in monastic churches, but in all other churches. The prayers for the Blessing and the Breaking of the Artos the Slavs added for themselves, for they are not found in the Greek books; and the prayer for the breaking is not even appointed in the Slavonic typika, except our own; while others break it without a prayer.
[^635]: If a Hierarch officiates, we deem that he is to make the beginning of Vespers in the Sanctuary, censing before the Holy Table and proclaiming from there the Verses, then departing to his Throne; and at **"Gladsome Light"**, after being illuminated with the Dikirion, entering into the Sanctuary, and from the Holy Doors reading the Gospel, and returning to his Throne.
[^636]: Some deem (among whom is also the new Typikon of the Patriarchate of Constantinople) that throughout all of Bright Week Vespers is to begin not from **"Blessed is our God"**, but from **"Glory to the holy"**, as at Matins. But they are deceived, for in all the editions which we possess it is expressly said: **"Blessed is our God"**, *Εὐλογητὸς ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν*; and the Greek Typikon of Sabbas: *Σφραγίζει σταυροειδῶς μετὰ τοῦ θυμιατοῦ, λέγων: Εὐλογητὸς ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν* [He signs crosswise with the censer, saying: Blessed is our God] (*On the Sunday of Pascha at Vespers*).
[^637]: The earlier Typikon at Vespers of Monday evening and of the other days of Bright Week is silent concerning sacred vestments; but since on Monday at the beginning of Matins it clearly appoints only the epitrachelion, so also at the beginning of Vespers it is to be thus. At the beginning of Matins the Pentecostarion says: *ἄνευ μέντοι τῆς Ἱερατικῆς στολῆς* [without, however, the priestly vestment]; and the Typikon of the University of Lviv, and our manuscript, and the Moscow Typikon likewise; similarly also our Pochaiv Triodion of 1786; but this adds: "Or whatever custom is anywhere"; and: "In secular churches the Priest vests in robes, that is, in the phelonion." The *Tserkovne Oko* appoints also the phelonion.
[^638]: Concerning censing, our Triodion and the Moscow Typikon mention no censing here whatever; nevertheless, the Greek Pentecostarion, and our manuscript Typikon, and that of the University of Lviv, and the *Oko* appoint censing: at the beginning of Matins, only of the Holy Table and the Sanctuary; but at the beginning of the Canon, only of the Church and the Brethren according to custom. *Δεῖ δὲ γινώσκειν, ὅτι μετὰ τὸ εὐλογῆσαι τὸν Ἱερέα, ἐν τῷ ψάλλεσθαι τὸ Χριστὸς ἀνέστη, θυμιᾷ τὴν ἁγίαν Τράπεζαν, ὡς σύνηθες, καὶ ὅλον τὸ Ἱερατεῖον; εἰς δὲ τὴν ἔναρξιν τοῦ Κανόνος ἐξέρχεται, καὶ θυμιᾷ τὸν Ναὸν, καὶ τοὺς ἀδελφοὺς, κατὰ τὴν συνηθισμένην τάξιν.* [One ought to know that after the Priest has given the blessing, while "Christ is risen" is being chanted, he censes the Holy Table, as is customary, and the whole Sanctuary; but at the beginning of the Canon he goes out and censes the Church and the Brethren, according to the accustomed order].

[^662]: Since the last prayer, "O God, great and eternal", is the eighth of the prayers said by the Priest during the Introductory Psalm, on this account it is to be omitted either there or here. The number of these prayers varies according to the difference of editions of the Triodion. Our Pochaiv Triodion appoints seven prayers, as does also the Greek Triodion of the Propaganda. The manuscript Slavonic Triodion which we possess appoints seven prayers, yet it does not appoint the prayer "O God, great and eternal", but another: "O Master Almighty". The *Tserkovne Oko* appoints only four prayers: the 1st: "O Most Pure", after the Prokeimenon; the 2nd after "Let us all say": "O Lord our God"; the 3rd and 4th after "Vouchsafe, O Lord": "O Ever-flowing", and "O Heavenly King". The *Moscow Typikon* appoints 5: "O Most Pure", "O Lord Jesus Christ", and after "Vouchsafe, O Lord": "O Ever-flowing", and the other two which are in the Triodion. The Typikon of the University of Lviv and another manuscript which is among us mention only three Prayers in general.
